Leon (via coordinator) approved the exact change set verbatim — new §10 bullet, Revision 30 → 31, this PM Updates entry, Last Action update — and directed the [TRIVIAL] tag. PRD Status line `[TRIVIAL] — Revision 31`; `**Revision**` bumped 30 → 31; Date 2026-10-05. The new §10 bullet is the verbatim Proposed Shared Convention from that review.
  - **Why [TRIVIAL]**: no new/changed AC, no new AC ID, no new module, no new dependency, no DB migration, no module-boundary change, no phase-plan change, no user-facing behavior change. This is an engineering convention governing *how* a reused `expo-video` `VideoView` swaps sources on iOS — it codifies the fix direction for the AC-039/AC-133 swipe-navigation loading-cover behavior, not a new requirement. The primary artifact is `production.md` (Shared Conventions); the `prd.md` §10 bullet is a minimal cross-reference note, consistent with the prior data-access convention entries (2026-10-04).
  - **The convention (verbatim, as recorded in §10)**: Reused `expo-video` `VideoView` source swaps (iOS) — when a single persistent `VideoView`/`VideoPlayer` swaps sources via `replaceAsync` rather than remounting, (1) drive the load *cover* down on `onFirstFrameRender` (first composited frame), not on `statusChange: readyToPlay` (buffer-only, fires before the frame paints), and (2) render the `VideoView` at `opacity: 0` while loading so the previously-decoded frame held by `AVPlayerLayer` cannot bleed through — iOS has no `useExoShutter` equivalent (that prop is Android-only). Keep the `statusChange: error` path wired to the error UI so the cover never hangs.
  - **For Doc-Sync**: add this convention to the `production.md` Shared Conventions section (adjacent to the other MOD-005/video-playback engineering conventions). It is the verbatim Proposed Shared Convention from the 2026-10-05 follow-up #2 Tech Lead review. PM edited only `prd.md` §10 (the minimal note) + this PM Updates tag + Last Action; PM did not edit `production.md` or any module spec. No module spec changes required (project-wide conventions live in `production.md`). **Note**: the source fix for the residual flash routes separately and directly to `engineer-mod-beta-video` per that review (invisible `VideoView` during load + clear on first composited frame); this PM entry covers only the project-wide convention.

- **2026-10-04 [TRIVIAL]** — Two data-access Shared Conventions from the `fetchBetaVideosByUser` runtime failure (log-before-throw in service functions; manual PostgREST verification for new/modified embedded join strings). Leon (via coordinator) requested and approved logging the two Proposed Shared Conventions from the Tech Lead review dated 2026-10-04 (see `## Tech Lead Reviews` → `Review — 2026-10-04 — change (two data-access conventions from the `fetchBetaVideosByUser` runtime failure ...)`), and directed the [TRIVIAL] tag. Root cause context: `fetchBetaVideosByUser` threw a generic user-facing string on device because its `.select()` nested an FK-hinted embed inside another embed (`routes!beta_videos_route_id_fkey(name, gyms(name))`), a shape PostgREST rejects at runtime; the fully-mocked unit test never validated the string against PostgREST, and the function swallowed the raw error. The source fix already routed through `engineer-mod-beta-video` (Tech Lead confirms commit `cf77912`); this PM entry covers only the two project-wide conventions. Unlike the prior production.md-only convention entries, this one records a minimal note of both conventions in `prd.md` §10 (Non-Functional Requirements), alongside the existing engineering-convention bullets (native dependency installation, native build gate, file upload) — so the PRD revision is bumped: Status line `[TRIVIAL] — Revision 30`; `**Revision**` 29 → 30; Date 2026-10-04.
  - **Why [TRIVIAL]**: no new/changed AC, no new module, no new dependency, no DB migration, no module-boundary change, no phase-plan change. These are observability/data-access process rules that govern *how* queries are written and verified, not *what* a query returns — no user-facing behavior change. The primary affected artifact is `production.md` (Shared Conventions); the `prd.md` §10 bullets are a minimal cross-reference note.
  - **For Doc-Sync**: add **both** conventions below to the `production.md` Shared Conventions / data-access area (place Convention B adjacent to the existing "User-Owned Tables … Reference public.users" convention, per the Tech Lead review). These are the verbatim "Proposed Shared Conventions" from the 2026-10-04 Tech Lead review. PM edited only `prd.md` §10 (the minimal note) + this PM Updates tag; PM did not edit `production.md` or any module spec. No module spec changes required (project-wide conventions live in production.md).
    1. **Convention A — Log the raw Supabase error before throwing a user-facing string.** Any Supabase data-access function (service files, RPC wrappers, storage helpers) that catches a Supabase `error` and throws a user-facing `Error` MUST first call `console.error('[functionName] error:', error)` so the raw Supabase/PostgREST diagnostic is visible in device/Metro logs. The user-facing string stays generic; the raw error goes to the console, never to the user. Canonical form:
       ```ts
       if (error) {
         console.error('[fetchBetaVideosByUser] error:', error);
         throw new Error('Failed to load beta videos. Please try again.');
       }
       ```
       Applies to every module's service files. Reference implementations: `mod-beta-video/beta-video-service.ts` `uploadBetaVideo` (storage-error, insert-error, and outer-catch sites) and `fetchBetaVideosByUser`. Known existing gap to sweep when this lands: `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` in the same file throws without logging first (follow-up for the owning Engineer, plus a grep for other `throw new Error` sites inside Supabase service functions project-wide).
    2. **Convention B — Manually verify every new or modified PostgREST embed/join string against a real PostgREST instance before shipping.** Whenever an engineer adds or modifies an embedded join in a `.select()` string — especially a nested embed, and most especially an FK-hinted embed nested inside another embed (e.g. `routes!beta_videos_route_id_fkey(name, gyms(name))`) — they MUST exercise the string against the live or local Supabase instance before marking the AC done: run the equivalent query in the Studio SQL Editor, or hit the raw PostgREST endpoint directly. A `.select()` embed string is an untyped query DSL that neither TypeScript nor a mocked Supabase client can validate — mocked unit tests short-circuit before the string is ever parsed by PostgREST and therefore CANNOT substitute for this step. This is the data-access corollary of the existing "tests must not mock away a production incompatibility" convention; record it adjacent to the "User-Owned Tables … Reference public.users" convention. Nested FK-hinted embeds (more than one level of `!fk_name`) should be avoided in favour of a single disambiguated outer hint or a server-side RPC. Recommended (advisory): note the verification — the equivalent SQL or the PostgREST URL hit — in the module `status.md` Engineering Progress entry so the check is auditable.
  - **Run `claude --agent doc-sync`.** Doc-Sync NOT invoked here.

- **2026-10-04 [TRIVIAL]** — **Correction to the Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern convention in `production.md`** (production.md-only change; **no PRD revision bump** — `prd.md` untouched, no behavioral AC change). Leon (via coordinator) requested and approved logging this correction. The "Exempt" clause in the recently-added Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern convention is **wrong**: it named `RouteDetailScreen` (`mod-route-catalog`) and `UserProfileScreen` (`mod-social-feed`) as exempt on the mistaken premise that an ancestor named `*Navigator` implies a react-navigation `NavigationContainer`. Both screens then crashed on device with "Couldn't find a navigation object. Is your component inside NavigationContainer?" after `useFocusEffect` was added. **Confirmed root cause**: the entire app has **no `NavigationContainer` anywhere** — all `*Navigator` components (`AuthNavigator`, `GymNavigator`, `RouteNavigator`, `ProfileNavigator`, `HomeNavigator`) are custom React `useState` state machines, not react-navigation navigators. The ban on `@react-navigation/native` navigation-context hooks is therefore **app-wide**, not keep-alive-only, and there is **no exemption**. Source: Tech Lead review dated 2026-10-04 (see `## Tech Lead Reviews` → `Review — 2026-10-04 — correction (the 2026-10-04 "Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern" Shared Convention exemption is wrong ...)`). The source fixes + test-mock removals for both screens already routed through `engineer-mod-route-catalog` and `engineer-mod-social-feed` (confirmed applied per the review); this PM entry covers only the project-wide convention correction.
  - **Why [TRIVIAL]**: no PRD text change, no new/changed AC, no new module, no new dependency, no DB migration, no module-boundary change, no phase-plan change. This is a correction to a project-wide Shared Convention that lives in `production.md`, not `prd.md` or any module spec. Consistent with the prior production.md-only convention entries.
  - **For Doc-Sync**: **Replace the entire `### Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern` section** in `production.md` with the corrected convention below. The following `### Keep-Alive Screen Test Pattern` section is **unchanged** (it already applies to all screens). PM did not edit `production.md` or any module spec — this PM Updates tag is the trigger for Doc-Sync to sync it in. No module spec changes required.
    - **Corrected convention (verbatim) — replaces the whole `### Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern` section**:
      **No screen in this app may use `useFocusEffect`, `useNavigation`, `useRoute`, or any other `@react-navigation/native` navigation-context hook.** The app intentionally uses custom state-machine navigators (`GymNavigator`, `RouteNavigator`, `ProfileNavigator`, `HomeNavigator`, `AuthNavigator`) and mounts no `NavigationContainer` anywhere. Any `@react-navigation/native` hook requiring navigation context will throw "Couldn't find a navigation object" at runtime.
      Rules:
      1. **Conditionally-mounted screens** (`RouteDetailScreen`, `UserProfileScreen`, and any screen rendered with a conditional expression) — use a plain `useEffect` for data loading. These screens unmount and remount on each navigation, so `useEffect` fires on every mount, equivalent to `useFocusEffect` without the crash.
      2. **Keep-alive screens** (`ProfileScreen`, and any screen rendered via AppShell's `display:none`/`display:flex` keep-alive pattern) — use an `isActive: boolean` prop + `useEffect([isActive])` for focus-triggered behavior. These screens never unmount, so remounting is not available as a refresh trigger.
      3. Each keep-alive screen must carry a top-of-file comment: "This screen is rendered via AppShell's keep-alive pattern and has no NavigationContainer ancestor. Do not use useFocusEffect or any other @react-navigation/native hook that requires navigation context. Use the isActive prop instead."
      4. Tests for any screen — keep-alive or conditionally-mounted — must NOT mock `@react-navigation/native` or `useFocusEffect`. If a screen's test needed that mock to pass, the mock was hiding a production crash.
    - The false "Exempt" clause is replaced by rule (1), which explains *what to use instead* for conditionally-mounted screens, not that they are exempt from the ban.
  - **Run `claude --agent doc-sync`.** Doc-Sync NOT invoked here.

- **2026-10-04 [TRIVIAL]** — Two engineering Shared Conventions for keep-alive screens (production.md-only change; **no PRD revision bump** — `prd.md` untouched, no behavioral AC change). Leon (via coordinator) requested and approved logging the two Proposed Shared Conventions from the Tech Lead review dated 2026-10-04 (see `## Tech Lead Reviews` → `Review — 2026-10-04 — blocker (ProfileScreen crashed on device: useFocusEffect ...)`), and directed the [TRIVIAL] tag with no revision bump. These are engineering/testing conventions, not user-facing behavior; the affected artifact is `production.md` (Shared Conventions section), not `prd.md`. Root cause context: `ProfileScreen` crashed on device because it called `useFocusEffect` while rendered via `AppShell`'s keep-alive mount (`display:none`/`display:flex`), which has no `NavigationContainer` ancestor; the crash was invisible in CI because the test mocked `@react-navigation/native`. The source fix + test-mock removal route separately through `engineer-mod-auth-profile` (already applied per the review) — this PM entry covers only the two project-wide conventions.
  - **Why [TRIVIAL]**: no PRD text change, no new/changed AC, no new module, no new dependency, no DB migration, no module-boundary change, no phase-plan change. Project-wide conventions live in `production.md`, not `prd.md` or a single module spec. This mirrors the prior production.md-only convention entries (e.g. 2026-09-30 file-upload convention, 2026-09-25 conventions).
  - **For Doc-Sync**: add **both** conventions below to the `production.md` Shared Conventions section. These are the verbatim "Proposed Shared Conventions" from the 2026-10-04 Tech Lead review. PM did not edit `production.md` or any module spec — this PM Updates tag is the trigger for Doc-Sync to sync them in. No module spec changes required.
    1. **Keep-alive screens must not use `useFocusEffect` (or any navigation-context hook).** `AppShell` uses a keep-alive mount strategy (`display:none` / `display:flex`) for the main tab screens to preserve state across tab switches; these screens are **not** inside a `NavigationContainer`. Any `@react-navigation/native` hook that requires navigation context (`useFocusEffect`, `useNavigation`, `useRoute`) will throw at runtime on them. Rules: (1) Any screen rendered via AppShell's keep-alive pattern must receive an `isActive: boolean` prop and use `useEffect([isActive])` for focus-triggered behavior — never `useFocusEffect`. (2) Screens rendered inside a stack/tab navigator (e.g. `RouteDetailScreen`, `UserProfileScreen`) are exempt and may use `useFocusEffect` normally. (3) Each keep-alive screen must carry a top-of-file comment: "This screen is rendered via AppShell's keep-alive pattern and has no NavigationContainer ancestor. Do not use useFocusEffect or any other @react-navigation/native hook that requires navigation context. Use the isActive prop instead." Affected keep-alive screens (current): `ProfileScreen`. Correct alternative: `isActive` prop + `useEffect([isActive])`. Exempt: any stack/tab navigator screen.
    2. **Tests must not mock away a test-vs-production mount incompatibility.** Tests for keep-alive screens must **not** mock `useFocusEffect` or `@react-navigation/native` — those screens render in production without a `NavigationContainer`, and the test must reflect that reality so any future navigation-context hook crashes CI immediately. To assert focus-triggered behavior on a keep-alive screen, flip the `isActive` prop (false → true) directly rather than simulating navigation events. Generally: if mocking a dependency makes a test pass that would fail in production, the mock is hiding a real bug — fix the incompatibility, do not mock over it.
  - **Run `claude --agent doc-sync`.** Doc-Sync NOT invoked here.

If no existing video (`hasExistingVideo === false`), the picker opens directly with no dialog. Fail-closed: unreadable size (size === 0) is treated as oversized and rejected.     - **§8 MOD-005 — AC-170 (new, Rev 24)**: when `beta-videos` Supabase Storage usage reaches 800 MB (80% of the 1 GB free-tier total shared across all buckets), surface an operator storage-watch trigger to begin evaluating the Phase 2 migration. Operational early-warning threshold; does not block uploads at 800 MB. Cited in US-004's criteria list.
    - **US-004** — added a "Known Phase 1 trade-off (accepted, not a bug)" note: the 45 MB gate + the 60 s duration cap (AC-030) mean a full-bitrate iPhone 1080p HEVC clip (~16 Mbps) hits 45 MB at ~22 s, so long high-quality clips are rejected; users trim or lower recording quality. Poster/follower keep seeing it. Composes WITH the reported-video exclusion (AC-142) and the blocked-user filter (AC-154). `beta_video_privacy` read from the poster's user record via the existing `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` poster join (AC-144); follower relationship via the `Follow` table (`follower_id = viewer`, `followee_id = poster`).

Independent from the profile privacy setting (AC-063), which is edited via MOD-001's Edit Profile entry point (AC-117), not here.

- **2026-09-27 [TRIVIAL]** — AC-035 `.mov` container ruling: accept `.mov` alongside `.mp4` (PRD Revision 12). QA raised a valid UX concern — iOS camera records natively in `.mov` (QuickTime), and the prior MP4-only rule (engineer rejected `.mov` in commit bdccdab) forced users to convert iPhone-camera climbing footage before upload, blocking the primary MOD-005 capture loop. **PM ruling: ALLOW both `.mov` and `.mp4`.** Rationale: (1) iOS `.mov` is H.264/HEVC — the same codec family already accepted for `.mp4`; Supabase Storage stores both and `expo-video` plays both on iOS, so no transcoding is needed; (2) accepting `.mov` removes friction for the primary use case (iPhone camera footage of routes); (3) the original MP4-only choice was standardization, which does not outweigh blocking the core capture path on an iOS-only Phase 1; (4) all safety constraints stay — ≤60s duration (AC-030), codec = H.264/HEVC video + AAC audio, and size limits are container-independent and remain enforced. **PRD edits**: AC-035 (§8) marked (revised) — now accepts MP4 (`.mp4`) or QuickTime (`.mov`) containers, rejects on ingest only when video codec is not H.264/HEVC or audio codec is not AAC, stores both as-is (no server-side transcoding), duration/size caps apply regardless of container; AC-031 (§8) compression-output sentence updated to H.264/HEVC + AAC in `.mp4` or `.mov` (cross-refs AC-035); §10 NFR Video line updated with the accepted-container clause (both stored as-is, iPhone `.mov` uploads without conversion). Status line `[TRIVIAL] — Revision 12`; `**Revision**` bumped 11 → 12. **Tagged [TRIVIAL]**: constraint relaxation on existing ACs only — no new user story, module, or AC; no data-model change; module boundaries, dependencies, and the phase plan are all unchanged; no new modules. **Affected spec for Doc-Sync**: MOD-005 (mod-beta-video) — carry the revised AC-035 (both containers, codec-based rejection) and the updated AC-031 compression clause into the spec's Acceptance Criteria Covered and Key Implementation Notes; the spec's MP4-only / `.mov`-rejection wording must be updated to the two-container rule. PM did not edit `production.md` or any module spec — this tag is the Doc-Sync trigger. **Note for Engineer (not part of this spec ruling, for the coordinator to route after Doc-Sync)**: this reverses the prior `.mov` rejection — `validateVideoFormat()` in `BetaVideoUploader` must accept `.mov` again, and the error copy ("Only MP4 videos are supported" in `en` + zh-TW catalogs) must be updated to reflect MP4/MOV acceptance. Doc-Sync NOT invoked here.

- **2026-09-25 [TRIVIAL]** — Two project-wide native-dependency engineering conventions added (PRD Revision 11). Proposed by Tech Lead in the 2026-09-25 build-failure review (iOS build blocker: `expo-av@15.0.2` (SDK 52-era) was installed with `npm install` under Expo SDK 57, so `ExpoModulesCore/EXEventEmitter.h` was not found; the mismatch passed Jest via the `expo-av` mock but failed the native build, and MOD-005 had been marked QA-ready without a native build ever being run). Two conventions logged as project-wide engineering standards in §10 NFR:
  1. **Native dependency installation** — all Expo native dependencies must be installed via `npx expo install <package>`, never `npm install <package>`; Expo resolves the SDK-compatible version automatically, whereas `npm install` can silently install an SDK-incompatible version that passes Jest (via mocks) but fails the native build. Hand-pinning a native module's version string in `package.json` is prohibited.
  2. **Native build gate before QA handoff** — when a module adds or changes a native dependency (a package with iOS/Android native code), the engineer must run the native build (`npx expo run:ios`, or the platform equivalent) and confirm it compiles before marking the module QA-ready; passing unit tests alone are insufficient because Jest mocks native modules.
  - **PRD edits**: §10 Non-Functional Requirements — two new bullets inserted after the Security bullet, before Soft-launch timeframe. Status line `[TRIVIAL] — Revision 11`; `**Revision**` bumped 10 → 11.
  - **Tagged [TRIVIAL]**: process/tooling engineering standards only — no user story, module, or acceptance criterion added; no data-model change; module boundaries, dependencies, and the phase plan are all unchanged; no new modules.
  - **For Doc-Sync**: carry both conventions into `production.md` Shared Conventions (these mirror the Tech Lead's "Proposed Shared Conventions" in the 2026-09-25 review under `## Tech Lead Reviews`). PM did not edit `production.md` — this PM Updates tag is the trigger for Doc-Sync to sync them in. No module spec changes required (project-wide conventions live in production.md, not a single module spec).

- **2026-09-24 [TRIVIAL]** — AC-031 thumbnail-scope ruling (PRD Revision 10). QA (qa-mod-beta-video, commit bcf7082) flagged AC-031 as a spec issue, not a bug: the MOD-005 engineer shipped a Phase 1 simplification (`localThumbnailUri = asset.uri` in `BetaVideoUploader.tsx` line 157) that uploads the video URI as the thumbnail placeholder rather than an extracted still frame, and asked PM to rule whether client-side frame extraction is required in Phase 1 or deferrable. **PM ruling: acceptable Phase 1 simplification — frame extraction deferred to Phase 2.** Rationale: (1) true client-side frame extraction requires a new native dependency (`expo-video-thumbnails` / `ffmpeg-kit-react-native`), neither installed — adding a native module is a scope/dependency decision, and AC-031 never named a specific extraction library; (2) the Phase 1 loop is not blocked — upload/playback (AC-032/033/034) works and `thumbnail_url` is populated with a valid, retrievable URL; the harm is a heavier preview and no still frame, a quality issue not a broken loop; (3) it fits the established Phase 1 simplification pattern (auto-approve default, Studio-only admin, no offline queue, Cloudflare deferred). PRD edits: AC-031 (§8) marked (revised) with an explicit "Phase 1 simplification" clause (thumbnail may use the video URI as a placeholder; true frame extraction e.g. a frame at 1 second is Phase 2; `BetaVideo.thumbnail_url` must still be a valid retrievable URL in Phase 1); §10 NFR Video line clarified with the same placeholder note; §7 Phase 2 module TBD list and §13 roadmap Phase 2 both += "beta-video thumbnail frame extraction (Phase 1 uses a placeholder thumbnail)." PRD Status line `[TRIVIAL] — Revision 10`; `**Revision**` bumped 9 → 10. **Tagged [TRIVIAL]**: scoping/wording only — module boundaries (MOD-005 still owns capture/compression/thumbnail/upload/playback), dependencies, and the phase plan are unchanged; no new modules; no AC removed; the AC still requires a client-generated thumbnail. **Affected spec for Doc-Sync passthrough**: MOD-005 (mod-beta-video) — carry the revised AC-031 Phase 1 clause into the spec's Acceptance Criteria Covered and Key Implementation Notes (the spec's line 113 "frame extracted at 1 second" note should be annotated as Phase 2). **Note for Engineer/Tech Lead (not part of this spec ruling)**: QA also observed the placeholder upload uses `contentType: 'image/jpeg'` while the stored bytes are a video file (`beta-video-service.ts`). Fixing the content-type/URI of the placeholder is an implementation-quality decision for engineer-mod-beta-video (with Tech Lead input if a dependency is added), independent of this scope ruling; this ruling only settles that a placeholder is acceptable for Phase 1. The separately-flagged AC-035 `.mov` bug was already fixed by Engineer in commit c014a14 and is out of scope for this ruling.
