## Tech Lead Review — Navigation AC Ownership (2026-09-23)

**Context**: PRD Revision 5 [SUBSTANTIVE] adds six cross-module navigation ACs via the PM's navigation-gap audit. This is an ownership confirmation only — not an architecture review. I verified each AC's placement against §6 module boundaries and inspected the shipped screens/navigators to classify each as a code gap (needs engineering) vs. a doc gap (code already works, spec didn't say so). Advisory only; no source, migration, spec, or PRD files changed.

| AC | Ownership confirmed | Code gap or doc gap | Notes |
|----|--------------------|--------------------|-------|
| AC-005 | **Confirmed — MOD-002** (destination entry point lives on `GymDetailScreen`) | **Code gap** | `GymDetailScreen.tsx` has only an `onBack` prop — no "View Routes" affordance exists. More important: `RouteNavigator` is fully built and self-contained (takes `gymId`+`gymName`, wires RouteList→RouteDetail) but is **never mounted anywhere** — nothing imports it outside its own file, and `App.tsx` renders `GymNavigator` with no route flow beneath it. So AC-005 is not just "add a button": MOD-002's engineer must add an `onViewRoutes(gymId, gymName)` prop to `GymDetailScreen`, `GymNavigator` must gain a `routes` view state that mounts `RouteNavigator`, passing gym context. **This is the one AC that actually connects MOD-002 → MOD-003 at runtime; without it MOD-003's entire UI is currently unreachable.** Boundary note: the button + navigator mount are MOD-002-owned; `RouteNavigator` itself is MOD-003 and already accepts the required props, so no MOD-003 code change is needed for AC-005. |
| AC-006 | **Confirmed — MOD-002** | **Doc gap** | Already implemented. `GymListScreen.tsx` row `onPress={() => onSelectGym(item.id)}` → `GymNavigator.navigateToDetail(gymId)` → `GymDetailScreen gymId={...}`. Passes the identifier as AC-006 requires. Spec/QA documentation-only; no engineering. |
| AC-042 | **Confirmed — MOD-003** | **Doc gap** | Already implemented. `RouteListScreen.tsx` row `onPress={() => onSelectRoute(item.id)}` → `RouteNavigator.navigateToDetail(routeId)` → `RouteDetailScreen routeId={...}`. Documentation-only. **Caveat**: this path is only reachable once AC-005 mounts `RouteNavigator` — AC-042's code exists but is dead until AC-005 lands. Not an AC-042 defect; a dependency ordering note. |
| AC-037 | **Confirmed — MOD-005** (owns the upload flow the entry point launches) | **Code gap** | `RouteDetailScreen.tsx` currently has a log-send entry point (`onLogSend`) and a placeholder text block for beta videos (`routes.detail.betaVideosPlaceholder`) — no "Add beta video" affordance. **Boundary flag**: the entry point *renders in* MOD-003's `RouteDetailScreen`, but the capture/upload flow it launches is MOD-005-owned. Recommend MOD-005's engineer owns the AC (it's their flow + route-context contract), implemented as a small MOD-003 host change: MOD-003 exposes a slot/prop (`onAddBetaVideo` or a MOD-005-provided component) on `RouteDetailScreen`, MOD-005 fills it. Same host-screen/owning-module split as AC-005. Since MOD-005 is Not started, this fits naturally into MOD-005's build; no separate MOD-003 change order is needed if MOD-005 owns the whole slot. |
| AC-058 | **Confirmed — MOD-007** (owns the notification inbox, the source entry point) | **Code gap** | MOD-007 Not started. Placement correct: the inbox is MOD-007's; tap-through resolves `target_id` → the beta video on its `RouteDetailScreen`. Cross-module boundary: MOD-007 (source) navigates to a MOD-003 screen showing a MOD-005 video. MOD-007's engineer owns the navigation call + `target_id` resolution; depends on MOD-003's `RouteDetailScreen` accepting a route/video target and MOD-005 rendering the video inline (AC-033). Flag for the eventual MOD-007 spec: define the navigation contract (does it deep-link by `route_id` derived from the beta video, or scroll-to-video?) — that contract touches MOD-003/MOD-005 and should be pinned before MOD-007 build. |
| AC-064 | **Confirmed — MOD-008** (owns the destination: profile + send history) | **Code gap** | MOD-006 and MOD-008 both Not started. Placement correct per destination-ownership: the feed/user-reference is MOD-006's (source), the profile+history destination is MOD-008's. MOD-008's engineer owns the destination screen and the privacy gate (AC-063 followers-only → hidden/403). The source affordance (tappable user reference) is MOD-006-owned and must be built when MOD-006 ships. Privacy enforcement (AC-063) is MOD-001's profile-privacy rule applied at the MOD-008 destination — confirm the followers-only/403 check runs server-side (RLS/RPC), not just client-side hiding, consistent with the MOD-006 feed-RPC convention already in the PRD. |

**Summary**: All six PM placements are correct — no ownership corrections needed. Two are documentation-only gaps (AC-006, AC-042): the code already navigates correctly and only the specs/QA records need to catch up. Four are code gaps (AC-005, AC-037, AC-058, AC-064). The single most important finding is AC-005: `RouteNavigator` (MOD-003) is fully implemented but never mounted, so the entire route-catalog UI is currently unreachable from the running app — AC-005 is the missing seam that makes MOD-003 (and therefore AC-042's already-shipped code) actually reachable. Three code-gap ACs (AC-037, AC-058, AC-064) sit on module boundaries where the *source affordance or entry point* renders in one module's screen but the *owning flow/destination* belongs to another; in each case I confirmed the PM's destination-ownership assignment is right and flagged the host-screen split so the owning engineer knows they need a small hosting change in the neighboring module's screen (or a slot the neighbor exposes). No architectural concerns with the navigation approach itself — the state-machine-per-navigator pattern already shipped in `GymNavigator`/`RouteNavigator` extends cleanly to all six. No new modules, no dependency changes. Next step: Doc-Sync carries AC-005/AC-006 into MOD-002 spec, AC-042 into MOD-003, AC-037 into MOD-005, AC-058 into MOD-007, AC-064 into MOD-008; Engineering should treat AC-005 as a near-term MOD-002 change (unblocks MOD-003 reachability), while AC-037/AC-058/AC-064 fold into their respective not-yet-started module builds.

## Tech Lead Reviews

### Review — 2026-10-13 — change (Rev 35 follow-up: batched-RPC conversion for like/save/follow status; caching strategy per query type; confirm Decision 1 AC-180 visibility scope)

**Context**: Follow-up to the Rev 35 architecture review above. Three questions evaluated against the actual codebase (not just the PRD): (1) whether like/save/follow status queries should become batched RPCs following the `routes_with_new_beta` (AC-180) pattern; (2) a caching strategy per query type across three candidate approaches; (3) confirmation of Decision 1 (AC-180 visibility scope, full Option B — all three filters server-side). Advisory only — no source code touched; the human and PM decide what to adopt, Doc-Sync carries any approved conventions into `production.md`. The project uses plain async/await service files (no React Query/SWR) and custom `useState` state-machine navigators (no React Navigation, no `NavigationContainer`) — both are deliberate house patterns reflected in existing conventions, and these recommendations stay inside them rather than proposing a data-fetching library.

**Grounding — what the code actually does today** (verified, not assumed):
- **Like status** (`fetchLikeInfo` → `get_like_info` SECURITY INVOKER RPC): in `BetaVideoReelViewer` it fires **one call per `currentIndex` change** (genuinely one-at-a-time as the user swipes). In `FeedScreen` it is already a `Promise.all` fan-out over all beta-video items (parallel, but still N PostgREST round-trips per feed load).
- **Save status** (`fetchSavedRouteIds` → single `saved_routes` SELECT): **already batched correctly** — returns the full set of the current user's saved route IDs in **one** query. `RouteListScreen` and `RouteDetailScreen` each call it once per screen and test membership client-side. This is NOT an N+1 and does not match the "checked individually per card" description in the question — the code already does the right thing.
- **Follow status** (`fetchIsFollowing(followeeId)` → single `follows` SELECT): only consumer is `UserProfileScreen`, **one call per screen** (one profile at a time). Followers/Following **lists** render via `fetchFollowersList`/`fetchFollowingList`, which already return the full list in one call; they do **not** currently fetch per-row follow status for each listed user.

---

#### Question 1 — Should like / save / follow status become batched RPCs?

**Like status — YES for the feed; NO (not a batch problem) for the reel viewer. Recommend a `get_like_info_batch(target_ids[])` SECURITY INVOKER RPC, consumed only by FeedScreen.**

1. *Right fix?* A batched RPC is the right fix **for FeedScreen only**. The reel viewer's one-call-per-swipe pattern is not an N+1 in the batch sense — it fetches exactly the one video the user is looking at, on demand; batching the whole playlist's like info up front would prefetch likes for videos the user may never swipe to, and the count can go stale between prefetch and view. Leave the reel viewer as-is (single `fetchLikeInfo` per active index). FeedScreen's `Promise.all` fan-out, by contrast, issues N PostgREST round-trips on every feed load — that is the real N+1 to collapse.
2. *Query shape:* new RPC in **MOD-006** (owns `reactions` and the existing `get_like_info`): `get_like_info_batch(p_target_type text, p_target_ids uuid[]) RETURNS TABLE (target_id uuid, like_count int, user_has_liked bool)`, `SECURITY INVOKER STABLE`, `SET search_path = public`, EXECUTE to `authenticated` only. Body: `SELECT t.id, COUNT(r.user_id), BOOL_OR(r.user_id = auth.uid()) FROM unnest(p_target_ids) t(id) LEFT JOIN reactions r ON r.target_id = t.id AND r.target_type = p_target_type GROUP BY t.id`. SECURITY INVOKER keeps RLS scoping intact; `auth.uid()` (never a user_id param) drives `user_has_liked`, consistent with the existing `get_like_info` and the SECURITY DEFINER/INVOKER conventions.
3. *Expected gain:* FeedScreen drops from **1 + N** round-trips (feed RPC + one like call per video) to **1 + 1** (feed RPC + one batch like call). On a 20-item feed over LTE that removes ~19 sequential-ish network calls — directly serves the "feed initial render ≤2s with a warm cache" NFR. The reel viewer sees no change (correctly).
4. *Risks/downsides:* Low. `BOOL_OR(r.user_id = auth.uid())` is correct for the one-like-per-user constraint. One edge: a `target_id` with zero reactions must still return a row with `like_count = 0, user_has_liked = false` — the `unnest ... LEFT JOIN` shape above guarantees that (every input id appears). Verify that against live PostgREST per the `Manually Verify Every New or Modified PostgREST Embed/Join String` convention (this is an RPC, not an embed, but the "exercise it live before done" spirit applies to the GROUP BY/LEFT JOIN correctness).

**Save status — NO. Already optimal; do not convert.** `fetchSavedRouteIds()` is a single-query set fetch, which is strictly better than a batched RPC keyed by visible IDs (it needs no input array, returns a small ID set, and membership is an O(1) `Set.has()` client-side). Converting it to `routes_with_saved_status(route_ids[])` would be a **regression** — more payload shape, an input array to thread, and no round-trip saved. Keep as-is. The one caveat: as the saved-routes set grows unbounded in Phase 2+ this becomes a large unfiltered fetch; for Phase 1 (realistic saved counts) it is fine. If it ever matters, the fix is pagination, not a per-ID batch RPC.

**Follow status — NO batch needed for current surfaces; YES *only if* a future surface renders a follow button per row in a user list.**
1. *Right fix?* For the two current consumers (UserProfileScreen = one profile; Followers/Following lists = no per-row follow state today) there is **no N+1 and nothing to batch**. `fetchIsFollowing` for a single profile is correct as-is. A batched RPC is only warranted if a list-of-users surface starts showing a per-row "Following/Follow" button for each listed user (the question's hypothetical). **This is not in the current code** — do not build it speculatively.
2. *Query shape (for the hypothetical only):* MOD-006-owned `follow_status_batch(p_user_ids uuid[]) RETURNS TABLE (user_id uuid, is_following bool)`, `SECURITY INVOKER STABLE`: `SELECT u.id, EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM follows f WHERE f.follower_id = auth.uid() AND f.followee_id = u.id) FROM unnest(p_user_ids) u(id)`. Called once per list screen with all visible user IDs.
3. *Expected gain (hypothetical):* a followers list with per-row buttons would otherwise be 1 (list) + N (one `fetchIsFollowing` per row); the batch collapses it to 1 + 1.
4. *Risks:* none structurally; the real risk is **building it before a surface needs it**. Defer until the per-row-button requirement actually lands.

*Pattern note:* the AC-180 `routes_with_new_beta` RPC and the proposed `get_like_info_batch` are the same shape as the existing `Personal Cross-Module Data Overlays` convention (batched, own-user-scoped, `fetch...(ids[])`, SECURITY INVOKER, called once per screen). The like-batch is a clean in-convention application; no new convention is needed for it beyond what already exists. The only genuinely new general rule worth codifying is the negative one — *don't* batch a set-membership fetch that's already a single query, and *don't* batch a single-item on-demand fetch (reel viewer). That is captured in Proposed Shared Convention 1 below.

---

#### Question 2 — Caching strategy per query type

Framing: with no React Query/SWR, there is no cache layer today — every screen re-fetches on mount/focus. The three candidates map cleanly to three different data characteristics, so the recommendation is **per-query-type, not one-size-fits-all**. I weight all of these against the house preference for the smallest mechanism that works (same ethos as "prefer RN built-ins; no native dep for a small behavior").

**Like status / like count → Option A (in-memory module-level Map), short TTL ~30s; combine with optimistic write-through on like/unlike. Not B, not C.**
- *Why A:* like counts are high-churn and cheap to refetch; they must never survive an app restart (C is wrong — a persisted stale count is worse than no cache). A context (B) is overkill and would re-render every consumer on any count change.
- *TTL:* ~30s is reasonable. The dominant benefit is not the TTL though — it's **write-through on the user's own like/unlike**: when the user taps like, update the cached entry immediately (optimistic) so the reel viewer and feed agree without a refetch. The existing reel-viewer code already does optimistic local state; a module-level Map lets that optimistic value be shared with FeedScreen.
- *Invalidate:* on the user's own like/unlike (write-through, immediate), and by TTL expiry for other users' likes. Clear the whole Map on sign-out.
- *Caveat:* a module-level mutable Map is a singleton with test-isolation implications — provide a `__clearLikeCache()` for tests and clear on auth change, or the cache leaks across logged-in users. Flagged as a condition on adopting A anywhere.

**Save status → Option B (app-level context holding `savedRouteIds: Set<string>`), no TTL, invalidate on user action. Not A, not C (as the primary).**
- *Why B:* save status is **user-action-driven, low-churn, and read on many screens** (RouteList, RouteDetail, and the Rev 35 Projecting Routes section on HomeScreen all need it). It only changes when *this user* saves/unsaves — there is no external churn to TTL against. A context whose `Set` is updated by the same `saveRoute`/`unsaveRoute` calls that write the DB is the exact fit: one fetch of `fetchSavedRouteIds()` on provider mount, then mutate the Set on every save/unsave, and every consumer reads from context. This also eliminates the current repeated `fetchSavedRouteIds()` calls across RouteList/RouteDetail/Home.
- *TTL:* none. Invalidated only by the user's own save/unsave (the context updates the Set and calls the service).
- *Invalidate:* on `saveRoute`/`unsaveRoute` (context mutates its Set + calls service), and a full refetch on sign-in / provider mount. Clear on sign-out.
- *Optional C as a cold-start nicety (not required):* persisting the saved-ID Set to AsyncStorage would let the Projecting Routes section paint instantly on cold start before the network returns, then reconcile. This is a legitimate use of C (saved routes are slow-changing, exactly C's stated sweet spot) but it is a **nice-to-have, not Phase 1 required** — the context (B) is the load-bearing choice; AsyncStorage is a cold-start hydration layer on top of it if the cold-start NFR needs it.

**Follow status → Option A (in-memory Map) with a short-to-medium TTL (~60s), OR none. Not B, not C.**
- *Why not B:* unlike saved routes, follow status is read on far fewer surfaces (just UserProfileScreen today) and is **about other users**, not a stable set owned by the current user, so an app-level Set is a poor fit (it would need every other user's id). 
- *Why A or none:* for the single-profile check, a tiny `Map<followeeId, {isFollowing, fetchedAt}>` with write-through on follow/unfollow avoids a refetch when navigating back to a profile you just followed. But given only one consumer, **"none" (keep `fetchIsFollowing` per screen mount) is an acceptable Phase 1 answer** — the win is marginal. If the per-row-button list surface from Q1 ever lands, revisit with A + write-through keyed by user id.
- *TTL (if A):* ~60s. *Invalidate:* on the user's own follow/unfollow (write-through), TTL otherwise, clear on sign-out. *Never C* — follow status should not survive a restart stale.

**New-beta tag (AC-180 `routes_with_new_beta`) → Option A (in-memory Map), very short TTL (~15–30s) OR none; invalidate on route-visit upsert. Not B, not C.**
- *Why not C:* the tag is inherently recency-relative (it compares `beta_videos.created_at` to `user_route_visits.last_viewed_at`); a persisted "has new beta" boolean goes stale the instant a new video is uploaded or the user visits the route. Persisting it (C) would surface a wrong dot after restart. 
- *Why A or none:* within a single session, HomeScreen and RouteListScreen both call `routes_with_new_beta(visibleIds)`; a short-TTL Map keyed by route id avoids recomputing for ids already resolved this session. But the **critical invalidation** is action-driven, not TTL: when the user opens a route (AC-179 fires the `user_route_visits` upsert), that route's tag must flip to false immediately — so the opening screen should **optimistically clear the cached tag for that route id** on visit, exactly like the like write-through. Given the RPC is already batched and cheap, **"none" (recompute per screen) is also perfectly acceptable for Phase 1**; the Map is an optimization only if profiling shows the repeated calls matter.
- *TTL (if A):* ~15–30s. *Invalidate:* immediately on route-visit upsert for that route id (write-through to false); TTL otherwise; clear on sign-out.

**Cross-cutting caution for any of these:** a module-level mutable cache (A) and a context (B) both hold per-user state that MUST be cleared on sign-out, or user B sees user A's cached likes/saves/follows after a logout-login on the same device. Whatever is adopted, the clear-on-auth-change hook is non-negotiable and should be wired through `useSession` (the existing reactive-session singleton). This is the single biggest risk in adding any client cache to this codebase and is called out as a condition in the Concerns below.

---

#### Question 3 — Confirm Decision 1 (AC-180 visibility scope, full Option B: all three filters server-side)

The human rejected the report-only filter (my prior recommendation (a)) and chose **full Option B**: `routes_with_new_beta` applies **all three** visibility filters server-side — AC-142 (reported/dismissed), AC-154 (blocked users), and AC-158 (beta-video privacy/follows). **This is architecturally sound. Confirmed, with three concrete query-correctness conditions the owning engineer (MOD-003) must meet.**

*Why it's sound:* pushing all three filters server-side makes the tag's "visible beta video" set **definitionally identical** to the set the user can actually open on the route-detail grid and in the viewer — so the worst case I flagged for option (a) (a tag with nothing behind it after the user taps in) cannot occur. That is a genuinely better user experience and removes the "best-effort indicator" caveat. The cost is exactly what I noted before: the three predicates now exist server-side **in addition to** wherever MOD-005 applies them client-side, so they can drift. Option B is the right call **provided the drift risk is contained by making the predicates reusable, not re-derived.**

*Updated query shape (replaces the sketch in the prior review's Item 2):*
```sql
CREATE FUNCTION routes_with_new_beta(route_ids uuid[])
RETURNS TABLE (route_id uuid) LANGUAGE sql STABLE SECURITY INVOKER
SET search_path = public AS $$
  SELECT r.id
  FROM unnest(route_ids) AS r(id)
  WHERE EXISTS (
    SELECT 1 FROM beta_videos bv
    WHERE bv.route_id = r.id
      -- AC-142 canonical predicate (copied verbatim, never re-derived):
      AND (bv.is_reported = false OR bv.report_dismissed = true)
      -- AC-154 blocked-user filter (symmetric block):
      AND NOT EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM blocks b
        WHERE (b.blocker_id = auth.uid() AND b.blocked_id = bv.user_id)
           OR (b.blocker_id = bv.user_id AND b.blocked_id = auth.uid())
      )
      -- AC-158 beta-video privacy: uploader public, OR self, OR (followers_only AND I follow them):
      AND (
        bv.user_id = auth.uid()
        OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM users u WHERE u.id = bv.user_id
                   AND u.privacy = 'public')
        OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM follows f
                   WHERE f.follower_id = auth.uid() AND f.followee_id = bv.user_id)
      )
      AND bv.created_at > COALESCE(
            (SELECT urv.last_viewed_at FROM user_route_visits urv
             WHERE urv.user_id = auth.uid() AND urv.route_id = r.id),
            'epoch'::timestamptz)
  );
$$;
```

**Concerns with the query (conditions on confirming Option B):**
1. **The three predicates MUST match MOD-005's canonical definitions exactly, and the privacy predicate in particular must be reconciled with the real `users` privacy model before this is written.** The AC-158 clause above is my reconstruction of "public OR self OR followers-only-and-following"; the exact column/enum (`users.privacy`, the `followers_only` value name, whether there is a third `private` state) must be read from the live schema / MOD-001's spec, not guessed. **I do not have the `users` privacy enum confirmed in front of me** — the engineer must confirm the actual column and values and adjust the clause. Flagging this rather than asserting it, per the "don't guess, confirm against evidence" rule.
2. **Drift control:** AC-142 already has a canonical-predicate convention ("Beta-Video Visibility Has One Canonical Exclusion Predicate") demanding verbatim reuse. AC-154 and AC-158, once pushed server-side, now need the same discipline — otherwise the client-side AC-154/AC-158 paths and this RPC can silently disagree. Best mitigation: if MOD-005/MOD-006 already expose these as SQL (e.g. a blocks predicate used in `get_activity_feed`), **reuse that exact SQL** here; do not hand-write a parallel copy. This should be a documented condition in the MOD-003 spec (see Proposed Shared Convention 2).
3. **Performance:** three correlated `EXISTS` subqueries per candidate beta video, across all `route_ids`, is heavier than the report-only version. For Phase 1 route-list sizes this is fine, but it makes the `(route_id)` index on `user_route_visits` (recommended in the prior review) and existing indexes on `blocks(blocker_id, blocked_id)` and `follows(follower_id, followee_id)` load-bearing. Confirm those indexes exist; the `follows`/`blocks` ones almost certainly already do (they back the feed RPC), but verify before QA handoff.
4. **Verify live before done:** this RPC composes four predicates and a correlated recency check — exactly the kind of multi-predicate SQL that mocked unit tests cannot validate. Exercise it against the live/local Supabase instance (Studio SQL editor or raw RPC call) before marking the AC done, per the existing manual-verification convention.

**Net:** Option B confirmed as architecturally sound and the better UX. It supersedes recommendation (a) in the prior review. Adopt the updated query shape above, subject to conditions 1–4 — condition 1 (confirm the real privacy model) must be resolved before Doc-Sync writes the MOD-003 spec so the spec carries the correct predicate.

---

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):
- **Any client cache (Options A or B, Question 2) MUST clear on auth change.** A module-level Map or a context holding per-user like/save/follow/new-beta state will leak across a logout→login on the same device if not cleared. Wire a clear-on-sign-out through the existing `useSession` singleton. This is a hard condition on adopting any caching, not a nice-to-have.
- **AC-180 Option B requires the real `users` privacy model to be confirmed before the RPC is written** (Question 3, condition 1). The privacy clause above is a reconstruction; the actual column/enum values must be read from MOD-001's schema and must match MOD-005's client-side AC-158 path verbatim. Resolve before Doc-Sync writes the MOD-003 spec.
- **Do not build the follow-status batch RPC or the per-row follow-button caching speculatively.** No current surface renders per-row follow state; building `follow_status_batch` now is premature. Defer until a surface actually needs it (Question 1).

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):
- Add `get_like_info_batch(target_type, target_ids[])` (MOD-006, SECURITY INVOKER) and switch **FeedScreen only** to it (1+N → 1+1). Leave the reel viewer's per-index `fetchLikeInfo` unchanged — it is on-demand single-item, not an N+1.
- Do **not** convert `fetchSavedRouteIds` to a per-ID batch RPC — it is already a single-query set fetch and a batch RPC would be a regression. If save status needs sharing across RouteList/RouteDetail/Home, use a save-status **context** (Option B) around the existing single fetch, not a new RPC.
- Caching, if adopted: like → Map + 30s TTL + optimistic write-through; save → context Set, action-invalidated, optional AsyncStorage cold-start hydration; follow → Map + ~60s TTL or none; new-beta tag → Map + short TTL with visit-upsert write-through, or none. "None" is a legitimate Phase 1 answer for follow and new-beta; the only clearly worthwhile caches are like-count write-through (agreement across reel viewer + feed) and the save-status context (eliminates repeated `fetchSavedRouteIds` across three surfaces).
- For AC-180 Option B: reuse MOD-005/MOD-006's existing blocked/privacy SQL predicates verbatim rather than hand-writing parallel copies; confirm supporting indexes on `user_route_visits(route_id)`, `blocks`, and `follows`; verify the composed RPC live before QA handoff.

**Approved** (looks solid as-is):
- Current **save status** access (`fetchSavedRouteIds` single-query set fetch, membership tested client-side) — already optimal, do not change the data-access shape.
- Current **reel-viewer like** access (one `fetchLikeInfo` per active index) — correct on-demand single-item fetch, not an N+1; leave as-is.
- Current **single-profile follow** check (`fetchIsFollowing` once per UserProfileScreen) — correct as-is.
- **AC-180 full Option B** (all three visibility filters server-side) as a design decision — architecturally sound and the better UX; confirmed, subject to the four query-correctness conditions above.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for Doc-Sync to carry into production.md, pending PM log + human approval):

1. **Batch a Per-Item Read Only When It Is Genuinely N-Across-Items; Never Batch a Set-Membership or On-Demand Single-Item Read** — A batched `fetch...(ids[])` / SECURITY INVOKER RPC over the visible ID set is the right tool ONLY when a screen would otherwise issue one call per visible item (true N+1), e.g. per-video like info across a feed, or the AC-180 new-beta tag across a route list. It is the WRONG tool for (a) a set-membership check already served by a single query returning the user's full owned-ID set (e.g. `fetchSavedRouteIds` → test membership with `Set.has()` client-side) — batching that is a regression; and (b) an on-demand single-item fetch for the one item currently in view (e.g. the reel viewer's per-swipe like fetch) — batching prefetches items the user may never see and risks staleness. Extends `Personal Cross-Module Data Overlays`: the batch shape is for N-across-items reads, not for every multi-call pattern.

2. **Server-Side Reuse of a Visibility/Access Predicate Must Copy the Canonical SQL Verbatim, Never Re-Derive It** — When an RPC pushes an existing access/visibility filter server-side (e.g. AC-180's `routes_with_new_beta` applying the AC-154 blocked-user and AC-158 beta-video-privacy filters in addition to the AC-142 reported predicate), it must reuse the canonical SQL predicate verbatim — ideally the exact SQL already used by the owning module's feed/listing RPC (`get_activity_feed` for blocks/privacy) — not a hand-written parallel copy. This extends the existing `Beta-Video Visibility Has One Canonical Exclusion Predicate` convention from the AC-142 predicate to the AC-154 and AC-158 predicates whenever they are enforced server-side, because a parallel copy will silently drift from the client-side path and surface a tag/filter that disagrees with what the user can actually open.

3. **Any Client-Side Cache (In-Memory Map or Context) Holding Per-User State Must Clear on Auth Change** — This codebase has no data-fetching library (no React Query/SWR); any caching added (module-level `Map`, app-level context) holds per-user state in process memory and MUST be cleared on sign-out/auth change, wired through the `useSession` singleton. Otherwise a logout→login on the same device leaks the previous user's cached likes/saves/follows/new-beta tags to the next user. Caching strategy is chosen per data characteristic, not globally: high-churn counts (likes) → in-memory Map + short TTL + optimistic write-through on the user's own mutation; user-owned low-churn sets read on many screens (saved routes) → app-level context Set, invalidated by the user's own action (optional AsyncStorage cold-start hydration for slow-changing sets only); recency-relative tags (new-beta) and other-user relational state (follow) → in-memory Map + short TTL with action write-through, or no cache at all. Never persist high-churn or recency-relative values to AsyncStorage — a stale persisted count/tag after restart is worse than refetching.

---

### Review — 2026-10-13 — change (Rev 35 architecture review: 5 flagged items — AC-174 banner source, AC-179/180 user_route_visits, AC-183 shared route card, AC-184 global header, AC-185 swipe-back)

**Context**: PRD Rev 35 (HomeScreen/navigation/global-UI overhaul) flagged five items for Tech Lead sign-off before Doc-Sync runs. Advisory review of each: table/schema/RLS choices, module ownership, query shapes, and cross-cutting mount strategy. No files edited except this review and the Proposed Shared Conventions below. Every recommendation is advisory — the human and PM decide what to adopt; Doc-Sync carries the approved conventions into `production.md`, and the owning-module assignments flow into the module specs on the next sync. These five sit alongside the already-flagged Rev 35 DB items that are not in this batch (`user_route_visits` is here; `saved_routes`, `beta_videos` reporting columns, and the `ascent_style` enum swap were reviewed in prior entries and are unchanged).

---

#### Item 1 — AC-174: Admin-managed Events Banner data source

**Recommendation: Option B — a dedicated `admin_banners` table.** Reject reusing `app_settings`.

Rationale:
- **`app_settings` is architecturally wrong for this.** Per the established `app_settings Table Pattern` convention (production.md), `app_settings` has **RLS enabled with NO policies and NO client-facing grants** — authenticated and anon users receive **zero rows**. It is read **only inside SECURITY DEFINER functions**. AC-174 requires **read-all-authenticated**, which is the exact opposite of that table's contract. Reusing it would force either (a) punching a client-facing SELECT grant into `app_settings` (which breaks the "feature toggles are never client-readable" guarantee that `submit_route` auto-approve and other DEFINER-gated toggles rely on), or (b) wrapping the banner read in a DEFINER RPC for no reason. Neither is acceptable. `app_settings` stays DEFINER-only.
- **The banner is richer than a key/value string.** AC-112 (one banner at a time, hidden when none active) plus the natural operator need (title, body, optional image, an active flag, optional expiry) do not fit a single `value TEXT` cell without serializing JSON into it — which loses column-level typing, makes the "is there an active banner right now?" query awkward, and gives the Studio-editing operator a worse authoring surface.
- A dedicated table is one migration — cheap, and it is the clean home for the read-all/write-admin-only RLS split the AC asks for.

Proposed schema (advisory — final wording is Doc-Sync's to carry in):
```
admin_banners
 - id uuid PK default gen_random_uuid()
 - title text (nullable)
 - body text (nullable)
 - image_url text (nullable)
 - active boolean NOT NULL default false
 - expires_at timestamptz (nullable)
 - created_at timestamptz NOT NULL default now()
 - updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL default now()
```
RLS (read-all-authenticated / write-admin-only, using the existing `public.is_admin()` helper — do NOT inline the JWT check):
- SELECT policy: `TO authenticated USING (true)` (all authenticated users read).
- INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy: `USING (public.is_admin()) WITH CHECK (public.is_admin())`.
- `GRANT SELECT` to `authenticated`; admin writes go through service_role (Studio) which bypasses RLS, so the admin-write policy is belt-and-suspenders for the `is_admin()` claim path (Phase 1.5-ready, consistent with the `Admin Identity` convention).

"One banner at a time" selection (AC-112): the read query selects the single active, non-expired banner, most-recent first:
```sql
SELECT * FROM admin_banners
WHERE active = true AND (expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > now())
ORDER BY created_at DESC
LIMIT 1;
```
If none returned, the section hides (AC-112). The client never needs to enforce "exactly one" — `LIMIT 1` on the ordered active set does it, and the operator is responsible for not leaving multiple banners `active` (acceptable for a Studio-only, solo-operator surface in Phase 1).

**Owning module: MOD-012 (Home).** MOD-012 owns the Events Banner section and the HomeScreen that renders it, so it owns both the `admin_banners` migration and the read service (`fetchActiveBanner()`). This mirrors MOD-012 already owning the Home surface and its data reads. No other module reads banners in Phase 1. This supersedes the existing `src/lib/banners.ts` static-banner convention for this surface — the banner is now data-sourced, not a static constant; `banners.ts` becomes dead/removable for the admin banner (coordinate with Doc-Sync whether to retire it).

---

#### Item 2 — AC-179/AC-180: `user_route_visits` table + the "new beta video" query

**Table placement / ownership: MOD-003 (Route Catalog).** MOD-003 owns `routes`, owns the route detail screen where the upsert fires (AC-179), owns `saved_routes`, and already owns the AC-180/AC-181 tag logic per its revised Purpose. Placing `user_route_visits` anywhere else splits route-scoped ownership. MOD-003 owns the migration and the upsert service.

**RLS: own-rows-only, confirmed.** Mirror the confirmed `saved_routes` / `saved_gyms` design exactly: `user_id DEFAULT auth.uid()` FK to `public.users(id)` ON DELETE CASCADE, `route_id` FK to `routes(id)` ON DELETE CASCADE, composite PK `(user_id, route_id)`, own-rows RLS for SELECT / INSERT / UPDATE (`user_id = auth.uid()`), `GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` to `authenticated`. **Note the one deliberate difference from `saved_routes`: this table needs UPDATE** (saved_routes was INSERT/DELETE only), because the upsert re-writes `last_viewed_at` on every visit. No DELETE grant is needed. The FK must target `public.users(id)` not `auth.users(id)` per the `User-Owned Tables That Participate in PostgREST Joins Reference public.users` convention (future-proofs an embedded join even if Phase 1 doesn't use one).

**Upsert pattern (AC-179), confirmed:**
```sql
INSERT INTO user_route_visits (user_id, route_id, last_viewed_at)
VALUES (auth.uid(), $1, now())
ON CONFLICT (user_id, route_id) DO UPDATE SET last_viewed_at = now();
```
Fires once per route detail open. `user_id` is NOT a client parameter — it derives from `auth.uid()` (the DEFAULT plus the RLS WITH CHECK enforces this). This is a plain authenticated upsert under RLS, not a DEFINER RPC — the user owns their own visit rows, so no privilege escalation is needed.

**Indexing:** the composite PK `(user_id, route_id)` already serves the per-(user,route) lookup for AC-180's tag check and the upsert conflict target — no extra index needed there. **Add a secondary index on `(route_id)`** only if AC-180 is implemented as a server-side join across many routes (see below). For the recommended client-side batch shape it is not strictly required, but it is cheap and harmless; I recommend adding `CREATE INDEX ON user_route_visits (route_id)` to keep the server-side option open and to support the AC-175 most-recently-viewed-gym derivation (which joins visits → routes → gym).

**AC-180 tag computation — owning module and query shape.**

*Owning module:* **MOD-003.** Its revised Purpose already claims "the 'new beta video' tag logic and rendering on route cards." The tag is route-scoped and belongs with the route card data. MOD-012's HomeScreen and MOD-003's RouteListScreen both *consume* it; MOD-003 *computes* it. MOD-005 is read-only here (its visibility predicate is reused, not re-owned).

*Query shape — recommend a SECURITY INVOKER RPC that takes a set of route IDs and returns the qualifying subset.* This is the cleanest fit for the existing conventions and avoids an N+1:

```sql
-- MOD-003-owned, SECURITY INVOKER (RLS scopes user_route_visits to auth.uid()
-- automatically; the beta-video visibility predicate is applied in-query).
CREATE FUNCTION routes_with_new_beta(route_ids uuid[])
RETURNS TABLE (route_id uuid) ... SECURITY INVOKER STABLE AS $$
  SELECT r.id
  FROM unnest(route_ids) AS r(id)
  WHERE EXISTS (
    SELECT 1 FROM beta_videos bv
    WHERE bv.route_id = r.id
      AND (bv.is_reported = false OR bv.report_dismissed = true)   -- AC-142 canonical predicate
      AND bv.created_at > COALESCE(
            (SELECT urv.last_viewed_at
             FROM user_route_visits urv
             WHERE urv.user_id = auth.uid() AND urv.route_id = r.id),
            'epoch'::timestamptz)   -- never-visited => epoch => any video qualifies (AC-180)
  );
$$;
```

Why this shape:
- **Batched, not N+1.** Follows the `Personal Cross-Module Data Overlays` convention: the consuming screen calls it **once per screen** with all visible route IDs (`routes_with_new_beta(visibleRouteIds)`), never once per card. Both HomeScreen (Projecting section) and RouteListScreen call it with their visible set.
- **SECURITY INVOKER + `auth.uid()`** means `user_route_visits` is RLS-scoped automatically and the tag is correctly per-viewer.
- **The never-visited case (AC-180) is handled by `COALESCE(..., 'epoch')`** — no visit row ⇒ every visible beta video is "newer than epoch" ⇒ route qualifies. Clean, no special-casing in the client.
- **Beta-video visibility:** the AC-142 reported-video predicate is reused verbatim in-query. **Important caveat for the owning engineer:** AC-180 says the "visible beta videos" set must also respect the **blocked-user filter (AC-154)** and **beta-video privacy (AC-158)**. AC-154 is currently a *client-side* filter over a fetched set (per its own AC wording and the existing MOD-005 implementation), and AC-158 composes a Follow-relationship check. Pushing all three into this RPC is possible but duplicates MOD-005's visibility logic server-side and risks it drifting from the client-side AC-154 path. **Flag for PM/human decision (see Concerns):** either (a) accept that the "new beta video" tag uses only the AC-142 predicate server-side and is a best-effort indicator that may occasionally over-count when a blocked/private video exists (low-stakes — it's a tag, not content exposure), or (b) require the RPC to also apply AC-154/AC-158 server-side, accepting the duplicated predicate. My recommendation is **(a) for Phase 1**: the tag is an advisory "something's new" dot, not a content surface; a blocked/followers-only video is never actually *shown* on the card (AC-154/AC-158 still gate the route detail grid and viewer), so the worst case is a tag with no visible new video behind it after the user opens the route. That is an acceptable Phase 1 trade-off and avoids duplicating the visibility stack. This is a PM/human call, not mine to decide.

*Alternative considered and not recommended:* computing the tag purely client-side by fetching each route's beta videos. Rejected — it is N+1 across the route list and re-implements the visibility predicate in the client for every surface. The RPC keeps one canonical definition.

---

#### Item 3 — AC-183: Shared route card component

**Recommendation: Option A — app-level `src/components/RouteCard.tsx`.** Reject Option B (MOD-003 exports it).

Rationale:
- **The existing `Cross-Module Imports` convention permits importing another module's public *service functions* and *navigator entry-point components* — not its internal `screens/` or `components/`.** A route card is a plain presentational `components/` item. If MOD-003 "exported" it, MOD-012 and MOD-008 would be importing from MOD-003's internal `components/` directory, which the convention explicitly forbids. So Option B as literally stated violates the import rule. The convention does **not** currently extend to UI components — and the right fix is not to loosen the cross-module rule (that invites every module reaching into every other module's components), but to put genuinely shared, module-agnostic presentational components where they belong: a top-level `src/components/`.
- **Precedent already exists for app-level shared code.** `src/lib/` holds cross-cutting non-component code (`theme.ts`, `i18n.ts`, `banners.ts`, `supabase.ts`) owned by no single module. A route card consumed by three unrelated modules (MOD-012 Home, MOD-003 RouteList, MOD-008 send history) is the component analogue. It belongs to the app, not to one module.
- **Avoids a circular/awkward dependency.** MOD-008 (send history) does not currently depend on MOD-003; making it import a MOD-003 component to render a send row would create a new cross-module coupling purely for a visual element.

**Ownership of the app-level component:** this is the one wrinkle — `src/components/RouteCard.tsx` is not inside any module directory, so "which engineer owns it" needs an explicit answer. **Recommend: MOD-012 (Home) owns app-level shared presentational components** as the aggregator/shell module, the same way it owns the cross-cutting shell. It is already the aggregator that composes other modules and the natural home for app-level UI concerns in this codebase. The alternative — a new "shared/app-shell" ownership — is heavier than warranted for Phase 1. (If the human prefers, MOD-003 could own the *file* while it lives at `src/components/` since the card is route-domain — but then the "no importing another module's internals" rule is satisfied by location, and the import path is app-level. I lean MOD-012 for consistency with it owning the other two Rev 35 cross-cutting surfaces, but this is a human call.)

**Import contract for consumers:** all three consumers import `RouteCard` from `src/components/RouteCard.tsx` (app-level path), never from a module directory. The card is **pure/presentational**: it takes a route-shaped prop plus a `hasNewBeta: boolean` prop (computed by the consumer via MOD-003's `routes_with_new_beta` RPC, Item 2) and an `onPress` callback. The card does **not** fetch the tag itself — keeping it presentational means it has no module-service dependency and the N+1 concern stays with the screen, not the card. `photo_url` is read straight off the route prop (no schema change, per AC-183).

**New Proposed Shared Convention needed** (below) to document that `src/components/` is the home for app-level shared presentational components, owned by MOD-012, imported by app-level path — because the current `Cross-Module Imports` convention has no provision for shared UI components and would otherwise be read as forbidding this.

---

#### Item 4 — AC-184: Global app-level header

**Owning module: MOD-012 (Home).** It owns the tab shell (`AppShell`) and is already the aggregator that composes MOD-007 (inbox) and MOD-010 (settings) via their public navigator entry points. The header wires to both, so MOD-012 is the correct owner. Confirmed.

**Where it mounts — recommend Option B (tab-shell level in MOD-012), NOT Option C (App.tsx root).** Reasoning:

- **The exclusion set rules out a naive app-root absolute overlay.** AC-184 excludes the header from (1) the fullscreen reel viewer (AC-133) and (2) auth/signed-out screens. Auth screens render *below* `AppShell` in the composition root (`SafeAreaProvider → ThemeProvider → AuthNavigator → AppShell`), so a header mounted at `App.tsx` root above everything (Option C) would either paint over the auth screens (wrong) or need conditional logic reaching up from deep screens to hide itself (fragile). Mounting at the **AppShell/tab-shell level** naturally excludes auth screens — `AppShell` only mounts *after* authentication, so the header simply doesn't exist on auth screens by construction.
- **The reel-viewer exclusion is cleanly handled** because the reel viewer is a full-screen RN `<Modal presentationStyle="overFullScreen">` (per the `Custom Fullscreen Media Viewers` convention) — a modal renders *above* the shell's header regardless, so the header is visually absent while the reel is open without any special hide logic. This is actually an argument for the header living at the shell level: the modal covers it for free.
- **Option A (each screen imports the header itself) is rejected** — it scatters a cross-cutting surface across every screen, guarantees drift, and makes the "except these screens" exclusion a per-screen opt-out that will be forgotten on new screens. One mount point, defined exclusions, is the maintainable choice.

Concretely: the header renders inside `AppShell`, above the active tab's navigator content and above the bottom tab bar's sibling (i.e., `AppShell` lays out `[GlobalHeader][active tab subtree (keep-alive)][TabBar]`). Because all three tab subtrees are kept alive via `display:none`/`flex` (existing convention), the single header sits above whichever tab is visible. Screens pushed *within* a tab's navigator (RouteDetail, UserProfile overlay, etc.) render inside the tab subtree, so the shell header remains above them — correct, since those screens should show the global header per AC-184. The reel-viewer modal and the (pre-shell) auth screens are the only exclusions, both handled structurally as above.

**Unread-count badge (AC-173) — recommend Option A (a context/provider at the shell level holding the unread count).** Reject Option B (each screen fetches independently):
- There is now exactly **one** header instance (shell-level), so there is exactly **one** consumer of the count — the badge on that single bell. Option B's "each screen fetches" problem largely dissolves because there is only one header, but the count still needs to (a) load on shell mount, (b) refresh when the inbox batch-marks-read (AC-172) so the badge clears, and (c) ideally refresh when a new notification arrives.
- **Recommend a small `UnreadCountProvider` context owned by MOD-007**, mounted by MOD-012 inside `AppShell` wrapping the header. It exposes `{ unreadCount, refresh() }`. MOD-007 owns it because MOD-007 owns the notification domain and the `Notification` read. The header (MOD-012) subscribes to it for the badge; `NotificationInboxScreen` (MOD-007) calls `refresh()` after its AC-172 batch mark-read so the badge clears (AC-173). This keeps the count's source of truth in the notification module and gives the shell a clean subscribe-only surface. This mirrors the existing pattern of `useSession` being a singleton consumed reactively across the shell.
- Phase 1 scope note: live push-driven refresh (badge increments the instant a push arrives) is a nice-to-have; a `refresh()` on shell focus / inbox close is sufficient for AC-173 as written (badge reflects unread count, clears on inbox open). Real-time subscription can be a Phase 2 refinement.

**ProfileScreen (MOD-001) loses its bell + settings header icons — confirmed.** AC-171 (revised) and AC-184 move the bell to the global header; the settings control also moves to the global header's right side. Per the existing `Relocated Cross-Module Entry Points Are Removed from the Old Location` convention, **MOD-001's engineer must remove the bell and the settings affordance from the ProfileScreen header** so exactly one entry path exists for each. Two bells (one in ProfileScreen, one in the global header) would be a defect, not redundancy. This is a MOD-001 code change to coordinate alongside the MOD-012 header build. (Note the Logout control already moved to SettingsScreen per AC-167/AC-168 — this is the same relocation pattern applied to the bell and settings icons.)

---

#### Item 5 — AC-185: Swipe-to-go-back

**Implementation approach — recommend `PanResponder` (RN core), NOT `react-native-gesture-handler`.** This is a direct application of the existing `Prefer RN PanResponder for Simple Swipe Gestures` convention, which states verbatim: do not add `react-native-gesture-handler` (a native dependency requiring a rebuild + root provider) for gestures expressible with core `PanResponder` (single-axis swipe, threshold-based). A left-to-right edge swipe that fires an `onBack` callback past a distance/velocity threshold is exactly a single-axis threshold gesture. The reel viewer already uses `PanResponder` for its swipe-up/down navigation (per that convention and the `Custom Fullscreen Media Viewers` work), so there is precedent and no new native dependency, no `GestureHandlerRootView` provider, and no native rebuild. **This overrides the item's premise that gesture-handler is "the natural choice"** — in this codebase, which deliberately runs no `NavigationContainer` and prefers RN built-ins, `PanResponder` is the house pattern. Adding gesture-handler now would also pull in a root provider the composition root currently does not have, which is a larger change than the behavior warrants.

**Component: `SwipeBackWrapper` at `src/components/SwipeBackWrapper.tsx`** (app-level, same reasoning and ownership as the route card in Item 3 — MOD-012 owns app-level shared components). It wraps a screen's content and accepts an `onBack: () => void` prop, firing it when a left-to-right horizontal swipe exceeds the threshold. Per the convention, **always pair the gesture with the existing on-screen back control as the accessible fallback** — the back icon stays; the swipe is additive. The wrapper calls the *same* `onBack` the back icon already calls, so there is one pop path.

**Conflict mitigation (horizontal scroll views) — real, and `PanResponder` handles it via the responder negotiation, not `simultaneousHandlers`.** `simultaneousHandlers`/`activeOffsetX` are gesture-handler props and do not apply to `PanResponder`. The `PanResponder` equivalents:
- Gate `onMoveShouldSetPanResponder` on a **horizontal, rightward, edge-originating** gesture: require `dx > threshold`, `dx > |dy|` (clearly horizontal), and ideally that the touch **started near the left edge** (`evt.nativeEvent.pageX < ~30` at grant) — a left-edge-origin requirement is the standard way to avoid stealing horizontal-scroll gestures that begin in the middle of the screen (V-grade tabs, the Projecting Routes horizontal strip). An inner horizontal `ScrollView` in the middle of the screen never starts at the left edge, so the wrapper won't claim it.
- The reel viewer is **excluded** entirely (see below), so its horizontal/vertical swipe navigation never contends with the wrapper.
- Flag for the owning engineer: the left-edge-origin heuristic is the primary conflict defense; verify on device against the Projecting Routes horizontal strip and the V-grade filter tabs specifically (both named in the item). This is a device-verification item, not something unit tests can confirm (consistent with the native-build/device-verification conventions).

**Reel viewer (AC-133) explicitly excluded — confirmed.** The reel viewer owns its own full-screen swipe navigation (up/down for next/previous, per AC-039) and its own back control (AC-133). It must **not** be wrapped in `SwipeBackWrapper`; its horizontal space and gesture handling belong to the viewer. The exclusion is structural: the reel viewer is a MOD-005 modal and simply is not wrapped. (Auth screens likewise have no back-stack to pop and are not wrapped.)

**Owning module: MOD-012** (owns app-level shared components, consistent with Items 3 and the header). Individual screens across modules opt in by wrapping their content in `SwipeBackWrapper` and passing their existing `onBack`. Because the app uses custom state-machine navigators (no `NavigationContainer`), "pop" is whatever `onBack` each screen already does — the wrapper is navigator-agnostic by design, which is the right fit for this codebase.

---

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):
- **AC-180 visibility scope is ambiguous and needs a PM/human decision.** AC-180 says the "new beta video" set respects AC-142 (reported) AND AC-154 (blocked) AND AC-158 (beta-video privacy). AC-142 is a clean SQL predicate reusable server-side; AC-154 is currently a client-side filter and AC-158 composes a Follow check. Decide: **(a)** the tag RPC uses only the AC-142 predicate (recommended for Phase 1 — the tag is an advisory indicator, the actual video is still gated by AC-154/AC-158 on the route detail grid, so nothing leaks; worst case is a tag with nothing new behind it after opening), or **(b)** push AC-154/AC-158 into the RPC too, duplicating MOD-005's visibility stack server-side. This governs the exact query shape and must be resolved before Doc-Sync writes the MOD-003 spec.
- **App-level `src/components/` has no current owner or convention.** Items 3 and 5 both introduce app-level shared components (`RouteCard`, `SwipeBackWrapper`). The `Cross-Module Imports` convention as written would be read as forbidding modules from importing them (they're "components"). A new Proposed Shared Convention (below) must be adopted to legitimize `src/components/` as app-level, MOD-012-owned, app-path-imported — otherwise Doc-Sync has no basis to write these into specs without appearing to violate the import rule. Needs PM to log it and Doc-Sync to sync it.
- **AC-174 retires the static `src/lib/banners.ts` for the admin banner.** The existing `Shared Non-Module Constants` convention points the banner at a static constant file; AC-174 makes it data-sourced. Confirm with Doc-Sync whether `banners.ts` is removed or repurposed, so the convention and the code don't contradict each other after sync.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):
- Assign **MOD-012 as the owner of the new `src/components/` app-level shared-component directory** (RouteCard, SwipeBackWrapper) and of the `admin_banners` migration + read service. Assign **MOD-003** as owner of `user_route_visits` (migration + upsert + `routes_with_new_beta` RPC). Assign **MOD-007** as owner of the `UnreadCountProvider` context (consumed by the MOD-012 header). These three ownership lines should be written into the specs by Doc-Sync.
- Add a `(route_id)` index on `user_route_visits` even under the recommended client-batched RPC shape — cheap, and it keeps the server-side join option and the AC-175 recently-viewed-gym derivation performant.
- For AC-185, have the owning engineer device-verify the left-edge-origin heuristic against the two named horizontal scrollers (Projecting Routes strip, V-grade tabs) before QA handoff — this is a device check, not a unit-test check.

**Approved** (looks solid as-is):
- The AC-179 `user_route_visits` upsert pattern (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (user_id, route_id) DO UPDATE SET last_viewed_at = now()`), own-rows-only RLS, and composite PK as proposed in the item and §9 Data Model are correct and consistent with the confirmed `saved_routes`/`saved_gyms` design — adopt as-is (with the one noted addition of an UPDATE grant, which `saved_routes` did not need).
- Confirming MOD-012 as owner of the global header (AC-184) and MOD-003 as owner of the AC-180 tag computation matches both modules' already-written Rev 35 Purpose statements — no restructuring needed.
- The PanResponder/`src/components` direction for AC-185 and the dedicated-table direction for AC-174 both fall straight out of existing conventions (`Prefer RN PanResponder…`, `app_settings Table Pattern`) — no new architectural ground, just correct application.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for Doc-Sync to carry into production.md, pending PM log + human approval):

1. **App-Level Shared Presentational Components** — Genuinely module-agnostic presentational components consumed by three or more unrelated modules (e.g. `RouteCard`, `SwipeBackWrapper`) live at top-level `src/components/`, not inside any module's `components/` directory. This is the component analogue of `src/lib/` for cross-cutting non-component code. These components are **pure/presentational**: they take data + callback props and own no module service dependency (the consuming screen does the fetching and passes results down), so they create no cross-module coupling. MOD-012 (the aggregator/shell module) owns the `src/components/` directory. All modules import these via the app-level path `src/components/<Name>`. This is an explicit, narrow exception to the `Cross-Module Imports` rule's ban on importing another module's internal `components/`: it applies ONLY to components that live at `src/components/` (owned by no single module), never to reaching into a specific module's internal `components/` directory — that remains forbidden.

2. **Admin-Managed Content Uses a Dedicated Read-All/Write-Admin Table, Never `app_settings`** — `app_settings` is DEFINER-only (RLS enabled, no policies, no client grants; read only inside SECURITY DEFINER functions) and must stay that way. Any admin-authored content that authenticated clients must READ directly (e.g. the Events Banner) uses a dedicated table with a read-all-authenticated SELECT policy (`TO authenticated USING (true)`, `GRANT SELECT`) and admin-only write policies gated by `public.is_admin()` (never an inlined JWT check). The admin authors content via Supabase Studio (service_role bypasses RLS). Do not punch a client-facing SELECT grant into `app_settings` to serve client-readable content.

3. **"Is-New / recency" Indicators Use a Batched SECURITY INVOKER RPC Over the Visible ID Set** — A per-item "new/unseen" tag that compares another module's content timestamps against a per-user last-seen table (e.g. the "new beta video" tag comparing `beta_videos.created_at` against `user_route_visits.last_viewed_at`) is computed by a `SECURITY INVOKER STABLE` RPC owned by the module that owns the item domain. The RPC takes the screen's full visible ID array and returns the qualifying subset — called ONCE per screen, never once per card (extends `Personal Cross-Module Data Overlays` to the is-new/recency case). SECURITY INVOKER lets RLS scope the per-user table to `auth.uid()` automatically. A missing last-seen row is treated as "everything is new" via `COALESCE(last_seen, 'epoch')`. Reused beta-video visibility predicates (e.g. AC-142's `is_reported = false OR report_dismissed = true`) must be copied verbatim from the canonical predicate, never re-derived.

---

### Review — 2026-10-11 — audit (pre-build completeness check for MOD-009 / MOD-010 / MOD-011 — how much is already implemented from earlier cross-module work?)

**Context**: Advisory audit requested by the coordinator before formally building MOD-009 (Moderation), MOD-010 (Localization & Theming), and MOD-011 (Analytics). The coordinator suspected some ACs may already have been implemented as a side effect of earlier cross-module work. Each module's spec was audited against the actual codebase (source, migrations, Edge Functions, locale catalogs, package.json). Advisory only — no files edited. This is not a phase-end Integration & Optimization Review; it is a build-readiness audit.

**Headline finding**: MOD-009 and MOD-010 are effectively fully built and already wired into their consuming modules (MOD-005, MOD-006, MOD-001). MOD-011 (Analytics / PostHog) is **100% not started** — zero code exists. The PM/coordinator should treat MOD-009 and MOD-010 as "verify + QA" work, not "build" work, and treat MOD-011 as the only genuinely greenfield module of the three.

#### MOD-009 — Moderation (Report & Block)

Files present: `src/modules/mod-moderation/{types.ts, moderation-service.ts}`, `components/{ModerationActions.tsx, ReportSheet.tsx}`, `screens/OtherUserProfileScreen.tsx`. Migrations present: `20260929000004_mod_009_moderation.sql`, `..._005_mod_009_block_feed_filter.sql`, `..._006_mod_009_fix_follows_block.sql`. Edge Function present: `supabase/functions/report-digest/index.ts`.

- **AC-080** (reportUser RPC, user-profile report) — **DONE.** `reportUser(targetUserId, category, note?)` inserts into `reports` with `status='open'` (moderation-service.ts L40–63). `reports` table + RLS (`reports_insert_own`, service-role-only SELECT) created in migration 004. Reached from both MOD-006's `UserProfileScreen` three-dots sheet and the MOD-009 `ReportSheet`.
- **AC-081** (reporter confirmation state) — **DONE.** `ReportSheet.tsx` renders a confirmation view after a successful submit (L109–123).
- **AC-082** (blockUser, hide blocked user's content) — **DONE.** `blockUser` inserts into `blocks` (idempotent on 23505); `get_activity_feed` filters blocked users from both `ascent_items` and `video_items` (migration 005).
- **AC-083** (blocked user cannot follow blocker) — **DONE.** `follows_insert_own` policy recreated with a symmetric block check, plus the `trg_remove_follows_on_block` AFTER-INSERT trigger that deletes existing follows in both directions (migration 006). This was a QA-found bug that is already fixed.
- **AC-084** (symmetric content hiding) — **DONE.** `get_activity_feed` checks both block directions in `qualifying_followees` and again (defense-in-depth) in both item CTEs (migration 005). `fetchBlockedUserIds()` returns both directions (moderation-service.ts L141–165).
- **AC-151** (report action also calls blockUser sequentially) — **DONE.** Implemented in MOD-006's `UserProfileScreen.tsx` three-dots "Report" handler: `await reportUser(targetUserId, 'inappropriate')` then `await blockUser(targetUserId)` (L254–255), defaulting category to `'inappropriate'` with no note, exactly per Rev 20. Covered by `UserProfileScreen.test.tsx` (AC-151 test).
- **AC-152** (unblockUser) — **DONE.** `unblockUser(blockedUserId)` deletes the block row (moderation-service.ts L112–128); consumed by both `ModerationActions` and MOD-010's `BlockedAccountsScreen`.
- **AC-153** (UserProfileScreen block-status check — MOD-006 integration) — **DONE.** MOD-006's `UserProfileScreen` imports `blockUser/reportUser/unblockUser` and tracks `isBlockedByMe` state (L114, L173), driving the three-dots sheet. Covered by `UserProfileScreen.test.tsx`.
- **AC-154** (beta-video list filters blocked users client-side — MOD-005 integration) — **DONE.** `mod-beta-video/components/BetaVideoSection.tsx` imports and calls `fetchBlockedUserIds()` (L51, L143) with graceful-degradation on failure; covered by `BetaVideoSection.test.tsx` AC-154 test.
- **AC-155** (Blocked Accounts screen — MOD-010 integration point) — **DONE on both sides.** The MOD-009 service half (`fetchMyBlockedUsers()`, moderation-service.ts L201–228) and the MOD-010 UI half (`BlockedAccountsScreen.tsx`) both exist and are wired. See MOD-010 below.
- **AC-085** (6-hourly Edge Function email digest) — **PARTIAL.** The function exists (`report-digest/index.ts`): it queries `reports WHERE status='open' AND created_at < now()-12h`, builds a correct digest body, and reads `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (not the reserved `SUPABASE_` prefix) per spec. **What is missing / unverified:** (1) it does **not actually send email** — the "send" path only does a GET to `/auth/v1/admin/users` to probe reachability and then `console.log`s the body; real delivery (Resend/SMTP via pg_net) is a documented TODO. The spec's AC-085 wording ("emails the admin") is therefore not literally satisfied — only logged. (2) The 6-hour **cron schedule registration is not verifiable from the repo** — it must be registered in the Supabase dashboard or via `functions deploy --schedule`; no evidence it is scheduled. (3) Deployment status to the live project is unverified. These three are operational/delivery gaps, not code-structure gaps.

**MOD-009 verdict**: All app-facing ACs (080–084, 151–155) are implemented and cross-wired. The only real gap is **AC-085 actual email delivery + cron registration**, which is an Edge-Function delivery/ops concern, not core moderation logic.
