#### MOD-011 — Analytics

- **AC-100** (PostHog events: signup, home gym set, first send, first beta video upload, beta video liked, session start) — **NOT STARTED.** There is no `src/modules/mod-analytics/` directory (it does not exist), no `posthog` dependency in `package.json`, no `posthog` import anywhere in `src/`, and none of the event-name constants (`signup_completed`, `home_gym_set`, `first_send_logged`, `first_beta_video_uploaded`, `beta_video_liked`, `session_start`) appear anywhere in the codebase.
- **AC-101** (no PII beyond user_id) — **NOT STARTED (vacuously — no analytics code exists to evaluate).**

**MOD-011 verdict**: Entirely greenfield. This is the one module of the three that is genuinely a "build from scratch" effort: install the PostHog client SDK, add the client init + event-name constants (MOD-011 owns these), then wire the six instrumentation call sites into their owning modules (signup → MOD-001, home gym set → MOD-001/MOD-012, first send → MOD-004, first beta video → MOD-005, like → MOD-005/MOD-006, session_start → app root). Note the cross-module wiring: although MOD-011 lists "Integration Points: none", in practice every event except `session_start` fires from a *different* module's action site, so building MOD-011 touches several modules' code. The engineer-mod-analytics agent will need those modules' cooperation (or the coordinator must route each call-site insertion to the owning engineer).

---

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):
- **AC-085 is not fully met** despite the Edge Function existing: no real email is sent (only logged), and the 6-hour cron schedule is not registered/verifiable from the repo. Decide whether AC-085 can be marked PASS on "logged digest + Studio review" as a Phase 1 fallback (the function's own comments claim this is acceptable), or whether real Resend/SMTP delivery + a registered cron schedule are required before sign-off. This is a PM/human decision, not a Tech Lead decision.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):
- Reclassify MOD-009 and MOD-010 from "to build" to "to verify + QA" in whatever phase tracking the coordinator maintains. Running the normal QA pass per AC is still warranted (especially the 4-way dark×zh-TW matrix for MOD-010 screens and the symmetric-block behavior for MOD-009), but no greenfield engineering is expected.
- For MOD-011, decide up front how the cross-module instrumentation is routed: a single engineer-mod-analytics owning the client + constants, with each call-site insertion delegated to the owning module's engineer, keeps module ownership clean and avoids one agent editing six modules' source.
- Confirm whether the i18n zh-TW key-parity CI gate (an explicit MOD-010 Key Implementation Note) exists; if not, log it as an outstanding MOD-010 deliverable.

**Approved** (looks solid as-is):
- MOD-009 block symmetry is implemented with defense-in-depth (both the `qualifying_followees` filter and per-CTE block checks in `get_activity_feed`), and the AC-083 follow-prevention bug was already caught and fixed with both an RLS check and a server-side trigger. This is a robust implementation.
- MOD-010 cleanly respects module ownership: it reads/writes `beta_video_privacy` only through MOD-001's public service and consumes MOD-009's block services by direct import, exactly as the specs' Cross-Module Imports convention requires.

### Review — 2026-10-11 — blocker (MOD-007 notification pipeline produces no Notification rows even though likes/pg_net now work)

**Context**: Advisory review requested by the coordinator. pg_net is now enabled (which fixed likes reverting); the Edge Function `notify-beta-video-like` is deployed to project `hxvzjpynhdexhtoccmmh`; but a beta-video like produces no `Notification` row. Four suspected gaps were raised: (1) `app_settings.notify_edge_function_url` possibly empty, (2) `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` secret possibly unset, (3) whether the function is invoked at all, (4) pg_net errors. Advisory only — no source files edited. Any fix routes through `engineer-mod-notifications` (or PM if the trigger-payload change is treated as a spec change).

**Evidence I could gather directly (non-secret):**
- `supabase functions list` → `notify-beta-video-like` is ACTIVE, version 2, and crucially **`verify_jwt: true`**.
- `supabase secrets list` (digests only, not plaintext) → `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` **EXISTS**, updated 2026-10-11T00:08:36Z (most recently set). `EXPO_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` etc. all present. So the Edge Function's `Deno.env.get('SERVICE_ROLE_KEY')` and `Deno.env.get('SUPABASE_URL')` will both resolve.
- `supabase/config.toml` → **no** `[functions.notify-beta-video-like]` block, so there is no `verify_jwt = false` override; the deployed default (`true`) stands.
- The trigger body (`supabase/migrations/20260924000007_mod_007_notifications.sql` lines 315-319) sends `net.http_post` with **only** `headers := jsonb_build_object('Content-Type','application/json')` — **no Authorization header at all.**

**Evidence I could NOT gather (requires DB password or Dashboard — human must produce):**
- The current value of `app_settings.notify_edge_function_url` (needs an authenticated SQL query; I will not fish for the DB password).
- Edge Function invocation logs (Dashboard → Functions → logs).
- `net._http_response` / `net.http_request_queue` rows (needs SQL).

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):

- **PRIMARY ROOT CAUSE — `verify_jwt: true` + trigger sends no Authorization header (guaranteed 401 at the gateway).** This is the highest-confidence finding and it is independent of the `app_settings` URL. Even if the URL is set correctly and `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is present, the Supabase Edge gateway rejects any request to a `verify_jwt: true` function that has no valid JWT in the `Authorization` header **before the function body runs**. The trigger's `net.http_post` sends only `Content-Type`. Result: pg_net records an HTTP response (a 401), the function's own logs show **zero invocations** (the body never executes), no `Notification` row, no push. This exactly matches the reported symptom (likes work, pg_net fires, but nothing downstream happens). Two mutually-exclusive fixes — the human/PM should choose one:
  - **Option A (recommended): make the trigger send the service-role JWT as a Bearer token.** Add an `Authorization: Bearer <service_role_key>` header to the `net.http_post` call. The trigger function is already `SECURITY DEFINER`; read the key from `app_settings` (same pattern as the URL) or Vault — do NOT hardcode it in the migration and do NOT commit it. This keeps `verify_jwt: true` (defense in depth) and is the posture the spec implies ("the function reads SERVICE_ROLE_KEY from Supabase secrets" presumes the caller authenticates). Note the function body today does NOT itself validate the caller's JWT — it relies entirely on the gateway — so leaving `verify_jwt: true` and authenticating the trigger is the correct secure combination.
  - **Option B: deploy the function with `verify_jwt = false`** (add a `[functions.notify-beta-video-like]` block to `config.toml` and redeploy, or `supabase functions deploy notify-beta-video-like --no-verify-jwt`). This makes the function publicly invokable by anyone who learns the URL, with no auth check in the body — a meaningful attack surface (a spoofed POST could insert arbitrary notification rows via the service-role client). **Not recommended** unless the function body is simultaneously hardened to verify a shared secret in the payload/header. Flagging because it is the quickest unblock and the team may reach for it — do so only with eyes open.

- **SECONDARY GAP — `app_settings.notify_edge_function_url` value is unverified and is the other necessary condition.** The trigger guard (`IF v_edge_function_url IS NOT NULL AND v_edge_function_url <> ''`) silently no-ops when the seeded value is still `''`. I cannot read the value without the DB password. **The human must confirm it** via Studio SQL: `SELECT key, value FROM public.app_settings WHERE key = 'notify_edge_function_url';` — it must equal exactly `https://hxvzjpynhdexhtoccmmh.supabase.co/functions/v1/notify-beta-video-like`. If it is empty, pg_net never fires and `net._http_response` will have **no** rows for this URL — that distinguishes "URL unset" (empty queue) from "URL set but 401" (401 rows present). This is the single fastest diagnostic to run first.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):

- **Run this diagnostic ladder in order (each step tells you which gap is live):**
  1. `SELECT value FROM public.app_settings WHERE key='notify_edge_function_url';` — empty ⇒ fix the URL first (secondary gap). Non-empty ⇒ go to step 2.
  2. `SELECT id, status_code, error_msg, created FROM net._http_response ORDER BY created DESC LIMIT 10;` — a `401` row ⇒ confirms the PRIMARY root cause (auth header). A `200` row but still no Notification row ⇒ the problem is inside the function body (go to step 3). No rows at all ⇒ the trigger guard short-circuited (URL empty) or the trigger didn't fire.
  3. Dashboard → Functions → `notify-beta-video-like` → Logs. **Zero invocations** despite a like ⇒ gateway rejected (401, auth header) — consistent with step 2. Invocations present with an error ⇒ read the logged `console.error` to see which downstream query failed (recipient resolve, notification insert, token fetch).
- **After the fix, verify end-to-end with a non-self like**: the function has a self-like guard (`recipientId === actor_id` → skipped). Test with user B liking user A's video, then `SELECT * FROM public.notifications ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 5;`. A self-like will correctly produce nothing and must not be mistaken for a failure.
- **Confirm pg_net is not only enabled but the `net` schema is reachable by the trigger's `SECURITY DEFINER` search_path.** The trigger sets `search_path = public` but calls `net.http_post` fully-qualified, so this is fine as written — noting it only so it isn't re-investigated.

**Approved** (confirmed not the cause):

- `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` secret is set (digest present, most-recently updated) — gap #2 is closed; rule it out. The Edge Function will authenticate to the DB correctly **once its body actually runs**.
- The Edge Function source (`supabase/functions/notify-beta-video-like/index.ts`) logic is sound: idempotent insert with `23505` treated as success, self-like guard, preference gate (AC-057), active-token fan-out, and it correctly reads `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (not the reserved `SUPABASE_` prefix) per the spec's §Key Implementation Notes. No code defect in the function body itself.
- The idempotency unique constraint and RLS posture in the migration match the MOD-007 spec requirements.

**Architectural note (not a blocker, for the human/PM to weigh):** The current design depends on an operator setting two pieces of out-of-band runtime config (the `app_settings` URL and the deploy-time `verify_jwt` posture) that are invisible to CI and to the migration. This is exactly why the pipeline silently did nothing. Consider, in a follow-up, (a) storing the service-role token for the trigger in Vault and reading it in the trigger, and (b) pinning `verify_jwt` in `config.toml` so the deployed posture is version-controlled rather than whatever was last pushed from someone's shell. Both are hardening items for `engineer-mod-notifications`, not required to unblock today.

### Review — 2026-10-05 (follow-up #2) — blocker (MOD-005 BetaVideoReelViewer: residual previous-video flash persists after the first overlay fix)

**Context**: Advisory follow-up requested by the coordinator. The first fix (this review, entry below dated 2026-10-05) was applied in full by `engineer-mod-beta-video` — all three recommended changes landed (early `setIsVideoLoading(true)` on swipe commit; `useEventListener(player,'statusChange')` clearing the overlay on `readyToPlay` and routing `error` → `urlError`; the black overlay moved *outside* the `dragY` `Animated.View` so it covers the full screen during the slide). Human QA re-tested on device: the overlay is improved but a **brief flash of the previous video still appears** before the next video starts. **Advisory only — no source files edited.** The fix routes through `engineer-mod-beta-video`.

**Verification performed** (read-only, grounding the finding — real API/platform evidence, not restated hypothesis):
- `src/modules/mod-beta-video/components/BetaVideoReelViewer.tsx` read in full at current state (the three prior fixes are present: early overlay set at lines 518 & 535; `statusChange` listener at lines 357–363; overlay as sibling of the `Animated.View` at lines 875–879, style `videoLoadingOverlay` opaque `#000000` at lines 988–997).
- **Platform confirmed iOS-only**: `app.json` → `expo.platforms: ["ios"]`, `expo.ios: true`, `expo.android: false`. This is decisive for ruling the fix (see below).
- expo-video re-inspected at installed `expo-video@57.0.5`:
  - `node_modules/expo-video/build/VideoView.types.d.ts` lines 136–143: `useExoShutter` (the native shutter that "covers the `VideoView` before the first video frame is rendered") is **`@platform android` only, `@default false`**. There is **no equivalent shutter prop on iOS** — the iOS `VideoView` is backed by `AVPlayerLayer`, which holds the *last decoded frame of the previous source* on screen until the new source composites its first frame. Nothing in the expo-video iOS surface hides that frame automatically.
  - `VideoPlayer.types.d.ts` lines 290–295: `readyToPlay` = *"loaded enough data to start playing **or to continue playback**"* — a buffer/data-availability state, explicitly **not** a "first frame of the new source has been composited to the layer" guarantee.

**Confirmed root cause of the *residual* flash — the overlay is cleared on `readyToPlay`, which precedes the first composited frame of the new source; and the `VideoView` (iOS `AVPlayerLayer`) stays composited the whole time holding the previous frame.** Mapping to the three hypotheses in the task brief:

- **Hypothesis C — CONFIRMED as the governing cause on this iOS-only target.** The `VideoView` is never hidden or unmounted across the source swap; the single `AVPlayerLayer` is continuously composited and displays the previous source's last frame. When the overlay is cleared (step below), that layer is what's underneath, still showing the old frame for the brief window until the new frame draws. On iOS there is no `useExoShutter` to mask this (Android-only). The overlay fix reduced the window but cannot eliminate it while the `VideoView` remains visible and the clear signal fires before the new frame.
- **Hypothesis B — CONFIRMED as the trigger for *when* the old frame leaks.** The overlay clears on `statusChange === 'readyToPlay'`. Per the documented semantics above, `readyToPlay` means "enough data buffered," which on iOS fires *before* the first frame of the new source is drawn to the `AVPlayerLayer`. So clearing at `readyToPlay` lifts the black cover during the exact gap where the layer still shows the previous frame — producing the residual flash. This is the specific ordering defect left after fix #1.
- **Hypothesis A — NOT the primary cause here, and largely already mitigated.** React state-batching latency between `setIsVideoLoading(true)` and the paint of the overlay is real in general, but fix #1 already sets the flag on swipe commit (before the 250 ms slide) and the overlay is now a fixed sibling that covers the screen during the slide, so the *entry* side is covered. The residual flash is reported on the *new-video-start* side, which is the B+C window, not the A entry window. A is not driving the symptom QA still sees.

**Why fix #1 helped but did not finish the job**: it closed the *entry* window (old frame sliding out with no cover) by raising the overlay early and fixing its z-order. It did not close the *exit* window — the overlay comes **down** one step too early (`readyToPlay`, Hypothesis B) onto a `VideoView` that is still showing the previous frame (Hypothesis C). Both remaining halves must be addressed for the flash to disappear entirely.

**Recommended approach** (for `engineer-mod-beta-video`; all APIs verified present in `expo-video@57.0.5`; pick the combination, in priority order):

1. **Hide the `VideoView` while loading (addresses Hypothesis C — the guaranteed fix).** While `isVideoLoading` is true, render the `VideoView` with `opacity: 0` (preferred over `display:'none'` so the native view and its player attachment are not torn down/re-created on each swap — opacity keeps it mounted and composited-but-invisible). Restore `opacity: 1` only when loading clears. With the `VideoView` invisible, no previous-frame compositing can bleed through *regardless of async timing*, which is exactly the property the task brief calls out as guaranteed. This is the single most robust change and should be done first. (Alternative to opacity: keep the opaque black overlay above the `VideoView` and only clear it on the correct signal per #2 — but opacity-0 on the `VideoView` itself is the belt that makes the outcome timing-independent.)

2. **Delay clearing the overlay past the first *composited* frame, not just `readyToPlay` (addresses Hypothesis B).** Two acceptable options, Engineer's choice:
   - **(a) Preferred — gate the clear on `onFirstFrameRender` for the frame-paint signal, while keeping `statusChange` only for the `error` → `urlError` path and as a *loading=true* (re-arm) signal.** `onFirstFrameRender` (`VideoView.types.d.ts` lines 131–135) is documented precisely as *"used to hide any cover images that conceal the initial loading of the player"* — it is the paint-time signal, whereas `readyToPlay` is the buffer-time signal. Its documented caveat (may also fire on mid-playback track/quality changes) is harmless here: an extra fire only re-clears an already-clear overlay. Net: clear `isVideoLoading` on `onFirstFrameRender`, not on `readyToPlay`.
   - **(b) If staying on `statusChange`**: after `readyToPlay`, defer the clear by one or two `requestAnimationFrame` ticks (schedule `requestAnimationFrame(() => requestAnimationFrame(() => setIsVideoLoading(false)))`) so the new frame has a compositing pass to land before the cover lifts. This is a timing hack; prefer (a). Either way, keep the `statusChange === 'error'` → `urlError` branch so the overlay yields to the error view (do not let it hang).

3. **Keep the overlay fully opaque and fixed (already done — verify only).** `videoLoadingOverlay` is opaque `#000000` and now sits outside the `dragY` `Animated.View` (lines 875–879, 988–997). No change needed; just confirm #1's opacity change doesn't reintroduce any transparent layer between the overlay and the `VideoView`.

**Expected outcome / trade-off**: combining #1 (invisible `VideoView` during load) with #2a (clear on first composited frame) eliminates the flash deterministically — there is no window in which a visible `VideoView` shows an old frame with the cover down. The accepted cost, noted in the task brief, is a marginally longer *perceived* black-cover duration on each swap (cover stays up until the new frame actually paints rather than until data is merely buffered). That is the correct trade for zero bleed-through.

**Not a PRD-level change.** Still a behavioral-fidelity fix to AC-039/AC-133 (swipe navigation + persistent loading cover), not a new requirement — no PRD/spec edit. Route directly to `engineer-mod-beta-video` with this diagnosis; no PM tag required.

**Shared Convention — now proposing for adoption (recurrence threshold met).** The prior review (below) flagged this as "flagging only, not routing unless it recurs." It has now recurred within the same component across two fix cycles, and the iOS-specific half (no shutter; `AVPlayerLayer` holds the previous frame) generalizes to any reused-`VideoView` source swap. Proposed Shared Convention for Doc-Sync to carry into `production.md`:
> **Reused `expo-video` `VideoView` source swaps (iOS):** when a single persistent `VideoView`/`VideoPlayer` swaps sources via `replaceAsync` rather than remounting, (1) drive the load *cover* down on `onFirstFrameRender` (first composited frame), not on `statusChange: readyToPlay` (buffer-only, fires before the frame paints), and (2) render the `VideoView` at `opacity: 0` while loading so the previously-decoded frame held by `AVPlayerLayer` cannot bleed through — iOS has no `useExoShutter` equivalent (that prop is Android-only). Keep the `statusChange: error` path wired to the error UI so the cover never hangs.

Routing note for the coordinator: this is a **Proposed Shared Convention**, which is not a single hop — invoke `pm` to log it as a [TRIVIAL] PRD/convention change, then `doc-sync` to sync it into `production.md`. It is independent of the source fix above, which routes straight to `engineer-mod-beta-video` now.

### Review — 2026-10-05 — blocker (MOD-005 BetaVideoReelViewer: previous video still visible on swipe before the black loading overlay appears)

**Context**: Advisory review requested by the coordinator. Human QA confirmed on device: when the user swipes to the next/previous video in `BetaVideoReelViewer`, the *previous* video's frame remains visible for a moment before the black loading overlay (`isVideoLoading`) covers it and the new video loads. The intended behavior is for the black overlay + spinner to appear *immediately* on swipe and stay up until the new video's first frame renders. **Advisory only — no source files edited.** The fix routes through `engineer-mod-beta-video`.

**Verification performed** (read-only, grounding the finding):
- `src/modules/mod-beta-video/components/BetaVideoReelViewer.tsx` read in full.
- expo-video API surface inspected directly in `node_modules/expo-video/build/` (installed version confirmed `expo-video@57.0.5`, `expo@~57.0.24` — this is the SDK 57 line, matching the constraint). The proposed fix uses only APIs present in this installed version; verified below.

**Confirmed root cause — a single `VideoView` + single `player` instance is reused across index changes; the overlay clears one render too early because `onFirstFrameRender` is unreliable as the "new video is ready" signal.**

There are two compounding problems, both rooted in the same architecture (one persistent `player` from `useVideoPlayer`, one persistent `VideoView` that is never remounted between videos — only the player's *source* is swapped via `replaceAsync`):

1. **`onFirstFrameRender` is not a reliable per-source "ready" signal, and its docs say so.** In this installed version, `node_modules/expo-video/build/VideoView.types.d.ts` lines 131–135 document `onFirstFrameRender` as *"A callback to call after the mounted `VideoPlayer` has rendered the first frame into the `VideoView`"* with the explicit note: *"This event may also be called during playback when the current video track changes (for example when the player switches video quality)."* Two consequences:
   - It is tied to the **mounted** player/view, which never remounts here — so there is no contractual guarantee it re-fires cleanly for each `replaceAsync` source swap. The current code assumes one fire per source change; the API does not promise that.
   - Because the same `VideoView` keeps rendering the AVPlayer's current output, the *last frame of the previous video* stays on screen through the gap between swipe → `currentIndex` change → signed-URL fetch → `replaceAsync` → new first frame. The black overlay is the only thing meant to hide that gap.

2. **The overlay is painted, but the window where the old frame leaks is the swipe-out animation itself — and during part of that window the overlay is not yet up.** Trace the swipe-up path (lines 490–500): on release, `Animated.timing(dragY → -screenHeight)` runs for **250 ms** showing the *current* (old) video sliding off, and only in that animation's completion callback does `setCurrentIndex(prev + 1)` fire. The `useEffect([currentIndex])` that sets `isVideoLoading = true` (lines 302–304) therefore runs **after** the 250 ms slide completes, not at the moment of release. So: the entire slide-out animation plays the old video with no overlay, `dragY` resets to 0 (snapping the old frame back to center), *then* the overlay appears. The visible artifact — "previous video still showing for a moment" — is exactly this ordering. The overlay z-order itself is fine (it is a sibling rendered after `VideoView` inside the same `absoluteFill` stack, lines 623–659, so it does paint above the video); the bug is **timing**, not z-index. Hypothesis #2 in the task brief (z-index) is ruled out; hypothesis #3 (state-reset timing) is confirmed and is the primary cause; hypothesis #1 (reused `VideoView`/stale frame) is the underlying enabler that makes the timing gap visible.

A secondary contributor: even once `isVideoLoading` is set true, it is cleared by `onFirstFrameRender` (line 630), whose per-source re-fire is not guaranteed (problem 1). If it ever fails to re-fire, the overlay would get stuck *on* instead of *off* — the opposite failure, but the same root unreliability. The fix should not depend on `onFirstFrameRender` as the sole clear signal.

**Recommended approach** (for `engineer-mod-beta-video`; all APIs verified present in `expo-video@57.0.5`):

1. **Set `isVideoLoading = true` at the moment of swipe commit, not after the animation.** Call `setIsVideoLoading(true)` inside the `onPanResponderRelease` branch *before* starting the `Animated.timing` slide-out, for both the swipe-up (line ~493) and swipe-down-to-previous (line ~508) paths — i.e. the moment it is decided that the index will change. Keep the existing `useEffect([currentIndex])` as a backstop. This closes the 250 ms window where the old frame plays with no cover. (Note: this only matters once the overlay actually covers the frame during the slide — see step 2.)

2. **Make the overlay opaque and keep it mounted for the whole transition.** Confirm the `videoLoadingOverlay` style (lines 958–967) is a fully opaque `#000000` fill above the `VideoView` — it is. The remaining gap is that during the slide-out the overlay slides *with* the content (it is inside the same `Animated.View`), so the off-sliding old frame is still momentarily exposed at the edges. Two acceptable options, Engineer's choice:
   - **(a) Simplest, recommended:** drop the 250 ms slide-out content animation on index change and rely on the overlay as the transition — set `isVideoLoading` true on release, change the index immediately (no `Animated.timing` deferral), reset `dragY` to 0 at once. The black overlay + spinner *is* the transition. This removes the window entirely and is the least code.
   - **(b) If the slide-out animation is desired for feel:** render the black loading overlay *outside* the `dragY`-transformed `Animated.View` (as a sibling of it, directly under the `styles.fullscreen` `View` at line 612), so it stays fixed and fully covers the screen while the content slides beneath it. The overlay must sit above the `Animated.View` in paint order.

3. **Do not rely solely on `onFirstFrameRender` to clear the overlay.** Replace/supplement it with the player's `statusChange` event, which *is* a per-source lifecycle signal in this version. Verified present: `node_modules/expo-video/build/VideoPlayerEvents.types.d.ts` line 9 (`statusChange(payload: StatusChangeEventPayload)`), payload `{ status, oldStatus?, error? }` (lines 73–86), and `VideoPlayerStatus = 'idle' | 'loading' | 'readyToPlay' | 'error'` (`VideoPlayer.types.d.ts` line 295). Subscribe via expo's `useEvent`/`useEventListener` (verified exported: `node_modules/expo/build/Expo.d.ts` line 17 `export { useEvent, useEventListener }`) and clear `isVideoLoading` when `status === 'readyToPlay'`; set `isVideoLoading = true` when `status === 'loading'`. This is tied to the actual source-load lifecycle rather than the mounted-view first-frame event, so it re-fires reliably on every `replaceAsync`. Keep `onFirstFrameRender` as an additional (not sole) clear for the very first frame paint if desired. The `sourceLoad` event (`VideoPlayerEvents.types.d.ts` line 62, "finished loading metadata … does not mean enough data buffered to start playback") is explicitly *not* the right clear signal — use `readyToPlay`.

4. **Guard against a stuck overlay.** Because `urlError` already short-circuits the overlay (`isVideoLoading && !urlError`, line 655), ensure the `statusChange === 'error'` case also sets `urlError` so the overlay yields to the error view rather than hanging.

**Not a PRD-level change.** This is a behavioral-fidelity fix to AC-039/AC-133 (swipe navigation + persistent controls), not a new requirement — no PRD/spec edit needed. The loading-overlay behavior is already the documented intent. Route directly to `engineer-mod-beta-video` with this diagnosis; no PM tag required.

**Possible Shared Convention (optional, Engineer/PM discretion — not proposed for adoption here):** "When a single reused `expo-video` `VideoView`/`player` swaps sources via `replaceAsync`, drive load-state UI from the player `statusChange` (`loading`/`readyToPlay`) lifecycle, not from `onFirstFrameRender`, whose docs scope it to the mounted view and allow mid-playback re-fires." Flagging only — I am not routing this as a Proposed Shared Convention unless the same pattern recurs in another module.

---

### Review — 2026-10-04 — change (two data-access conventions from the `fetchBetaVideosByUser` runtime failure: log-before-throw in service functions; manual PostgREST verification for new/modified Supabase join strings)

**Context**: Advisory review requested by the coordinator. `fetchBetaVideosByUser` (`src/modules/mod-beta-video/beta-video-service.ts`) threw the user-facing string "Failed to load beta videos. Please try again." on device. Root cause: the `.select()` nested an FK-hinted embed inside an already-nested embed — `routes!beta_videos_route_id_fkey(name, gyms(name))` sitting alongside `users!beta_videos_user_id_fkey(...)` — and PostgREST rejected that query shape at runtime. The unit test passed because the Supabase client is fully mocked, so the query string is never validated against a real PostgREST API; the mock returns canned data regardless of whether the string is syntactically acceptable to PostgREST. Two structural gaps made this slow to catch and diagnose, and both generalize beyond MOD-005. **Advisory only — no source, spec, or PRD files edited. The source fix already routed through `engineer-mod-beta-video` (Last Action commit `cf77912`); this review records the root cause and proposes two Shared Conventions to route PM → Doc-Sync into `production.md`.**

**Verification performed** (grounding the finding, read-only):
- `beta-video-service.ts` read in full. Confirmed `fetchBetaVideosByUser` now logs the raw error before throwing (line 251: `console.error('[fetchBetaVideosByUser] error:', error)`), as does `uploadBetaVideo` (lines 127, 162, 174). **Drift found**: `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` (lines 54–56) still throws its user-facing string with **no** `console.error` first — the raw PostgREST message is invisible in Metro for that path. This is the exact pattern Convention A exists to prevent, present in the same file, so A is not hypothetical.
- `production.md` §Shared Conventions reviewed. The existing "User-Owned Tables That Participate in PostgREST Joins Reference public.users" convention already governs *which schema an FK targets* so an embed is *possible*; neither it nor any other convention requires that a *new or modified* embed string actually be *exercised against PostgREST* before shipping — the gap Convention B fills. The 2026-10-03 "tests must not mock away a production incompatibility" convention (referenced in the prior review, line 317) is the closest relative; Convention B is its data-access-specific corollary.

**Confirmed root cause**:
A PostgREST `.select()` embed string is effectively an untyped query DSL. TypeScript cannot validate it, and a mocked Supabase client cannot either — the mock short-circuits before any string is parsed. The only thing that validates an embed string is PostgREST itself (live or local). So a join string that is accepted by the compiler and by every unit test can still fail at runtime on device, and when it does, the service swallows the raw PostgREST diagnostic behind a generic user-facing string unless it logged first.

**Fix already applied** (by the owning Engineer, confirmed via read — advisory confirmation only, not my work):
- `fetchBetaVideosByUser` reworked to a PostgREST-valid embed shape and now logs the raw error before throwing. Last Action records `engineer-mod-beta-video` bugfix at commit `cf77912`.

**Concerns** (must address before this is considered resolved):
- **C1 — Both conventions are the right scope but trivial in risk; neither changes user-facing behavior, so both are `production.md`-only (no PRD/AC change).** They are testing/observability/data-access process rules, correctly tagged [TRIVIAL] for PM to log and Doc-Sync to sync. If either were allowed to drift into "change what the query returns," that would be a PRD-level change — it is not; these only govern *how* queries are written and verified.
- **C2 — Convention A has an existing in-file violation to sweep.** `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` throws without logging (lines 54–56). Once A is codified, the owning Engineer should bring that function (and a quick grep of every other `throw new Error(...)` inside a Supabase service file across all modules) into line. This is a follow-up for `engineer-mod-*`, not part of this advisory.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):
- **R1 — Prefer the flat/separate-query shape over deeply-nested FK-hinted embeds where practical.** A single level of embed (`users!fk(...)`) is well-trodden here; a second level nested *inside* it (`routes!fk(name, gyms(name))`) is the shape that broke. Where a listing needs route+gym labels, consider either composing them server-side in a `SECURITY INVOKER` RPC (as MOD-006's feed already does) or doing a second scoped fetch, rather than stacking embeds. Advisory only — the current fix is acceptable; this is guidance for the next such query.
- **R2 — Make Convention B's verification leave a trace.** A join string verified only in someone's head is indistinguishable from one never verified. Suggest the Engineer paste the equivalent SQL (or the raw PostgREST URL they hit) into the module `status.md` Engineering Progress entry for any AC that adds/modifies an embed, so QA and Tech Lead can see the check actually happened. Routes through the owning Engineer's handoff note — advisory.
- **R3 — Longer term, consider generating Supabase types and/or a lightweight integration test tier that runs a handful of real `.select()` strings against a local `supabase start` instance in CI.** This is the only thing that structurally closes the "mock can't validate the DSL" gap; a prose rule (B) depends on discipline. Flagging as a Phase-2 build-infra investment, not a Phase-1 ask. (Routes through build config / owning Engineer — advisory.)

**Approved** (what is solid and should be kept):
- The applied fix and its log-before-throw at line 251 are correct and already embody Convention A.
- `uploadBetaVideo`'s existing log-before-throw at every throw site (storage error, insert error, outer catch) is the exact pattern Convention A generalizes — it is a good in-repo reference.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for PM → Doc-Sync to carry into production.md §Shared Conventions, both [TRIVIAL], `production.md`-only, no PRD revision bump):
- **Convention A — Log the raw Supabase error before throwing a user-facing string.** Any Supabase data-access function (service files, RPCs-wrappers, storage helpers) that catches a Supabase `error` and throws a user-facing `Error` MUST first call `console.error('[functionName] error:', error)` so the raw Supabase/PostgREST diagnostic is visible in device/Metro logs. The user-facing string stays generic; the raw error goes to the console, never to the user. Canonical form:
  ```ts
  if (error) {
    console.error('[fetchBetaVideosByUser] error:', error);
    throw new Error('Failed to load beta videos. Please try again.');
  }
  ```
  Applies to every module's service files. Reference implementations: `mod-beta-video/beta-video-service.ts` `uploadBetaVideo` (storage-error, insert-error, and outer-catch sites) and `fetchBetaVideosByUser`. Known existing gap to sweep when this lands: `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` in the same file throws without logging first (follow-up for the owning Engineer, plus a grep for other `throw new Error` sites inside Supabase service functions project-wide).
- **Convention B — Manually verify every new or modified PostgREST embed/join string against a real PostgREST instance before shipping.** Whenever an engineer adds or modifies an embedded join in a `.select()` string — especially a nested embed, and most especially an FK-hinted embed nested inside another embed (e.g. `routes!beta_videos_route_id_fkey(name, gyms(name))`) — they MUST exercise the string against the live or local Supabase instance before marking the AC done: either run the equivalent query in the Studio SQL Editor, or hit the raw PostgREST endpoint directly. A `.select()` embed string is an untyped query DSL that neither TypeScript nor a mocked Supabase client can validate — mocked unit tests short-circuit before the string is ever parsed by PostgREST and therefore CANNOT substitute for this step. This is the data-access corollary of the existing "tests must not mock away a production incompatibility" convention. Record this in `production.md`'s Supabase/data-access conventions area, adjacent to the "User-Owned Tables … Reference public.users" convention. Recommended (advisory, R2): note the verification — the equivalent SQL or the PostgREST URL hit — in the module `status.md` Engineering Progress entry so the check is auditable.

### Review — 2026-10-04 — correction (the 2026-10-04 "Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern" Shared Convention exemption is wrong: `RouteDetailScreen` + `UserProfileScreen` crashed the same way — the WHOLE app has no NavigationContainer)

**Context**: Advisory review requested by the coordinator after two more screens — `RouteDetailScreen` (`mod-route-catalog`) and `UserProfileScreen` (`mod-social-feed`) — crashed on device with "Couldn't find a navigation object. Is your component inside NavigationContainer?" after `useFocusEffect` was added to them. These are the two screens the 2026-10-04 Shared Convention (now in `production.md` §Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern, rule 2 and the "Exempt" line) explicitly named as **exempt** — on the mistaken assumption that, because their ancestor components are named `RouteNavigator` / `ProfileNavigator`, they sit inside a react-navigation `NavigationContainer`. They do not. The fixes are already applied (see below); this review records the root cause and proposes a corrected convention to replace the flawed one. **Advisory only — no source, spec, or PRD files edited. The source fixes + test-mock removals already routed through `engineer-mod-route-catalog` and `engineer-mod-social-feed`; the corrected convention routes PM → Doc-Sync into `production.md`, replacing the current §Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern text.**

**Verification performed** (grounding the finding, read-only):
- `grep -rn "NavigationContainer" src/` → zero real usages; every match is a comment documenting its *absence*. There is no `NavigationContainer` mounted anywhere in the app.
- `grep -rn "@react-navigation" src/` (source and tests) → zero imports. Nothing in `src/` imports any react-navigation symbol.
- `grep -rn "useFocusEffect|useNavigation|useRoute" src/` (non-test) → zero live calls; all remaining matches are comments documenting the intentional non-use.
- Composition root (`App.tsx`) confirmed: `SafeAreaProvider → ThemeProvider → AuthNavigator → AppShell`. All of `GymNavigator`, `RouteNavigator`, `ProfileNavigator`, `HomeNavigator` are custom `useState` state machines, **not** react-navigation navigators — consistent with the state-machine-per-navigator convention already in `production.md` (§, `AppShell.tsx` note).
- **Latent trap**: `@react-navigation/native@^7.4.1` and `@react-navigation/native-stack@^7.19.2` are still listed in `package.json` (lines 8–9) even though nothing imports them. The packages are installed and importable, so a future engineer can reintroduce the exact crash by autocompleting `useFocusEffect` — the type system will not stop them, and (until now) neither did CI. This is why R2 below (a CI-detectable guard) matters more than a prose rule.

**Confirmed root cause**:
The entire app intentionally uses custom React state-machine navigators and mounts **no `NavigationContainer` at any level**. Therefore **no screen anywhere** — not just keep-alive tab screens — may call `useFocusEffect`, `useNavigation`, `useRoute`, or any other `@react-navigation/native` hook that requires a `NavigationContainer` ancestor; every such call throws at runtime. The 2026-10-04 convention scoped the rule too narrowly (to keep-alive screens only) and then compounded the error with an explicit "exempt" clause naming the two screens that have now crashed. The screen's mount style (keep-alive vs conditionally-mounted) determines the *correct substitute* (see below), but it does **not** determine *whether* the ban applies — the ban is app-wide because the `NavigationContainer` is absent app-wide.

**Fixes already applied** (by the owning Engineers, confirmed via scan — advisory confirmation only, not my work):
- `RouteDetailScreen` (`mod-route-catalog`): `useFocusEffect` removed, replaced with plain `useEffect`; 24 tests passing. `@react-navigation/native` mock removed from its test.
- `UserProfileScreen` (`mod-social-feed`): `useFocusEffect` removed, replaced with plain `useEffect`; 15 tests passing. `@react-navigation/native` mock removed from its test.

**Concerns** (must address before this is considered resolved):
- **C1 — The current `production.md` Shared Convention is actively misleading and must be replaced, not just amended.** Rule 2 ("Screens rendered inside a stack/tab navigator (e.g. `RouteDetailScreen`, `UserProfileScreen`) are exempt and may use `useFocusEffect` normally.") and the trailing "Exempt: any stack/tab navigator screen." line are both false for this app — there are no react-navigation stack/tab navigators here, only state machines. Left in place, this convention will cause the next engineer to repeat the crash. Owner of the doc change: PM → Doc-Sync.
- **C2 — The exemption's premise (ancestor named `…Navigator` ⇒ inside a `NavigationContainer`) is the trap.** The corrected convention must state explicitly that this app's `*Navigator` components are state machines, not react-navigation navigators, so a "navigator" ancestor confers no navigation context.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):
- **R1** — Replace the whole §Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern with the app-wide convention in Proposed Shared Conventions below. Keep the keep-alive/conditionally-mounted distinction only as guidance on *which substitute* to use, not as an exemption from the ban.
- **R2** — Make this CI-detectable, since the packages remain installed and the type checker will not flag reintroduction. Cheapest effective guard: an ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule (or a `no-restricted-syntax` rule on the hook identifiers) that errors on any `@react-navigation/native` import or any `useFocusEffect`/`useNavigation`/`useRoute` call under `src/`. A lint rule is preferable to a grep-in-CI because it runs in-editor and names the offending line; a one-line `grep -rn "@react-navigation" src/ && exit 1` CI step is an acceptable fallback if adding the lint rule is deferred. (Implementation routes through the owning Engineer / build config — this is advisory only.)
- **R3** — Consider removing `@react-navigation/native` and `@react-navigation/native-stack` from `package.json` entirely, since nothing imports them. Removing the packages makes the crash class impossible by construction (the import would not resolve) and is stronger than any lint rule. Only defer this if a Phase 2 plan intends to adopt react-navigation — in which case the eventual introduction of a `NavigationContainer` is itself an architecture change warranting its own Tech Lead review. (Advisory; dependency change routes through the owning Engineer.)

**Approved** (what is solid and should be kept):
- The fixes applied to both screens are correct: a conditionally-mounted screen (mounted on navigation into it, unmounted on exit) should load data in a plain `useEffect([])`, which already fires on every mount and is the exact equivalent of "on focus" for a screen that is remounted each time it is shown.
- The keep-alive `isActive`-prop + `useEffect([isActive])` pattern remains correct for the keep-alive tab screens (`ProfileScreen`), which are *not* remounted and therefore cannot rely on mount to detect focus.
- Removing the `@react-navigation/native` mocks from both test files is correct and consistent with the 2026-10-03 "tests must not mock away a production incompatibility" convention.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for PM → Doc-Sync to carry into production.md — these REPLACE the existing §Keep-Alive Screen Focus Pattern text; the §Keep-Alive Screen Test Pattern below it stays, with the clarification that it applies to ALL screens, not only keep-alive ones):
- **No screen in this app may use `useFocusEffect` or any `@react-navigation/native` navigation-context hook — the app has no `NavigationContainer` anywhere.** This app intentionally uses custom React `useState` state-machine navigators (`AuthNavigator`, `GymNavigator`, `RouteNavigator`, `ProfileNavigator`, `HomeNavigator`, `AppShell`) and mounts **no `NavigationContainer` at any level** (composition root: `SafeAreaProvider → ThemeProvider → AuthNavigator → AppShell`). A component whose ancestor is named `*Navigator` is therefore **NOT** inside a react-navigation navigator — those are state machines, not react-navigation. Any `@react-navigation/native` hook that requires navigation context (`useFocusEffect`, `useNavigation`, `useRoute`, and the like) will throw "Couldn't find a navigation object" at runtime on **every** screen. There is **no exemption** for "stack/tab navigator screens" — that exemption was wrong and caused device crashes in `RouteDetailScreen` and `UserProfileScreen`. Rules for focus-triggered behavior (load/refetch when a screen is shown): (1) **Conditionally-mounted screens** (mounted when navigated into, unmounted on exit — e.g. `RouteDetailScreen`, `UserProfileScreen`) use a plain `useEffect([])` for load-on-show; mount already coincides with focus. (2) **Keep-alive screens** (AppShell `display:none`/`display:flex` mount, never unmounted — e.g. `ProfileScreen`) receive an `isActive: boolean` prop and use `useEffect([isActive])`, because they are not remounted and cannot use mount to detect focus. (3) Every screen carries a top-of-file comment noting the app has no `NavigationContainer` ancestor and that `@react-navigation/native` navigation-context hooks must not be used (state which substitute applies — `useEffect([])` for conditionally-mounted, `isActive` for keep-alive). If react-navigation navigation is ever genuinely needed, that requires introducing a `NavigationContainer`, which is an app-architecture change requiring its own Tech Lead review — not an ad-hoc hook call.
- **Enforce the above in CI, not just prose** (R2). `@react-navigation/native`/`native-stack` are still installed in `package.json` even though nothing imports them, so a future engineer can reintroduce the crash and neither the type checker nor existing tests will catch it. Add an ESLint `no-restricted-imports` (or `no-restricted-syntax`) rule that errors on any `@react-navigation/native` import or any `useFocusEffect`/`useNavigation`/`useRoute` call under `src/`; a `grep -rn "@react-navigation" src/` CI guard is an acceptable fallback. Stronger still, and preferred if no Phase 2 react-navigation adoption is planned, is removing both packages from `package.json` outright (R3) so the import cannot resolve at all.

### Review — 2026-10-04 — blocker (ProfileScreen crashed on device: `useFocusEffect` used inside AppShell's keep-alive mount, which has no NavigationContainer ancestor; invisible in CI because the test mocked `@react-navigation/native`)

**Context**: Advisory review requested by the coordinator after a device crash in `ProfileScreen`. Root cause (supplied and confirmed): `ProfileScreen` called `useFocusEffect` from `@react-navigation/native`, which requires a `NavigationContainer` in the tree. But `ProfileScreen` is rendered via `AppShell`'s keep-alive mount strategy (`display:none` / `display:flex` to preserve tab state across switches), which has **no `NavigationContainer` ancestor**. The hook threw at runtime on device. It was invisible in CI because `ProfileScreen.test.tsx` mocked `@react-navigation/native` so `useFocusEffect` degraded to a plain `useEffect` — the test environment did not match the production mount context. This is the same class of green-but-broken defect flagged on 2026-10-03 (test fixtures diverging from production wiring). **Advisory only — no source, spec, or PRD files edited. The fix and the test-mock removal route through `engineer-mod-auth-profile`; these two conventions route PM → Doc-Sync into `production.md` Shared Conventions.**

**Concerns** (must address before this is considered resolved):
- **C1 — Keep-alive screens must not call navigation-context hooks.** `AppShell`'s keep-alive screens have no `NavigationContainer` ancestor, so any `@react-navigation/native` hook requiring navigation context (`useFocusEffect`, `useNavigation`, `useRoute`) throws at runtime. Affected screen (current): `ProfileScreen`. Owner: `engineer-mod-auth-profile`.
- **C2 — The test masked the production incompatibility.** `ProfileScreen.test.tsx` mocked `@react-navigation/native`, making a hook pass in test that crashes in production. The mock must be removed so the mismatch surfaces in CI (it now is removed per the fix). Any mock that papers over a test-vs-production mount incompatibility is hiding a real bug.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):
- **R1** — Keep-alive screens receive an `isActive: boolean` prop and use `useEffect([isActive])` for focus-triggered behavior instead of `useFocusEffect`.
- **R2** — Add a top-of-file comment to each keep-alive screen stating it has no `NavigationContainer` ancestor and must not use `@react-navigation/native` navigation-context hooks (use `isActive` instead).
- **R3** — Tests for keep-alive screens exercise focus behavior by flipping the `isActive` prop (false → true), never by mocking navigation or simulating navigation events.

**Approved** (what is solid and should be kept):
- The `isActive`-prop + `useEffect` pattern is the correct substitute for `useFocusEffect` on keep-alive screens and should be the standard going forward.
- Removing the `@react-navigation/native` mock from keep-alive screen tests is correct — it makes any future reintroduction of a navigation-context hook fail CI immediately rather than only on device.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for PM → Doc-Sync to carry into production.md):
- **Keep-alive screens must not use `useFocusEffect` (or any navigation-context hook).** `AppShell` uses a keep-alive mount strategy (`display:none` / `display:flex`) for the main tab screens to preserve state across tab switches; these screens are **not** inside a `NavigationContainer`. Any `@react-navigation/native` hook that requires navigation context (`useFocusEffect`, `useNavigation`, `useRoute`) will throw at runtime on them. Rules: (1) Any screen rendered via AppShell's keep-alive pattern must receive an `isActive: boolean` prop and use `useEffect([isActive])` for focus-triggered behavior — never `useFocusEffect`. (2) Screens rendered inside a stack/tab navigator (e.g. `RouteDetailScreen`, `UserProfileScreen`) are exempt and may use `useFocusEffect` normally. (3) Each keep-alive screen must carry a top-of-file comment: "This screen is rendered via AppShell's keep-alive pattern and has no NavigationContainer ancestor. Do not use useFocusEffect or any other @react-navigation/native hook that requires navigation context. Use the isActive prop instead." Affected keep-alive screens (current): `ProfileScreen`. Correct alternative: `isActive` prop + `useEffect([isActive])`. Exempt: any stack/tab navigator screen.
- **Tests must not mock away a test-vs-production mount incompatibility.** Tests for keep-alive screens must **not** mock `useFocusEffect` or `@react-navigation/native` — those screens render in production without a `NavigationContainer`, and the test must reflect that reality so any future navigation-context hook crashes CI immediately. To assert focus-triggered behavior on a keep-alive screen, flip the `isActive` prop (false → true) directly rather than simulating navigation events. Generally: if mocking a dependency makes a test pass that would fail in production, the mock is hiding a real bug — fix the incompatibility, do not mock over it.

---

### Review — 2026-10-03 — blocker (two human-QA device failures after a green pipeline: AC-166 replace-dialog never shows; AC-031 HDR video still overexposed — plus the systemic reason 88 passing tests missed both)

**Context**: Advisory investigation requested by the coordinator after two bugs surfaced in human QA that the full PM → Doc-Sync → Engineer → QA pipeline (88 tests, 0 failures) had green-lit. I read `BetaVideoUploader.tsx`, `beta-video-service.ts`, `BetaVideoSection.tsx`, `RouteDetailScreen.tsx`, `BetaVideoReelViewer.tsx` (imports + player config), the `BetaVideoUploader.test.tsx` suite, and confirmed `expo-video@57.0.5` with no HDR/EDR/tone-map config anywhere in `src`. **Advisory only — no source, spec, or PRD files edited. Fixes below route through the owning Engineer per the coordinator; architectural options for Failure 2 route PM → Tech Lead re-review if they change behavior.**

---

#### FAILURE 1 — Replace-confirmation dialog never shows. ROOT CAUSE FOUND: the dialog was built in the wrong (dead) component.

This is **not** any of the four hypotheses in the brief (it is not a silent `checkExistingBetaVideo` failure, not a broken `Alert` Promise, not a missing `routeId`, not a stale `useCallback` dep). AC-166 was correctly implemented in `BetaVideoUploader.tsx` — the `Promise<boolean>` + `Alert.alert` pattern there is sound and the tests that exercise it genuinely pass. **The problem is that `BetaVideoUploader` is not the component that runs on the route detail screen.** There are TWO upload code paths in MOD-005:

1. `BetaVideoUploader.tsx` — has the AC-166 dialog, the mount-time `checkExistingBetaVideo`, the AC-169 size gate, `quality: 1`, no `preferredAssetRepresentationMode`. **This is where every AC-166 test points.**
2. `BetaVideoSection.tsx` → `handleLaunchUpload` (lines 216–285) — a **duplicate, parallel upload implementation** with its own permission check, duration check, format check, and `uploadBetaVideo` call. It has **no `checkExistingBetaVideo`, no AC-166 confirmation dialog, no AC-169 size gate**, still uses `quality: 0.7`, and still sets a `'thumbnail'` progress phase (removed in Rev 24).

`BetaVideoSection` renders `BetaVideoUploader` **only** in "legacy mode" — when `onRegisterUploadLauncher` is NOT provided (line 363: `onRegisterUploadLauncher ? null : <BetaVideoUploader …>`). But `RouteDetailScreen.tsx` **always** passes `onRegisterUploadLauncher={handleRegisterUploadLauncher}` (line 445, AC-130 mode). The AC-130 "Upload Beta Video" action-row button calls `uploadLauncherRef.current?.()` (line 232), which is wired to `BetaVideoSection.handleLaunchUpload` — path #2. **In the shipped app, `BetaVideoUploader` is dead code and every line of AC-166 never executes.** The picker opens immediately because path #2 has no dialog. That exactly matches the observed "picker opens immediately (or button does nothing)."

**Why 88 tests missed it**: the AC-166 tests render `BetaVideoUploader` in isolation (`render(<BetaVideoUploader {...DEFAULT_PROPS} />)`) — the legacy-mode component — and never render `BetaVideoSection` with `onRegisterUploadLauncher` set, which is the only configuration the app actually uses. The test exercised a component the user never reaches. This is a **test-fixture/production-wiring divergence**, the most dangerous class of green-but-broken: the code under test is correct and the test is correct, but neither is on the live path.

**Secondary finding (same root cause, latent)**: because path #2 also lacks the AC-169 size gate, the 45 MB upfront rejection (Rev 24) is *also* not enforced on the live path — a >45 MB file reaches `uploadBetaVideo` and fails as a raw storage 413 instead of the friendly bilingual error. QA passed AC-169 for the same reason it passed AC-166: the test hit `BetaVideoUploader`, not the live path. This should be verified and fixed in the same pass.

---

#### FAILURE 2 — HDR video still overexposed. ROOT CAUSE: this is a PLAYER problem, not a picker or upload problem. The Rev 26 fix targeted the wrong layer.

Of the three hypotheses in the brief, **#3 is correct** and #1 is also partially true. Concretely:

- **The picker assumption behind Rev 26 is unreliable (hypothesis #1).** `PHPickerViewController` in `Automatic` representation mode does **not** guarantee an HEVC/HLG → H.264 SDR transcode. `Automatic` lets the system choose a representation "best suited for the app," which for a video asset already in a widely-playable container (HEVC in a `.mov` is fully AVFoundation-playable) frequently means **returning the original HEVC/HLG file untouched**. Transcoding is neither promised by the API nor consistent across iOS versions / source asset types. So removing `preferredAssetRepresentationMode: Current` did not reliably change what the picker returns. The prior `mdls`/`ffprobe` evidence (BT.2020 HLG stored in the bucket) is consistent with the picker still handing back the original HLG file.
- **Even a successful H.264 transcode would not fix it (hypothesis #2).** H.264 can carry BT.2020 / HLG transfer characteristics in its VUI color metadata. A container/codec change is not a color-space change; the overexposure is driven by the transfer function, not the codec.
- **The actual cause is the player (hypothesis #3).** `BetaVideoReelViewer.tsx` renders `expo-video`'s `VideoView` (line 603) via `useVideoPlayer` (line 328) with **no dynamic-range / EDR configuration** — I grepped the entire `src` tree and there is zero HDR/EDR/tone-map/BT.2020/HLG handling. Under the hood AVPlayer renders HLG content correctly only when the view is on an EDR-capable rendering path; without that, HLG's upper range is mapped as if it were SDR and the midtones blow out — exactly "overexposed." This is independent of the codec/container and is why no picker or upload change can fix it.

**Conclusion for Failure 2**: overexposure is a **playback-layer rendering problem**. The Rev 26 picker change addressed a layer that was never the cause, which is why the device still fails. Do not patch the picker further.

---

#### HOLISTIC PROPOSAL

**Fix for Failure 1 (clear, low-risk, Engineer-owned):** Eliminate the duplicate upload path. The correct architecture is a single upload implementation. Recommended: delete `BetaVideoSection.handleLaunchUpload`'s inline logic and have the AC-130 launcher drive the *same* code path as `BetaVideoUploader` — i.e. lift the full `BetaVideoUploader.handleSelectVideo` flow (mount-time `checkExistingBetaVideo` → cached `hasExistingVideo` → AC-166 dialog → picker → AC-030/AC-035/AC-169 gates → `uploadBetaVideo`) into a single shared hook or function that both the AC-130 launcher and any legacy button call. There must be exactly one place where the picker is launched. Until that consolidation lands, the minimum fix is to port the AC-166 dialog, the `checkExistingBetaVideo` mount check, and the AC-169 size gate into `handleLaunchUpload` — but the duplication is the actual defect and will keep re-biting; collapse the two paths. This is a MOD-005 change; `RouteDetailScreen` wiring does not change. Route to `engineer-mod-beta-video`.

**Fix for Failure 2 (architectural — needs a real decision, likely PM → Tech Lead re-review):** Because the cause is the player, the options are:
1. **Fix the player's dynamic range (correct long-term fix).** Configure the `expo-video` `VideoView` for the device's dynamic range so HLG renders tone-mapped rather than blown out. Verify whether `expo-video@57` exposes an EDR / preferred-dynamic-range prop; if it does, set it and validate on a physical HDR-capture device. If it does not, this needs either an upstream capability or a small native view config — a larger lift that Tech Lead must re-scope.
2. **Transcode HDR→SDR for real, server-side (the already-planned Phase 2 path).** The PRD's Phase 2 AWS MediaConvert pipeline does a proper HDR→SDR tone-map. This is the architecturally clean fix and it is already on the roadmap — the honest answer may be that correct HDR playback is a Phase 2 capability and Phase 1 should *detect and warn* rather than claim a transcode it cannot reliably perform.
3. **Do a reliable on-device SDR transcode at upload (only if Phase 1 must ship correct playback).** Not via the picker's `Automatic` mode (unreliable, as shown) but via an explicit AVFoundation export with a forced SDR color space. This re-introduces a native transcode step (the `react-native-compressor`-class dependency removed in Rev 24) and should not be taken without a deliberate PM decision.
   **My recommendation**: stop treating this as a picker bug. Either (1) if `expo-video` exposes a dynamic-range prop (cheap, test on device first) or (2) accept it as a known Phase 1 limitation and correct AC-031 to say so honestly, deferring the real fix to the Phase 2 MediaConvert tone-map. Any of these changes AC-031's claimed behavior, so it is a PRD change: **route PM → Tech Lead re-review**, do not let it be patched silently.

**Fix for the systemic testing gap (the actual ask):** The pipeline is green-but-broken because tests validate components in isolation that the app never mounts, and because device-only behaviors (iOS picker output, AVPlayer HDR rendering, `Alert` event-loop timing, real upload) are mocked away. Recommendations:
1. **Integration test at the real entry point, not the leaf component.** AC-166/AC-169 must be tested by rendering `BetaVideoSection` *with* `onRegisterUploadLauncher` set (the production configuration) and firing the registered launcher — not by rendering `BetaVideoUploader` directly. A test that renders a component the app never reaches provides false assurance. This single rule would have caught Failure 1.
2. **Ban duplicated feature logic across components.** Two parallel upload implementations is the structural defect behind Failure 1. One behavior, one implementation (shared hook/function). This belongs as a Shared Convention (proposed below).
3. **Mandatory device-verification checklist before QA handoff for anything the harness cannot exercise.** Jest mocks `expo-image-picker`, `Alert.alert` (synchronous), `expo-file-system`, and cannot render `expo-video` playback at all. Any AC touching the picker, native file size, `Alert` dialogs, upload, or video rendering must carry an Engineer "verified on physical device" note in the module `status.md` before QA. A green Jest run is necessary but explicitly **not sufficient** evidence for these ACs — this mirrors the existing Native Build Gate convention and should be stated with the same force.
4. **Device-behavior assumptions must be verified, not assumed, before they become an AC.** Rev 26 encoded an unverified belief ("`Automatic` mode transcodes to SDR") directly into an acceptance criterion, and the brief itself notes there was never a post-change device test. Claims about third-party/OS runtime behavior (picker output, player rendering) need a captured device result before they are written as settled behavior.

**Concerns** (must address before this is considered resolved):
- **C1 — Duplicate upload path (Failure 1 root cause).** `BetaVideoSection.handleLaunchUpload` shadows `BetaVideoUploader` on the live AC-130 path; AC-166 and AC-169 do not execute in the shipped app. Must be consolidated to one path. Owner: `engineer-mod-beta-video`.
- **C2 — AC-031 encodes an unreliable assumption.** The picker `Automatic`-mode "transcodes to SDR" premise is not guaranteed by iOS; the real overexposure cause is the `expo-video` player having no dynamic-range config. AC-031 as written cannot deliver its stated outcome. Needs a PM decision among the three options above, then Tech Lead re-review. Do not patch the picker again.
- **C3 — Test fixtures diverge from production wiring.** The suite that "covers" AC-166/AC-169 renders a component configuration the app never uses. Until MOD-005's upload is integration-tested through `BetaVideoSection` + `onRegisterUploadLauncher`, those ACs are effectively untested on the live path.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):
- **R1** — Collapse the two upload implementations into one shared hook/function; there must be exactly one picker-launch site in MOD-005.
- **R2** — Add an integration test rendering `BetaVideoSection` in AC-130 mode and driving the registered launcher, asserting the AC-166 dialog and AC-169 gate fire on that path.
- **R3** — If `expo-video@57` exposes a dynamic-range/EDR prop, set it and device-test before pursuing any transcode option; this may be the cheapest correct fix for Failure 2.
- **R4** — For Failure 2, if no cheap player fix exists, correct AC-031 to state the Phase 1 limitation honestly and defer correct HDR playback to the Phase 2 MediaConvert tone-map (already on the roadmap).

**Approved** (what is solid and should be kept):
- The AC-166 dialog implementation *inside* `BetaVideoUploader` (the `Promise<boolean>` + `Alert.alert` three-arg pattern, mount-time `checkExistingBetaVideo` with cached `hasExistingVideo`, `cancelable: false`) is correct — it simply is not on the live path. Reuse it as the single shared implementation.
- The AC-169 fail-closed size gate in `BetaVideoUploader` is correct and should be the one that survives consolidation.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for PM → Doc-Sync to carry into production.md):
- **Single-entry-point for cross-module features.** A module that exposes a feature through a registered launcher/callback (the AC-130 pattern) must route that launcher through the *same* implementation as its standalone component — never a duplicated parallel code path. One behavior, one implementation. Duplicated feature logic across two components is a defect, not a style choice.
- **Integration tests must mount the production wiring.** Where a component is only ever rendered by a parent in a specific configuration (e.g. `BetaVideoSection` always passes `onRegisterUploadLauncher`), the behavior must be tested through that parent in that configuration. A passing test against a configuration the app never mounts is not evidence the feature works.
- **Device-verification gate for un-mockable behavior.** ACs that depend on native picker output, `Alert`/native-dialog event-loop timing, real file I/O, network upload, or video (`expo-video`) rendering require a documented physical-device verification note in the module `status.md` before QA handoff. A green Jest run is necessary but not sufficient evidence for these ACs — same force as the existing Native Build Gate.

### Review — 2026-10-03 — change (Rev 26 prose reconciliation: AC-031 switched the picker from `preferredAssetRepresentationMode: Current` to iOS `Automatic` transcode — surrounding prose in US-004, §5, §9, §10 still describes the old no-transcode model)

**Context**: Advisory review requested by PM. PRD Rev 26 revised AC-031 (line 597) so the picker no longer sets `preferredAssetRepresentationMode: Current` — it uses the iOS default `Automatic` representation mode, under which iOS transcodes HEVC/HLG source footage to H.264 SDR before returning the URI, and the app uploads that iOS-transcoded file as-is (still no additional client-side compression, still gated at 45 MB). AC-031 itself is internally consistent and correct. The problem is that AC-031's prose neighbors were written for the earlier "upload the original native HEVC file untouched" model and now contradict the transcode model. I read §2 Goals (line 27), US-004 known-trade-off note (line 100), §4 tech-stack video-hosting row (line 297), §5 architecture client bullet + data-flow loop 2 (lines 310, 321), MOD-005 purpose (line 369), AC-031/AC-035/AC-169 (lines 597, 605, 655), §9 Data Model BetaVideo upload-size note (lines 880–886), and §10 NFR Video bullet (line 968). **These are prose-only corrections with no behavioral change** — AC-031 is the single source of truth for behavior and it is already correct. No source, migration, spec, or PRD files changed here. **Advisory only — PM decides whether to apply, and whether to batch as [TRIVIAL]. Any PRD edit routes PM → Doc-Sync; I do not edit `prd.md`.**

**Recommended tagging**: **[TRIVIAL]** — batch all four corrections below in a single PM pass. Each is a factual/consistency correction to prose that describes behavior AC-031 already fully specifies; none changes an acceptance criterion, a data-model field, a dependency, or any user-facing behavior. Doc-Sync will then re-sync MOD-005's spec so the module reference prose matches. (If PM prefers, the §10 figure correction could stand alone, but there is no reason to split them — they are one logical reconciliation.)

**Concerns** (must address before Rev 26 is considered internally consistent):

- **C1 — US-004 trade-off figure is wrong under the transcode model (line 100).** The note cites "~16 Mbps" raw HEVC hitting 45 MB "at roughly 22 seconds." Under Rev 26 the picker returns an iOS `Automatic`-mode H.264 SDR export, whose bitrate is materially lower than raw HEVC (iOS H.264 1080p exports land ~8–10 Mbps, not ~16 Mbps). I confirmed the arithmetic: 45 MB = 45 × 8 = 360 Mbit; at 8 Mbps that is 45 s, at 10 Mbps 36 s. The practical limit is now **~36–45 s**, not ~22 s — the transcode actually *improves* the user-facing limit (closer to the 60 s cap). Leaving "~22 s" understates the product and contradicts AC-031. **Note**: the identical ~22 s figure is repeated in the §10 NFR bullet (line 968, "at full iPhone 1080p HEVC bitrate the 45 MB gate is reached at ~22 s") and must be corrected in both places (see C4).

- **C2 — §5 client bullet says "uploads the original native video file as-is" (line 310).** Under Rev 26 the uploaded file is the iOS-transcoded H.264 SDR file the picker returns, not the original native capture. "As-is" is still true *relative to the picker's output* (no further client re-encoding), but "original native video file" is now wrong — the original HEVC/HDR capture is not what gets uploaded. The data-flow loop 2 (line 321) repeats the same "original native video file as-is" phrasing and needs the same fix.

- **C3 — §9 Data Model and §10 NFR assert "no re-encoding" / "stored as-is" / "without conversion" (lines 880–886, 968).** These are now inconsistent with the transcode model. **Precision note for PM**: the §9 BetaVideo upload-size note (lines 880–886) does *not* actually contain the strings "HEVC stored as-is" or "no re-encoding" — it only says "Phase 1 performs NO client-side compression," which remains **true** under Rev 26 (iOS does the transcode, not the app). So §9's upload-size note needs only a one-clause clarification, not a rewrite. The "no re-encoding," "stored as-is," and "iPhone `.mov`/HEVC footage uploads without conversion" language actually lives in the **§10 NFR Video bullet (line 968)** and in **§5 (lines 310, 321)** — those are the strings that are flatly wrong and must change. (AC-035 at line 605, "Both containers are stored as-is (no server-side transcoding)," remains correct — it is about *server*-side, and the Rev 26 transcode is on-device, pre-upload. Do not touch AC-035.)

- **C4 — The ~22 s figure and the "no re-encoding" framing both recur in the §10 NFR bullet (line 968).** This bullet is the densest concentration of the inconsistency: it states "Phase 1 performs no client-side compression or re-encoding" (the "no re-encoding" half is now wrong — iOS re-encodes), "iPhone-camera `.mov`/HEVC footage uploads without conversion" (now wrong — it is converted to H.264 SDR by the picker), and "at full iPhone 1080p HEVC bitrate the 45 MB gate is reached at ~22 s" (now ~36–45 s at H.264 SDR). All three clauses in this one bullet need correcting together.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements, not blocking):

- **R1** — When PM applies these, add a short parenthetical pointer in each corrected passage back to AC-031 as the behavioral source of truth (e.g. "(the picker's iOS `Automatic`-mode H.264 SDR output; see AC-031)"), so a future reader who finds the prose does not re-introduce the "original native file" framing. This is the second time this cluster of prose has drifted from AC-031 (Rev 24 → Rev 26); a single explicit cross-reference reduces the chance of a third drift.
- **R2** — Keep the "no *client-side* compression" wording wherever it appears (it is still accurate and is a real Phase-1 property) but never let it sit next to "no re-encoding" or "original native file" — the distinction PM must hold throughout is: **the app performs no compression/encoding; iOS (the picker) performs a transcode.** Both statements are true simultaneously, and the prose must stop conflating them.

**Approved** (already correct — do not change):

- AC-031 (line 597) — internally consistent, correctly describes the `Automatic`-mode transcode, the H.264 SDR output, the 45 MB gate on the returned file, the lossy HDR→SDR Phase-1 limitation, and the Phase-2 MediaConvert removal. This is the source of truth.
- AC-035 (line 605) — "stored as-is (no server-side transcoding)" is about the server and remains correct; the Rev 26 transcode is on-device/pre-upload. Do not edit.
- AC-169 (line 655) — the 45 MB gate "applies to whatever file the picker returns"; consistent with Rev 26.
- §4 tech-stack video-hosting row (line 297) and the §9 storage-watch note — "stores original native files as-is in Supabase Storage (no transcoding)" at line 297 is borderline (the uploaded file is the picker's transcode, not the native capture), but it is describing *Supabase Storage's* behavior (no *server* transcoding), which is still accurate. **Optional** for PM to tighten "original native files" → "the files the client uploads" for precision; not required for correctness.

**Exact corrected text for PM to apply cleanly** (prose only; PM owns `prd.md`, applied via PM → Doc-Sync):

**Correction 1 — US-004 known-trade-off note (line 100).** Replace:

> **Known Phase 1 trade-off (accepted, not a bug)**: Phase 1 uploads the original native video with no client-side compression (AC-031). Because the 45 MB size gate (AC-169) applies to the raw file, a full-bitrate iPhone 1080p HEVC clip (~16 Mbps) reaches the 45 MB limit at roughly 22 seconds — well before the 60-second duration cap (AC-030). As a result, long high-quality clips are rejected with the AC-031 error, and users are expected to trim the clip or lower their camera's recording quality. This is a deliberate Phase 1 limitation of the no-compression approach; the Phase 2 server-side video pipeline (§4, §7) removes it by transcoding server-side.

with:

Because the 45 MB size gate (AC-169) applies to the picker's H.264 SDR output (~8–10 Mbps for iPhone 1080p), the gate is reached at roughly 36–45 seconds — much closer to the 60-second duration cap (AC-030) than under the earlier no-transcode model. As a result, only long clips are rejected with the AC-031 error, and those users are expected to trim the clip or lower their camera's recording quality. This is a deliberate Phase 1 limitation of the no-client-compression approach; the Phase 2 server-side video pipeline (§4, §7) removes it with a proper server-side HDR→SDR tone-map and transcode.

It authenticates via Supabase Auth (Email / Apple / Google) and stores the session locally.

with:

In Phase 1 the client performs **no** video compression of its own — it uploads the file the iOS picker returns as-is after an upfront size gate (≥ 45 MB is rejected; see §8 AC-031/AC-169). Because the picker runs in `Automatic` representation mode, that file is an iOS-transcoded H.264 SDR `.mov`, not the original HEVC/HDR capture (AC-031). It authenticates via Supabase Auth (Email / Apple / Google) and stores the session locally.

**Correction 3 — §5 data-flow loop 2 (line 321).** Replace the phrase:

> uploads the original native video file as-is to Supabase Storage after the upfront 45 MB size gate

with:

**Correction 4 — §10 NFR Video bullet (line 968).** Replace the three affected clauses. Specifically:

Because the picker runs in `Automatic` representation mode, iOS transcodes HEVC/HLG source footage to H.264 SDR before returning it, so the uploaded file is an iOS-transcoded H.264 SDR `.mov`, not the original native capture (AC-031, Rev 26)."
- Replace "because nothing is re-encoded, the file is stored as-is (per AC-035), so iPhone-camera `.mov`/HEVC footage uploads without conversion (Phase 1 relies on a container check only — iPhone camera/library footage is H.264 or HEVC + AAC by construction; a codec probe is a Phase 2 nicety)." with: "the file is stored as-is on the server (no *server-side* transcoding, per AC-035); the on-device iOS picker has already transcoded iPhone HEVC/HLG footage to an H.264 SDR `.mov` before upload (AC-031). Phase 1 relies on a container check only — the picker's output is H.264 + AAC by construction; a codec probe is a Phase 2 nicety."
- Replace "**Known Phase 1 trade-off**: at full iPhone 1080p HEVC bitrate the 45 MB gate is reached at ~22 s, well before the 60 s cap, so long high-quality clips are rejected and users trim or lower recording quality (see US-004) — this is removed by the Phase 2 server-side pipeline." with: "**Known Phase 1 trade-off**: at the picker's H.264 SDR export bitrate (~8–10 Mbps for iPhone 1080p) the 45 MB gate is reached at ~36–45 s, much closer to the 60 s cap, so only long clips are rejected and those users trim or lower recording quality (see US-004) — this is removed by the Phase 2 server-side pipeline." 

**Correction 5 (optional, precision) — §9 Data Model BetaVideo upload-size note (lines 880–886).** The note is not wrong (it only says "NO client-side compression," which stays true), but for consistency PM may append after "Phase 1 performs NO client-side compression (AC-031).": "(The iOS picker runs in `Automatic` mode and transcodes HEVC/HLG to H.264 SDR on-device before upload; the app adds no compression or re-encoding of its own — AC-031, Rev 26.)"
