### Review — 2026-10-01 — blocker (MOD-005 beta-video upload STILL 413 after the Rev-23 compression fix shipped — AC-169 size guard not actually gating)

**Context**: Follow-up to the earlier 2026-10-01 blocker review below. PRD Rev 23 added `react-native-compressor` + the AC-169 post-compression size guard, and that code shipped (`BetaVideoUploader.tsx`, commit 316b0d1). The 413 is now **back**: uploading `/Users/tsan/Downloads/IMG_7964.mov` (111 MB) on the iOS Simulator fails with HTTP 413 from Supabase Storage, and — critically — the AC-169 user-facing "too large" message is **not** shown first, so the guard is passing when it should block. Advisory only; I read the two source files, `production.md`, the installed `react-native-compressor@1.16.0`, `@supabase/storage-js`, and `expo-file-system@57.0.7` native/build sources, and probed the source video. No source, migration, spec, or PRD files changed here.

**What the probe established (the numbers rule out a "compression just isn't enough" theory):**
- Source `IMG_7964.mov`: 54.7 s, 1080×1920, **HEVC**, video bitrate **16.6 Mbps** → ~111 MB. (Note: HEVC, not H.264 — the compressor must transcode, not passthrough.)
- The manual config in `BetaVideoUploader.tsx` lines 194–204 targets `bitrate: 2_000_000` (2 Mbps) + AAC 128 kbps. For 54.7 s that is ≈ 2.0 Mbps × 54.7 s / 8 ≈ **13.7 MB video + ~0.9 MB audio ≈ ~15 MB**. That is far under the 45 MB guard and the 50 MB bucket. **So if compression actually ran and produced ~15 MB, the upload would succeed.** The 413 therefore means either compression did not run / did not shrink the file, or the guard never measured the real size. This eliminates root-cause (A) from the invocation (the bucket limit is not "effectively < 45 MB"; a genuinely-15 MB file would pass).

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):

- **PRIMARY ROOT CAUSE — `getInfoAsync` is imported from the wrong entry point and is a runtime-throwing deprecated shim in expo-file-system v57. This is the defect.** `BetaVideoUploader.tsx` line 20 does `import { getInfoAsync } from 'expo-file-system'`. In the installed `expo-file-system@57.0.7`, the top-level package does **not** export a working `getInfoAsync` — it only re-exports `legacyWarnings`, whose `getInfoAsync` is explicitly documented as: *"@deprecated Use `new File().info` or import this method from `expo-file-system/legacy`. **This method will throw in runtime.**"* (verified in `node_modules/expo-file-system/build/legacyWarnings.d.ts` and the top-level `build/index.d.ts`, which exports only `Paths`, `File`, `Directory`, `NetworkTasks`, types, and `legacyWarnings`). The working `getInfoAsync` lives at the `expo-file-system/legacy` subpath only. Consequences in the shipped flow:
  - Line 188 (`const sourceInfo = await getInfoAsync(asset.uri)`) is the **first** call and runs on the 300 MB source pre-guard, *before* compression. If this throws, control jumps to the `catch` at line 248 — but note the `catch` maps any thrown error's `.message` straight into the user error (line 249), so the user would see the legacy-throw message, not a 413. That is not the reported symptom, which means on this device/build the shim is **not** hard-throwing but instead returning a `FileInfo` with an **unreliable/undefined `size`** (the deprecated shim's documented behavior varies by patch; the simulator's virtual-filesystem path is exactly where size resolution is least reliable). Which of the two it is matters for the exact repro, but both paths lead to the same conclusion: this import is wrong and cannot be trusted as a size gate.
  - **Why the guard passes silently (the reported symptom):** the AC-169 check at lines 213–220 is `fileInfo.exists && fileInfo.size !== undefined && fileInfo.size > BUCKET_LIMIT_BYTES`. If `getInfoAsync` returns an object whose `size` is `undefined` (or `exists` is falsy) for the simulator path, **every term short-circuits to false, the guard is skipped, and the upload proceeds** with whatever `compressedUri` points at. That is precisely "guard passes, upload attempted, 413 returned." The guard is written to *fail open* — on any uncertainty about size it lets the upload through, which is the worst possible default for a size gate.
  - This is a self-inflicted inconsistency: **every other module already uses the correct v57 API.** `beta-video-service.ts` (line 18), `route-service.ts` (line 16), and `auth-service.ts` (line 12) all use `import { File as ExpoFile } from 'expo-file-system'` and `File.bytes()`. `BetaVideoUploader.tsx` is the *only* file in `src/` still importing the legacy top-level `getInfoAsync` (grep-confirmed). The size check should use the same `File` class the rest of the codebase already standardized on.

- **SECONDARY RISK — `react-native-compressor` on the iOS Simulator is an unreliable place to validate this at all.** The library compresses via AVFoundation (`NextLevelSessionExporter` → `AVAssetWriter`/VideoToolbox, verified in `ios/Video/VideoMain.swift`: `AVVideoCodecType.h264`, `AVVideoAverageBitRateKey: bitRate`). Hardware H.264/HEVC encode on the iOS Simulator runs through a software fallback that is historically slow and, for HEVC *input* specifically, can fail or produce a much larger-than-requested file. So even after the import bug is fixed, a simulator run may still produce an oversized file — at which point the *fixed* guard would correctly reject it with the AC-169 message, which is the right behavior but could be misread as "compression broke." **The compression result must be validated on a physical iOS device, not the simulator.** Expecting the simulator to prove this path end-to-end is itself a methodology risk. The project's own "Native build gate before QA handoff" convention (run `npx expo run:ios`, don't trust Jest which mocks the compressor) applies here — and should be extended to "verify compressed output size on a physical device."

- **TERTIARY — the storage-js 413 handling in `beta-video-service.ts` lines 130–131 is fragile and will not reliably catch a 413.** The service maps `(storageError as any).statusCode === '413' || status === 400` to the "too large" message. From the installed `@supabase/storage-js` (`src/lib/common/fetch.ts` lines 74–89): a 413 from the Supabase edge proxy usually has a non-JSON body, so `response.json()` rejects and the `.catch` branch sets `statusCode = String(status)` → `"413"` (so the check *can* match). **But** if the proxy resets/closes the connection on an oversized body (common for very large uploads), the failure surfaces as a non-`Response` error → `StorageUnknownError` with `statusCode === undefined` and `status === undefined`, and neither clause matches — the user gets the generic "Failed to upload" instead. This is a backstop quality issue, not the root cause (the guard should stop the upload long before storage sees it), but it means the service's 413 mapping cannot be relied on as the safety net.

**Recommendations** (advisory — human/PM/engineer decide; the fix itself is implementation under existing AC-169, route to `engineer-mod-beta-video`):**

- **Fix the import — this is the one change that resolves the reported bug.** Replace the two `getInfoAsync` calls in `BetaVideoUploader.tsx` with the `File` class already used everywhere else: `const info = new File(uri); const size = info.size;` (the v57 `File` class exposes a synchronous `size` property; `File.d.ts` declares `class File extends ExpoFileSystem.FileSystemFile implements Blob`, and `Blob` carries `size`). If a synchronous property is not populated for a freshly-written compressor output, use `await new File(uri).info()` and read `.size` from the result. Either way, import `File` from `'expo-file-system'` (the working top-level export) — do **not** reach for `expo-file-system/legacy`, since the rest of the codebase has already moved to the `File` class and adding a legacy-subpath import would re-introduce the same deprecated surface. This keeps MOD-005 consistent with `beta-video-service.ts`/`route-service.ts`/`auth-service.ts`.
- **Make the guard fail CLOSED, not open.** The current guard skips itself when size is unknown. Invert it: if the compressed file's size cannot be determined (undefined/null/0), treat that as a hard failure and either abort with the AC-169 message or log-and-block — never proceed to upload on an unknown size. A size gate that lets unknown-size files through is not a gate. (This is the Option-D "add a debug log of the real compressed size before upload" idea from the invocation, hardened into an actual gate rather than just a log — recommend doing both: log the measured size, and block on unknown/over-limit.)
- **Re-run the repro on a physical iOS device after the import fix**, per the Secondary risk. If the compressed `IMG_7964.mov` lands at ~15 MB on device, the upload will succeed and the bug is closed. If it lands over 45 MB on device (possible for HEVC input), the *fixed* guard will now correctly show the AC-169 message — that is the intended behavior, and the follow-on question (is 2 Mbps/1280 aggressive enough for 1080×1920 HEVC source) becomes a tuning decision, not a defect.
- **Harden the service 413 mapping as a backstop (optional, low priority).** In `beta-video-service.ts`, broaden the catch to also treat `StorageUnknownError` / undefined-status upload failures on the video path as a probable-too-large case, or at minimum log the raw `storageError` shape so a future 413-via-connection-reset is diagnosable. This is defense-in-depth; it is not the fix.
- **On the invocation's Option menu (A–D):** the right architecture for Phase 1 is unchanged from the prior review — **compress before upload (already chosen), with a correctly-wired post-compression size guard (Option D, hardened into a real gate)**. Option B (just enforce 50 MB with better diagnostics) is already the intent of AC-169 and only needs the import fixed. Option C (Edge Function proxy) and TUS resumable upload remain **out of scope for Phase 1** (see the TUS analysis in the prior review below — TUS does not reduce stored bytes and requires departing from the `File.bytes()`/`Uint8Array` upload convention). Do not reach for C or TUS to fix what is a one-line import bug.

**Approved**:
- The overall Rev-23 architecture (compress → size-guard → `File.bytes()` → `.upload()`) is sound and does not need rethinking. The defect is a wrong import of one API, plus a guard that fails open — both are small, local fixes in `BetaVideoUploader.tsx`.
- The compression config intent (2 Mbps / 1280 longest side) is reasonable for Phase 1 and mathematically lands a 54.7 s clip at ~15 MB; no change needed pending a physical-device measurement.
- The `File.bytes()` → `Uint8Array` → `.upload()` transport remains correct and is not implicated in the 413.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for Doc-Sync to carry into production.md, only if the human/PM approve):
- **Use the `expo-file-system` v57 `File` class for ALL file metadata and IO — never the deprecated top-level `getInfoAsync`/`readAsStringAsync`/etc. shims, which throw or return unreliable values at runtime in SDK 57.** File size for upload guards is read via `new File(uri).size` (or `await new File(uri).info()`), consistent with the existing `File.bytes()` upload convention. The legacy `expo-file-system/legacy` subpath is not to be used in new code. Reference the `File`-class usage in `beta-video-service.ts`, `route-service.ts`, and `auth-service.ts`.
- **Size/validation guards on uploads must fail CLOSED.** If a file's size cannot be determined, the guard blocks the upload (and surfaces the user-facing limit error) rather than letting an unmeasured file through. An upload guard that proceeds on unknown size is a defect.
- **Native-module behavior (video compression) must be verified on a physical iOS device before QA sign-off, not the iOS Simulator.** The simulator's software AVFoundation fallback is unreliable for H.264/HEVC transcode and for virtual-filesystem size reads; a green Jest run (which mocks the compressor and file system) is not evidence the real pipeline works. This extends the existing "Native build gate before QA handoff" convention to include a device-measured compression-output size check.

### Review — 2026-10-01 — blocker (MOD-005 beta-video upload HTTP 413: 50 MB bucket limit vs. 100–500 MB iPhone 60s videos)

**Context**: Human requested a Tech Lead review of a video upload size failure in MOD-005. The current upload path (`expo-file-system` `File.bytes()` → `Uint8Array` → `supabase.storage.upload()`) hits HTTP 413 because the Supabase `beta-videos` bucket default max is 50 MB, while an iPhone 60-second clip is commonly 100–500 MB. Four options are under consideration: (A) raise the bucket limit, (B) a client-side size guard, (C) client-side compression with `react-native-compressor`, (D) Supabase TUS resumable upload. Advisory only — no source, migration, spec, or PRD files changed here.

**Grounding in shipped code (important — the stated premise that compression already exists is not accurate):**
- `src/modules/mod-beta-video/components/BetaVideoUploader.tsx` does **not** actually compress. It calls `expo-image-picker` with `quality: 0.7` (line 153), which the code itself annotates as "Expo quality hint; actual compression is device-dependent" (line 153) and defers real codec-level compression to a note citing `ffmpeg-kit-react-native` (lines 9, 182–186). So AC-031's "run client-side video compression before upload" is **not met today** — the 413 is the symptom of a pre-existing gap, not a regression.
- `src/modules/mod-beta-video/beta-video-service.ts` lines 103–121: upload is `File.bytes()` → `Uint8Array` → `.upload(fileName, uint8Array, { contentType })`, exactly the project convention (production.md "File Upload to Supabase Storage", line 131). No size check anywhere in the upload path.
- The PRD/production.md already name the **compression library convention as `ffmpeg-kit-react-native`** (production.md line 127, "The recommended client-side compression library is `ffmpeg-kit-react-native` (H.264 output guaranteed)"), NOT `react-native-compressor`. The MOD-005 code comments reference the same library. This directly bears on Q1.

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):
- **Option A alone is not acceptable as the fix, and it is not a code-free no-op — it is an AC-031 violation plus an architecture-trigger accelerant.** Raising the bucket to 500 MB and doing nothing else stores raw, uncompressed iPhone footage indefinitely. AC-031 ("shall run client-side video compression before upload") is a hard requirement, so shipping A alone would leave an AC in a permanent fail state. It also drives straight at the PRD's documented video-hosting migration trigger (PRD §4 / production.md line 321: migrate to Cloudflare Stream when monthly cost > US$25 **OR** total storage > 20 GB). At 200–500 MB/video, 20 GB is ~40–100 videos — the project could trip its own Phase-2 migration trigger within the first week of real use, and the migration runbook is still an open risk (PRD §12, "no documented runbook yet"). A must only ship as a *headroom* raise behind compression, not as the mitigation.
- **The real defect is the missing compression step, which is the only one of the four options that fixes both the 413 and the AC-031 gap at once.** B (size guard) turns a 413 into a user-facing rejection but blocks legitimate uploads — a normal 60s iPhone video would be rejected, failing US-004's core promise. D (TUS) makes a 500 MB upload *succeed more reliably* but does not reduce the stored bytes, so it worsens the storage-trigger problem and does nothing for AC-031. Only C (compress before upload) addresses the root cause.
- **Library naming conflict that Doc-Sync/Engineering must not resolve silently.** The option under consideration is `react-native-compressor`, but the *existing documented convention* (production.md line 127, and the MOD-005 code comments) is `ffmpeg-kit-react-native`. These are not interchangeable and the project cannot carry two contradictory "the compression library is X" statements. Whichever is chosen, the production.md convention and the MOD-005 code comments must be reconciled to name exactly one — this requires a PM-logged change, not an ad-hoc edit by whoever implements it.

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements) — answering the five questions directly:

- **Q1 — library choice: recommend `react-native-compressor`, and reconcile the stale `ffmpeg-kit-react-native` convention. This is the one real decision for the human/PM.** `react-native-compressor` is the better-maintained, lower-risk choice for an Expo SDK 57 bare workflow in 2026: it is a thin (~2 MB) wrapper over the OS-native video pipeline (iOS AVFoundation), ships as an Expo config plugin, and has no large native toolchain to drag into the iOS build. `ffmpeg-kit-react-native` — the library currently named in the convention — is the riskier option now: the upstream FFmpegKit project was **officially retired/archived in early 2025**, its prebuilt binaries are being pulled from distribution, it is a large binary that inflates app size and iOS build time, and it carries LGPL/GPL licensing considerations for App Store distribution. Given the convention was written when FFmpegKit was still the default and the shipped code never actually integrated it (only a comment), switching the convention to `react-native-compressor` is low-cost. **This is advisory — the human and PM decide.** If they keep FFmpegKit they must account for the retirement/binary-availability risk; if they switch, the production.md convention (line 127) and the MOD-005 code comments must be updated to match via a PM-logged change → Doc-Sync. Either way, per production.md "Native dependency installation" (PRD §10 line 969), it must be installed with `npx expo install`, never hand-pinned, and per the "Native build gate before QA handoff" (line 970) the MOD-005 engineer must run `npx expo run:ios` and confirm the native build compiles before QA — a compressor is a native module, so passing Jest (which mocks it) is not sufficient evidence.
- **Q2 — TUS is NOT mutually exclusive with `File.bytes()`, but it is a different upload call, and it is not needed for Phase 1.** `File.bytes()` is how you obtain the bytes; TUS is a different *transport* for sending them (Supabase's `@supabase/storage-js` resumable/TUS path via `uploadToSignedUrl`/the tus-js-client integration, which takes a `Blob`/`File`/stream, not a bare `Uint8Array`). So adopting TUS *would* require departing from the `File.bytes()` → `Uint8Array` → `.upload()` convention for videos specifically, which is exactly why it should not be reached for casually: the `Response.blob()` ban exists because the RN blob path silently corrupted uploads (production.md line 131), and TUS on RN pulls the data through a comparable Blob/stream path that has historically been fragile in Expo. **Recommendation: do not adopt TUS for Phase 1.** Once compression (C) brings a 60s clip down to the tens-of-MB range, the single-shot `.upload(uint8Array)` the project already uses is adequate and keeps the one proven upload path. Revisit TUS in Phase 2 only if post-compression files or flaky-network resumability become a measured problem — and if so, treat it as a deliberate, scoped exception to the upload convention, documented as such, not a silent swap.
- **Q3 — compression runs AFTER the AC-030 60-second duration check, not before.** The duration gate is a cheap metadata read on the picked asset and is the cheapest possible rejection — rejecting an over-60s video before spending 5–20 seconds of device CPU/battery compressing it is strictly better UX and the only ordering that makes sense. The shipped code already does the duration check first (`BetaVideoUploader.tsx` line 124, "AC-030: Check duration before compression/upload begins"), so the correct pipeline is: **pick → AC-030 duration check (reject if >60s) → compress (show % progress via the existing AC-036 upload overlay, extended to cover the compress phase) → `File.bytes()` → `.upload()`**. Note the AC-036 progress overlay currently covers upload 0–100%; compression adds a visible pre-upload phase, so the overlay copy/states should distinguish "compressing" from "uploading" so the user isn't staring at a frozen 0%.
- **Q4 — yes, Option A alone has real storage/cost/architecture concerns (covered in Concerns above).** Raw-file retention at 100–500 MB/clip trips the 20 GB Cloudflare-migration trigger within ~40–100 videos and runs up Supabase Storage egress/cost far faster than compressed footage. A 500 MB bucket ceiling is still worth setting as *headroom behind compression* (a compressed clip occasionally exceeding a tighter limit should not 413), but the bucket ceiling is a backstop, not the fix. If A is applied, set the ceiling to a sane post-compression headroom value (e.g. 100–200 MB), not an unbounded 500 MB, so a compression failure can't silently dump a half-GB raw file into storage.
- **Q5 — this is an engineering implementation detail under existing AC-031 + AC-034, with one small exception that is a PRD-level change. Recommend NOT opening a new AC for the compression itself.** AC-031 already mandates client-side compression before upload; wiring a real compressor to satisfy an AC that already exists is implementation, not a new requirement — route it through the owning engineer (`engineer-mod-beta-video`), not a PRD revision. **However, two things ARE PRD-level and must go through PM:** (1) the library-convention reconciliation in Q1 (production.md line 127 + the MOD-005 code comment name `ffmpeg-kit-react-native`; if switching to `react-native-compressor`, that is a Shared Conventions change — Tech Lead finding → PM log → Doc-Sync, never an ad-hoc edit); and (2) if the team wants an explicit *post-compression size guard with a user-facing error* (a hardened Option B as a backstop for compression failures), that introduces new user-facing behavior and should be a small new AC under US-004 so QA has something concrete to verify. The bucket-limit raise (A) is a Supabase dashboard config change (operator action, no code), not an AC.

**Approved**:
- The existing `File.bytes()` → `Uint8Array` → `.upload()` convention for the upload transport itself — it is not the cause of the 413 and does not need to change for Phase 1. The fix sits *upstream* of it (compress before taking bytes), leaving the proven upload path intact.
- The existing pipeline ordering that already puts the AC-030 duration check first (`BetaVideoUploader.tsx` line 124) — compression slots in immediately after it.
- The existing AC-036 progress overlay as the surface for compression progress — extend its states to cover the compress phase rather than inventing a second overlay.
- The recommendation path itself as advisory: Option C (compress) is the fix, Option A is an optional headroom backstop, Options B and D are not needed for Phase 1.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for Doc-Sync to carry into production.md, only if the human/PM approve the library decision in Q1):
- **Client-side video compression runs after the AC-030 duration gate and before `File.bytes()`/upload, using the single confirmed compressor library.** Pipeline: pick → duration check (reject >60s) → compress to H.264/AAC MP4 (`react-native-compressor`, pending human/PM confirmation; supersedes the prior `ffmpeg-kit-react-native` note in the Video Pipeline Conventions if approved) → `File.bytes()` → `supabase.storage.upload(uint8Array)`. The compressor is a native module: install via `npx expo install`, never hand-pin, and run `npx expo run:ios` to confirm the native build before QA handoff. The Supabase bucket max-file-size is a headroom backstop behind compression (set to a post-compression-sized ceiling, e.g. 100–200 MB), never the primary size control. TUS/resumable upload is explicitly out of scope for Phase 1 — the single-shot `Uint8Array` upload is the one approved video upload transport; revisiting TUS in Phase 2 requires a documented, scoped exception to the File Upload convention.

### Review — 2026-09-30 — change (Rev 18: user-profile navigation from reel viewer + Followers/Following list stats — AC-135/AC-144/AC-146–AC-148, MOD-006→MOD-008 dependency)

**Context**: PM flagged four items for mandatory Tech Lead review before Doc-Sync on Rev 18 [SUBSTANTIVE]. All four concern threading navigation to another user's profile and sourcing per-user stats across module boundaries. Advisory only — no source, migration, spec, or PRD files changed here. Grounded in the shipped code: `src/modules/mod-beta-video/components/BetaVideoReelViewer.tsx` (reel viewer props + AC-135 no-op tap comment at Row 1), `src/modules/mod-home/AppShell.tsx` (`handleSelectClimber`/`handleNavigateToRoute` patterns + `UserProfileScreen` overlay mount), `src/modules/mod-social-feed/screens/UserProfileScreen.tsx` (props: `targetUserId`, `session`, `onBack`; `UserProfileNavigator.tsx` re-exports it as the module-root entry point), `src/modules/mod-profile-history/history-service.ts` + `supabase/migrations/20260929000002_mod_008_profile_history_v2.sql` (`get_profile_stats(p_user_id UUID)` single-user RPC), and `src/modules/mod-beta-video/beta-video-service.ts` + `mod-beta-video/types.ts` (`fetchBetaVideosForRoute` select already includes `user_id`; `BetaVideo` type already has `user_id`, `display_name`, `avatar_url`).

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):
- **Q1 — the profile surface the reel viewer must reach is MOD-006's `UserProfileScreen`, not "MOD-008" as AC-135 phrases it.** AC-135 (and AC-064) say "navigate to the MOD-008 other-user profile-and-history surface." In the shipped code the other-user profile surface is `src/modules/mod-social-feed/screens/UserProfileScreen.tsx` (exposed via `UserProfileNavigator.tsx`), which internally embeds the MOD-008 history/stats. `AppShell` already imports `UserProfileScreen from '../mod-social-feed/UserProfileNavigator'` and mounts it as an overlay via `handleSelectClimber(userId)`. If Doc-Sync/Engineering take "MOD-008" literally they will look for a profile screen MOD-008 does not own and get the module boundary wrong. The spec must name the concrete target: the overlay path already wired in `AppShell` (the same one used for AC-123 climber taps). This is a naming/ownership clarification, not a redesign — the surface already exists and is already reachable from the shell.
- **Q1 — the reel viewer's AC-135 Row-1 tap is currently a hard no-op and must be converted to a callback prop, mirroring the existing `onNavigateToRoute` prop, NOT a new shell state channel.** `BetaVideoReelViewer` already accepts `onNavigateToRoute?: (routeId: string) => void` (Row 2 / AC-136) and its Row 1 is documented as "AC-135: no-op tap in Phase 1." The revised AC-135 requires Row 1 to dismiss and navigate to the poster's profile. The correct shape is a new optional prop `onNavigateToUser?: (userId: string) => void` on `BetaVideoReelViewerProps`, called with `currentVideo.user_id` (already on the row), then `onDismiss()` — exactly parallel to how Row 2 calls `onNavigateToRoute(currentVideo.route_id)`. MOD-005 must not import `UserProfileScreen`/`UserProfileNavigator` — that would violate the Cross-Module Imports convention (deep-import into another module's flow) and invert the dependency direction (MOD-005 does not depend on MOD-006).

**Recommendations** (suggested improvements):
- **Q1 — thread `onNavigateToUser(userId)` down the same mount chain the reel viewer already sits on, and reuse the existing `AppShell` climber-overlay action as the sink.** The reel viewer is mounted `AppShell → GymNavigator → RouteNavigator → RouteDetailScreen → BetaVideoReelViewer`. `onNavigateToRoute` is already threaded down that chain, so `onNavigateToUser` follows the identical, already-proven path — no new architecture. At the top, wire it to the **same action `handleSelectClimber` already uses for AC-123** (`setTargetUserId(userId)`), which opens `UserProfileScreen` as the modal overlay. Do NOT invent a new "switch to Profile tab + deep-link userId into Tab 3" mechanism: Tab 3 (`ProfileNavigator`) is the *current user's* profile; another user's profile is an overlay in the current codebase, and AC-123 already established that pattern. Reusing it keeps one code path for "open another user's profile from anywhere," which is strictly better than adding a second (tab-deep-link) path that would diverge in back-behavior. **Ownership of the action**: MOD-012 (`AppShell`) owns the "open another user's profile" navigation action (it already does, for AC-123); MOD-005 only emits the intent via the callback; MOD-006 owns the profile screen. This is the same host-screen/owning-module split already documented in the "Relocated Cross-Module Entry Points" and "Cross-Module Imports" conventions.
  - Note on the "Profile tab accepts a `userId` deep-link" option raised in Q1: this is achievable but is a larger change (Tab 3 would need to switch between own-profile and other-profile modes, and define back-behavior returning to the reel's route context vs. the profile tab). It is not needed for Rev 18 and would introduce a second, divergent navigation path. Recommend deferring; the overlay reuse is the lower-risk Phase 1 choice. If the human/PM prefer the tab-deep-link model for consistency with the Gyms-tab `routeDeepLinkId` pattern, that is a decision for them — record it as a follow-up, do not silently pick it.
- **Q2 — use option (a): per-card `get_profile_stats(p_user_id)` calls on list render, one per visible card.** For Phase 1 list sizes (≤100 followers typical, FlatList windowing renders ~10–15 cards at a time) this is correct and matches AC-148's explicit "Fetching is per-card on list render (acceptable for Phase 1 list sizes)." The RPC is `SECURITY INVOKER STABLE`, so RLS + the in-RPC privacy gate apply automatically per target user (a `followers_only` non-follower correctly gets zeroed stats). Reject option (b) `get_profile_stats_batch` for Phase 1: it is a new RPC + migration + a second stats code path to keep consistent with the single-user RPC, unjustified at these list sizes (premature optimization). Reject option (c) client-side join on `ascents`/`routes`: it bypasses the MOD-008 stats service, would re-derive "highest grade" and "total sends (flash|top)" client-side, and risks disagreeing with the canonical `get_profile_stats` definition (total_sends counts `flash`+`top`; highest_grade is `MAX(grade)` over the same privacy gate) — exactly the "aggregate disagreeing with the source-of-truth reduction" failure the existing conventions warn against. Pin option (a) so the MOD-006 engineer wires each card to `fetchProfileStats(userId)` (the existing `history-service.ts` wrapper) and reads `total_sends` + `highest_grade` from the result.
  - Card render guidance: call `fetchProfileStats` lazily per card (e.g. in the card component's effect keyed on `userId`), not eagerly for the entire list up front, so only on-screen cards fetch. If a follower list ever exceeds ~200 in practice, revisit with option (b) as a Phase 2 optimization — record the threshold, do not build the batch RPC now.
- **Q3 — the MOD-006 → MOD-008 dependency is safe and already declared in the PRD; express it as a public-service import of `fetchProfileStats` from `mod-profile-history/history-service.ts`, no re-export or new shared service.** MOD-006's PRD dependency line already lists MOD-008 (`Dependencies: MOD-001, MOD-004, MOD-005, MOD-008`), so this is not a new edge in the dependency graph — Rev 18 only exercises it. It is consistent with the established cross-module public-service convention (MOD-003 calls MOD-004's `fetchMyAscentsForRoute`; MOD-008's own `fetchProfileHistory`/`fetchProfileStats` are the public data layer). Per the "Cross-Module Imports" convention, MOD-006 may import MOD-008's **public service function** directly (`import { fetchProfileStats } from '../mod-profile-history/history-service'`); it must not reach into `mod-profile-history/screens/` or `components/`. Do NOT add a thin re-export or a new shared service — `history-service.ts` is already the public surface and adding an indirection layer would be a boundary-obscuring anti-pattern here (there is exactly one consumer and one canonical function). Document the cross-module import in the MOD-006 spec so it is not mistaken for a boundary violation, per the existing convention text.
- **Q4 — confirmed: no migration needed for AC-144.** `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` already selects `user_id` explicitly (`'id, route_id, user_id, video_url, ...'`) and the embedded `users!beta_videos_user_id_fkey(display_name, avatar_url)` join is already in place (the FK repoint to `public.users(id)` shipped in migration `20260928000001`). The `BetaVideo` TypeScript type already declares `user_id: string` (line 13), `display_name`, and `avatar_url`. So AC-144's "add `user_id` to the poster join" is a no-op at the data layer — `user_id` is already returned and already typed. The only real change Rev 18 requires is wiring: the reel viewer must *use* `currentVideo.user_id` in the new `onNavigateToUser` callback (Q1). No schema change, no migration, no service-query change for AC-144.

**Approved**:
- The existing reel-viewer callback-prop architecture (`onNavigateToRoute` for AC-136) as the template for the new `onNavigateToUser` prop. It already correctly threads navigation intent out of a deeply-mounted media viewer without the viewer importing another module's screen — Rev 18 just adds a second, parallel callback.
- The existing `AppShell` climber-overlay path (`handleSelectClimber` → `setTargetUserId` → `UserProfileScreen` overlay) as the reuse target for "open another user's profile." It is already the AC-123 mechanism and already respects privacy (AC-063) inside `UserProfileScreen`.
- `get_profile_stats(p_user_id UUID)` as the single source of truth for per-user stats. Its `SECURITY INVOKER` + in-RPC privacy gate make per-card calls safe without any MOD-006-side privacy logic.
- The MOD-006 → MOD-008 public-service dependency as consistent with the codebase's cross-module conventions.
- The AC-144 data layer as already complete — no migration, confirmed.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for Doc-Sync to carry into production.md):
- **Navigating to another user's profile is a shell-owned action reached via a callback prop; deeply-mounted screens never import the profile screen.** Any screen that needs to open another user's profile (e.g. the beta-video reel viewer's poster row, a follower/following card, a feed author) must accept an `onNavigateToUser?: (userId: string) => void` callback prop and call it with the target user's `user_id`, then dismiss itself if it is a modal/overlay. The prop is threaded down the mount chain to `AppShell` (MOD-012), which owns the single "open another user's profile" action — in Phase 1 this opens MOD-006's `UserProfileScreen` (via `UserProfileNavigator`) as a modal-style overlay (the same mechanism as the AC-123 climber-chip tap: `setTargetUserId(userId)`). A deeply-mounted screen must NEVER import `UserProfileScreen`/`UserProfileNavigator` directly — that both violates the Cross-Module Imports rule and inverts the dependency direction. This mirrors the existing `onNavigateToRoute` callback pattern for route navigation. The "other user's profile" is an overlay, not the Profile tab (Tab 3 is the current user's own profile); do not deep-link another user's id into Tab 3 in Phase 1.
- **Per-user stats on any list of users are sourced per-card from MOD-008's `get_profile_stats` public service (`fetchProfileStats(userId)`), never from a client-side `ascents`/`routes` query and never from a bespoke batch RPC in Phase 1.** Consuming modules (e.g. MOD-006's Followers/Following cards) import `fetchProfileStats` from `mod-profile-history/history-service.ts` and call it lazily per visible card. The RPC is `SECURITY INVOKER STABLE` with a built-in privacy gate, so a `followers_only` non-follower correctly receives zeroed stats with no consumer-side privacy logic. Deriving "total sends" or "highest grade" client-side is prohibited (it risks disagreeing with the canonical `get_profile_stats` definition — total_sends = count of `flash`+`top`, highest_grade = `MAX(grade)` under the same privacy gate). A batch `get_profile_stats_batch(user_ids[])` is a Phase 2 optimization to revisit only if real list sizes exceed ~200; do not build it for Phase 1. This is the list-of-users analogue of the existing "Personal Cross-Module Data Overlays" and "Client-Side Aggregation Over Already-Fetched Arrays" conventions: reuse the owning module's canonical reduction, do not re-derive it downstream.

### Review — 2026-09-30 — change (file-upload convention: ExpoFile.bytes() over fetch+blob)

**Context**: Two independent modules shipped Supabase Storage file uploads using `fetch(localUri)` + `response.blob()`, and both failed in real-world use. MOD-005 (beta-video) hit it first with video uploads; MOD-003 (route-catalog) then repeated the same mistake with route-photo uploads (fixed 2026-09-30). The correct pattern — `expo-file-system`'s `File.bytes()` returning a `Uint8Array` directly — is now established in both modules, but it was never written down as a project-wide convention, so MOD-003 had no reference to prevent re-introducing the broken pattern. This entry records it as a Proposed Shared Convention so PM can tag it and Doc-Sync can carry it into `production.md`. Advisory only — no source, spec, or PRD files changed here.

Grounded in the shipped code: `src/modules/mod-beta-video/beta-video-service.ts` (video upload — imports `File as ExpoFile` from `expo-file-system`, `new ExpoFile(localVideoUri).bytes()` → `.upload(fileName, uint8Array, ...)`, with a header comment noting `File.bytes()` avoids the base64 detour / `fetch()` corruption risk) and `src/modules/mod-route-catalog/route-service.ts` (photo upload — same `ExpoFile(localUri).bytes()` → `.upload(storagePath, uint8Array, ...)` pattern, with an inline comment that the `fetch()+blob()` pattern is unreliable in RN/Expo SDK 57 and causes upload failures). Both reference implementations match the convention verbatim.

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):
- None. The pattern fix is already correct and shipped in both modules. The only gap is documentation: without a written convention, a third upload path (e.g. a future profile-avatar upload, a gym-photo admin flow, or any new binary asset) could reintroduce `fetch()+blob()` and fail the same way. Recording the convention closes that recurrence risk — this is the same class of "the fix wasn't written down, so the mistake repeated" gap as the Rev 11 native-dependency conventions.

**Recommendations**:
- Pin one canonical upload pattern in `production.md` Shared Conventions (below) so every current and future Supabase Storage upload of a local device file copies it. Point new engineers at the two reference implementations.
- When a new module needs to upload a local file, it must import `File as ExpoFile` from `expo-file-system` and pass the `Uint8Array` from `.bytes()` to `supabase.storage.from(bucket).upload(...)` with an explicit `contentType`. A code-review/QA check should reject any `Response.blob()` in an upload path.

**Approved**:
- The `ExpoFile.bytes()` upload approach as shipped in MOD-005 and MOD-003. It is the correct RN/Expo pattern: `File.bytes()` returns a `Uint8Array` with no base64 round-trip, unlike `Response.blob()`, which in RN/Expo SDK 57+ routes binary data through the native blob store via base64 and fails silently at real-world file sizes.

**Proposed Shared Conventions** (for Doc-Sync to carry into production.md):
- **File uploads to Supabase Storage must use `expo-file-system`'s `File.bytes()` — never `fetch + blob()`.** All Supabase Storage uploads of local device files (photos, videos, or any binary asset) must read the file with `new ExpoFile(localUri).bytes()` (importing `File as ExpoFile` from `expo-file-system`) and pass the resulting `Uint8Array` directly to `supabase.storage.from(bucket).upload(path, uint8Array, { contentType })`. The `fetch(localUri)` + `response.blob()` pattern must never be used: in React Native / Expo SDK 57+, `Response.blob()` copies data through the native blob store via base64 encoding, causing silent upload failures for real-world file sizes (photos, videos). `ExpoFile.bytes()` returns a `Uint8Array` directly with no base64 round-trip. Reference implementations: `src/modules/mod-beta-video/beta-video-service.ts` (video upload) and `src/modules/mod-route-catalog/route-service.ts` (photo upload, fixed 2026-09-30).

### Review — 2026-09-29 — change (setup.md secret-name correction, line 140)

**Context**: QA flagged a documentation error in `project-planning/setup.md`. Setup-doc correction only — no PRD change, no Doc-Sync, no source/migration changes. Advisory scope: setup.md is Tech Lead-owned.

**Concerns** (must address before proceeding):
- (resolved) `setup.md` line 140 instructed `supabase secrets set SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=...`. Supabase reserves the `SUPABASE_*` prefix for its own runtime-injected variables; a secret stored under that name is unreachable from Edge Functions. Every other reference in the project (`.env.example`, `production.md`, the `report-digest` Edge Function) already uses the unprefixed `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`. The setup doc was the lone divergent copy and would have caused a runtime failure at first invocation despite a successful `supabase secrets set`.

**Recommendations**:
- None beyond the fix. The corrected name now matches the rest of the project, so the whole codebase is consistent on `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`.

**Approved**:
- Fix applied: line 140 now reads `supabase secrets set SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=your-service-role-key`. Single-line change, no other edits to setup.md.

### Review — 2026-09-28 — change (Rev 16: MOD-005 reel viewer overlay + one-tap beta-video report flow — AC-133–AC-144, AC-039/AC-080 revised)

**Context**: PRD Revision 16 [SUBSTANTIVE] adds the reel-viewer identity overlay + JS marquee (AC-133–AC-138), the one-tap beta-video report flow (AC-139–AC-143), the `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` join (AC-144), revises AC-039 (swipe-down = previous-or-dismiss) and AC-080 (user-profile-only), and shifts beta-video reporting from MOD-009 to MOD-005. PM flagged six items for mandatory Tech Lead review **before Doc-Sync**: (1) migration for the two new `beta_videos` columns; (2) RLS on `video_reports`; (3) SECURITY DEFINER RPC scope for AC-140; (4) `Report`/`VideoReport` coexistence; (5) the single shared exclusion filter reused by `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` (AC-142) and the MOD-006 feed RPC (AC-141); (6) the poster `display_name`+`avatar_url` join (AC-144). Advisory only — no source, migration, spec, or PRD files changed here. Findings grounded in the shipped code: `20260924000005_mod_005_beta_video.sql` (beta_videos DDL — **`user_id` FK targets `auth.users(id)`, not `public.users(id)`**), `20260924000006_mod_006_social_feed.sql` (`get_activity_feed` SECURITY INVOKER RPC — `video_items` CTE has no report filter today), `beta-video-service.ts` (`fetchBetaVideosForRoute` — explicit column select, no join, no filter), `mod-beta-video/types.ts` (`BetaVideo` interface), `20260923000002_mod_003_route_approval_schema.sql` (the shipped `submit_route` SECURITY DEFINER pattern), and `production.md` §"SECURITY DEFINER RPCs". Doc-Sync carries the decisions/conventions into the MOD-005/MOD-006/MOD-009 specs + production.md; Engineering implements in `change`-mode work.

Headline: **one blocking prerequisite** — `beta_videos.user_id` currently references `auth.users(id)`, so PostgREST's embedded join `select('*, users(display_name, avatar_url)')` for AC-144 will NOT work until the FK is repointed to `public.users(id)`. Everything else is a clean, additive, single-migration change with an established RPC pattern to copy.

---

#### Q1 — Migration filename + file grouping: ONE new file, both columns + the table + the RPC together.

- **Next filename**: following the shipped `YYYYMMDDNNNNNN_mod_NNN_<slug>.sql` convention and the existing MOD-005 file (`20260924000005_mod_005_beta_video.sql`), the new migration should be dated after the latest shipped migration (`20260924000007_mod_007_notifications.sql`). Recommended name: **`20260928000001_mod_005_video_reports.sql`** (use the current date `20260928`; keep `mod_005` since MOD-005 now owns beta-video reporting per the Rev 16 boundary shift). Migrations are immutable and ordered by filename — do not edit the existing `20260924000005` file; this is a new forward migration.
- **One file, not two.** Put all four DDL objects in this single migration, in this order: (1) `ALTER TABLE public.beta_videos ADD COLUMN is_reported ... ADD COLUMN report_dismissed ...`; (2) the FK repoint (Q6 — drop the `auth.users` FK, add the `public.users` FK); (3) `CREATE TABLE public.video_reports ...` + RLS + grants; (4) the `report_beta_video` SECURITY DEFINER RPC + grants. Rationale: they are one logical feature, none is subject to the PG15 ADD-VALUE in-transaction restriction (that rule is enum-only — see the Rev 9 review), and keeping them together makes the report feature apply atomically on `supabase db reset`. This is NOT the two-file enum case.
- **Two `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` are safe in one transaction** — adding columns with a constant `DEFAULT false NOT NULL` is a metadata-only operation on PG11+ (no table rewrite), fast even on a populated `beta_videos`. Existing rows get `is_reported = false, report_dismissed = false`, which is the correct "not reported" state.

#### Q2 — SECURITY DEFINER RPC for AC-140: confirmed, with the exact signature + body below. Idempotent, own-scope, minimal writes.

- **Confirmed signature (refined)**: `report_beta_video(p_beta_video_id uuid) RETURNS void`. Takes only the video id — **never** a `reporter_id` parameter (production.md §"SECURITY DEFINER RPCs": "Never accept `user_id` as a parameter — always derive from `auth.uid()`"). The reporter is `auth.uid()` inside the function.
- **Exact recommended body** (mirrors the shipped `submit_route` pattern — `LANGUAGE plpgsql`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `SET search_path = public`, EXECUTE granted to `authenticated` only, revoked from `anon`/`public`):
  ```sql
  CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.report_beta_video(p_beta_video_id UUID)
  RETURNS VOID
  LANGUAGE plpgsql
  SECURITY DEFINER
  SET search_path = public
  AS $$
  DECLARE
    v_owner UUID;
  BEGIN
    -- Must be authenticated
    IF auth.uid() IS NULL THEN
      RAISE EXCEPTION 'Not authenticated';
    END IF;

    -- Video must exist; capture owner to forbid self-report (AC-139: report
    -- affordance is hidden for own videos — enforce server-side too, defense in depth)
    SELECT user_id INTO v_owner FROM public.beta_videos WHERE id = p_beta_video_id;
    IF v_owner IS NULL THEN
      RAISE EXCEPTION 'Beta video not found';
    END IF;
    IF v_owner = auth.uid() THEN
      RAISE EXCEPTION 'Cannot report your own video';
    END IF;

    -- (1) Insert exactly one report row; idempotent on the unique (beta_video_id, reporter_id)
    INSERT INTO public.video_reports (beta_video_id, reporter_id)
    VALUES (p_beta_video_id, auth.uid())
    ON CONFLICT (beta_video_id, reporter_id) DO NOTHING;

    -- (2) Flip the flag — the ONLY field this RPC writes on beta_videos
    UPDATE public.beta_videos
      SET is_reported = TRUE
      WHERE id = p_beta_video_id;
  END;
  $$;

  REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.report_beta_video(UUID) FROM anon, public;
  GRANT  EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.report_beta_video(UUID) TO authenticated;
  ```
- **Scope is deliberately minimal** (addresses the §12 over-grant risk): the RPC writes exactly two things — one `video_reports` row and `beta_videos.is_reported = TRUE`. It must **never** touch `report_dismissed` (admin-only, Studio-only per AC-143) or any other `beta_videos` column. Do not add a general "update beta_videos" DEFINER function; keep this single-purpose.
- **Idempotency**: `ON CONFLICT ... DO NOTHING` on the `(beta_video_id, reporter_id)` unique key makes a double-tap / retry a no-op (no duplicate report row, `is_reported` stays true). This matches the AC-140 "insert exactly one row" requirement and the project's idempotency posture (cf. the MOD-007 notification unique constraint).
- **RLS on `video_reports`** (Q2 continued): enable RLS from the creating migration (project convention). Policies:
  - **INSERT own rows only**: `WITH CHECK (reporter_id = auth.uid())` — AC-140/§10 NFR. Grant `INSERT` to `authenticated`.
  - **SELECT**: recommend **no SELECT policy for `authenticated`** (do not grant SELECT to `authenticated`). Regular users never need to read `video_reports` — the client only needs the `is_reported`/`report_dismissed` flags on `beta_videos` (which drive AC-139 affordance visibility). The admin reads `video_reports` in Supabase Studio via the service role, which bypasses RLS. Keeping SELECT closed avoids leaking who reported what. **Note**: the DEFINER RPC's `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` needs no SELECT policy because it runs as the function owner (DEFINER), not the caller.
  - **No UPDATE, no DELETE** grants/policies — reports are immutable once filed (Studio/service-role can delete out-of-band if ever needed).
  - Grant: `GRANT INSERT ON public.video_reports TO authenticated;` only. Do **not** grant SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE.

#### Q3 — `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` join + filter: exact query change below. BLOCKED on the Q6 FK repoint for the join.

- **Current query** (`beta-video-service.ts:37-41`): explicit column select, `.eq('route_id', routeId)`, `.order('created_at', {ascending:false})`. No join, no report filter.
- **Exact change** — add the two new columns, the embedded users join, and the exclusion filter:
  ```ts
  const { data, error } = await supabase
    .from('beta_videos')
    .select(
      'id, route_id, user_id, video_url, thumbnail_url, duration_seconds, caption, created_at, ' +
      'is_reported, report_dismissed, users!beta_videos_user_id_fkey(display_name, avatar_url)'
    )
    .eq('route_id', routeId)
    .not('is_reported', 'eq', true)                 // see filter note below
    .order('created_at', { ascending: false });
  ```
- **The exclusion filter — express it as `is_reported = false OR report_dismissed = true`** (equivalently: exclude `is_reported = true AND report_dismissed = false`, AC-142). PostgREST cannot express an `AND`-inside-`OR` negation as cleanly as SQL, so use the `.or()` form for correctness:
  ```ts
  .or('is_reported.eq.false,report_dismissed.eq.true')
  ```
  This returns rows that are either not reported, OR reported-but-admin-dismissed (visible again per AC-143). Do NOT use a bare `.eq('is_reported', false)` — that would permanently hide admin-dismissed videos, violating AC-143. **This exact predicate is the single shared exclusion filter (Q5).**
- **The embedded join `users(display_name, avatar_url)` requires an FK from `beta_videos.user_id` to `public.users(id)`** — which does NOT exist today (Q6). Until the FK is repointed, PostgREST returns an error like `Could not find a relationship between 'beta_videos' and 'users'`. Use the explicit FK hint `users!beta_videos_user_id_fkey(...)` after the repoint names the constraint `beta_videos_user_id_fkey` (Q6). **This join is BLOCKED on Q6 — it is the one hard prerequisite in this change.**
- **Result shape note for the service/type**: PostgREST returns the embedded relation as a nested object `users: { display_name, avatar_url }` (or `null`). The service should flatten it to top-level `display_name` / `avatar_url` on the returned `BetaVideo` (AC-144 says the *result* includes them) so the viewer (AC-134) reads `video.display_name` directly. Flatten in `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` after the query; do not leak the nested `users` object to the UI.

#### Q4 — MOD-006 feed RPC: `get_activity_feed`'s `video_items` CTE has NO report filter today. Add the same predicate.

- **Confirmed**: `get_activity_feed` (`20260924000006_mod_006_social_feed.sql:233-253`) currently selects beta videos with only the follow/block/privacy composition and `JOIN ... bv.route_id` — **no `is_reported`/`report_dismissed` filter**. Post-migration, without a change, a reported-and-undismissed video would leak into the feed, violating AC-141.
- **Exact change**: add a `WHERE` clause to the `video_items` CTE mirroring the shared filter:
  ```sql
  -- in video_items CTE, after the JOINs:
  WHERE (bv.is_reported = FALSE OR bv.report_dismissed = TRUE)
  ```
  (The `ascent_items` CTE needs no change — ascents are not reportable in Phase 1.)
- **Ownership**: `get_activity_feed` is MOD-006-owned. This is a **coordinated cross-module change** — MOD-005 owns the two new columns + the report RPC (its migration); MOD-006 owns the feed-RPC edit. The feed-RPC change must ship in a **MOD-006 migration** (`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.get_activity_feed(...)` with the added `WHERE`), NOT inside the MOD-005 `video_reports` migration — keep each module's owned object in its own module's migration. **Migration ordering**: the MOD-006 feed-RPC change references `bv.is_reported`/`bv.report_dismissed`, so it must run AFTER the MOD-005 migration that adds those columns. Since the MOD-005 migration is dated `20260928000001`, name the MOD-006 change with a later filename, e.g. `20260928000002_mod_006_feed_exclude_reported.sql`. Flag both engineers (MOD-005 and MOD-006) and the ordering dependency for Doc-Sync.
- **The filter predicate is identical** to the `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` filter (Q3/Q5) — pin it as one canonical clause in production.md so the two paths cannot drift.

#### Q5 — Single shared exclusion filter: pin `is_reported = false OR report_dismissed = true` as the ONE canonical beta-video visibility predicate.

- Both surfaces (the client `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` and the server `get_activity_feed` RPC) must use the **same** predicate: **a beta video is visible iff `is_reported = false OR report_dismissed = true`** (equivalently: hidden iff `is_reported = true AND report_dismissed = false`, AC-141/AC-142).
- **Cannot be literally one shared function** (one is PostgREST client-side, the other is SQL in a Postgres RPC), so pin it as a **named convention in production.md** with both concrete forms (the PostgREST `.or('is_reported.eq.false,report_dismissed.eq.true')` and the SQL `WHERE (is_reported = FALSE OR report_dismissed = TRUE)`), so any future beta-video listing path (e.g. a profile grid, a search) copies the exact predicate. This is the §12 "a listing path forgets the filter and a reported video leaks" mitigation.
- **The fullscreen viewer set (AC-039/AC-141) needs no separate filter** — the viewer reuses the in-memory array from `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` (per the Rev 13 review, Q3), so once the fetch excludes reported videos, the grid and the swipe reel are both correct automatically. No extra work for the viewer.

#### Q6 — BLOCKING PREREQUISITE: repoint `beta_videos.user_id` FK from `auth.users(id)` to `public.users(id)` so AC-144's PostgREST join works.

- **The problem**: `beta_videos.user_id` currently `REFERENCES auth.users(id)` (migration `20260924000005` line 28). PostgREST embedded joins (`select('*, users(...)')`) only resolve when there is an FK between the two tables *in the exposed schema* (`public`). There is no FK from `beta_videos` to `public.users`, so AC-144's join cannot be expressed as an embedded resource — it would error at runtime.
- **Why this is safe**: `public.users.id` is itself `REFERENCES auth.users(id)` (MOD-001 migration line 20) with the SAME UUID as the auth user. Every `beta_videos.user_id` value already equals a `public.users.id` value (a user cannot have a beta video without a profile row). So repointing the FK to `public.users(id)` is value-preserving — no data migration, no orphan risk.
- **The fix** (in the Q1 migration, before the `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` join can work):
  ```sql
  ALTER TABLE public.beta_videos
    DROP CONSTRAINT beta_videos_user_id_fkey;
  ALTER TABLE public.beta_videos
    ADD CONSTRAINT beta_videos_user_id_fkey
      FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES public.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE;
  ```
  (Verify the existing constraint name with `\d beta_videos`; PostgreSQL auto-names it `beta_videos_user_id_fkey`, which the `users!beta_videos_user_id_fkey(...)` hint in Q3 relies on. Keep the name stable.)
- **This matches the established convention**: every *other* user-owned `public` table that participates in feed/profile joins already references `public.users(id)` — `ascents.user_id`, `follows.follower_id/followee_id`, `saved_gyms.user_id`, `saved_routes.user_id`, `notifications.*`. `beta_videos.user_id` (and `reactions.user_id`) targeting `auth.users` are the outliers. Repointing `beta_videos` aligns it with the convention AND unblocks AC-144. (Do not repoint `reactions` in this change — out of scope; note it as a latent inconsistency for a future cleanup only.)
- **`video_reports.reporter_id` FK**: per the PRD (§9) it `REFERENCES users(id)` — target **`public.users(id)`** to match the convention (not `auth.users`). `beta_video_id` `REFERENCES public.beta_videos(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (so reports vanish if the video is deleted, matching the PRD).

#### Q7 — Marquee (AC-137): confirmed pure-JS `Animated`, NO new native package. Report-icon visibility (AC-139): confirmed no extra query.

- **AC-137 marquee — confirmed pure JS.** React Native's core `Animated` API (a JS/no-native-module primitive — already available, no install) drives a looped `Animated.timing` on a `translateX` of the Row-2 `<Text>` inside a clipped (`overflow: 'hidden'`) container `View`. Pattern: measure the text width via `onLayout` (or `onTextLayout`); if text width > container width, run `Animated.loop(Animated.timing(translateX, { toValue: -(textWidth - containerWidth), duration, useNativeDriver: true }))` (ping-pong or reset-to-start); if text fits, render static (no animation) per AC-137. `useNativeDriver: true` is fine for `translateX` (a transform) and keeps it off the JS thread without any native *package*. **This is consistent with the Rev 13 convention** "prefer RN built-ins; do not add native gesture/animation packages for simple effects" — no `reanimated`, no `react-native-marquee`, no native rebuild.
- **AC-139 report-icon visibility — confirmed no additional query.** Both signals the viewer needs are already on the `BetaVideo` row after this change:
  1. **Is the current user the owner?** Compare `video.user_id` to the current session user id (`session.user.id`) client-side. `user_id` is already selected. Hide the report icon when `video.user_id === currentUserId` (AC-139).
  2. **Is `report_dismissed = true`?** Now selected on the row (Q3). Hide (or permanently disable) the report icon when `video.report_dismissed === true` (AC-139/AC-143).
  No second round-trip: the current user id comes from the auth session already in context; `report_dismissed` and `user_id` come from the `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` result. Confirmed.
- **Note on `is_reported` for own-viewer state**: after a user taps report, the local row should optimistically set `is_reported = true` so the icon updates immediately — but because the fetch filter (Q5) excludes `is_reported = true AND report_dismissed = false`, the reported video disappears from *everyone's* next fetch (AC-141), including the reporter's. For the reporter's current in-viewer session, the engineer may either (a) leave the video in the open reel until dismissed/re-fetched, or (b) advance/close the viewer after report. Flag as a UX detail for the MOD-005 spec (not an architecture concern); AC-141 governs the next-fetch hiding, which the filter already enforces.

---

#### Concerns (must address during the Rev 16 change work)

- **BLOCKING: `beta_videos.user_id` must be repointed from `auth.users(id)` to `public.users(id)` before AC-144's join works** (Q6). This is the single hard prerequisite — the PostgREST embedded join `users(display_name, avatar_url)` cannot resolve without a `public`-schema FK. The repoint is value-preserving (no data migration) but must land in the same MOD-005 migration, ordered before anyone relies on the join. Without it, AC-144/AC-134 fail at runtime while unit tests (which mock Supabase) stay green — the same class of gap as the Rev 9 native-build issue.
- **The report RPC must write exactly two things and never touch `report_dismissed`** (Q2). `report_dismissed` is admin-only (AC-143, Studio/service-role). A DEFINER function that could flip `report_dismissed` would let a reporter un-hide (or an over-broad function get abused). Keep `report_beta_video` single-purpose: one `video_reports` insert (idempotent) + `is_reported = TRUE`.
- **The MOD-006 feed RPC MUST gain the exclusion filter** (Q4) — it has none today, so a reported video would leak into the feed post-migration (AC-141 violation). This is a coordinated MOD-006 migration (`CREATE OR REPLACE get_activity_feed` with the added `WHERE`), ordered AFTER the MOD-005 column-add migration. Both engineers + the ordering dependency must be routed.
- **The exclusion predicate must be `is_reported = false OR report_dismissed = true`, not a bare `is_reported = false`** (Q3/Q5) — a bare equality would permanently hide admin-dismissed videos and violate AC-143 (dismissed videos must reappear). Pin the exact predicate as one canonical convention so the client filter and the feed RPC cannot drift.
- **Do NOT add any native package for the marquee** (Q7) — AC-137 is explicit ("no new native package"), and RN core `Animated` covers it. No `reanimated`, no `react-native-marquee`.

#### Recommendations

- Create one MOD-005 migration `20260928000001_mod_005_video_reports.sql` containing, in order: (1) `ADD COLUMN is_reported`/`report_dismissed` (both `boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`); (2) the `beta_videos.user_id` FK repoint to `public.users(id)` (Q6); (3) `CREATE TABLE public.video_reports` (`id uuid PK DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()`, `beta_video_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.beta_videos(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, `reporter_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.users(id)`, `reported_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`, `UNIQUE (beta_video_id, reporter_id)`), RLS enabled, INSERT-own-rows policy (`reporter_id = auth.uid()`), `GRANT INSERT ... TO authenticated` only (no SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE); (4) the `report_beta_video(uuid)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC (Q2 body), `SET search_path = public`, EXECUTE granted to `authenticated`, revoked from `anon`/`public`.
- Ship the MOD-006 feed-RPC change in a separate, later-dated MOD-006 migration (`20260928000002_mod_006_feed_exclude_reported.sql`) that `CREATE OR REPLACE`s `get_activity_feed` with `WHERE (bv.is_reported = FALSE OR bv.report_dismissed = TRUE)` in the `video_items` CTE. Ordered after the MOD-005 migration.
- Update `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` (Q3): add `is_reported, report_dismissed, users!beta_videos_user_id_fkey(display_name, avatar_url)` to the select, add `.or('is_reported.eq.false,report_dismissed.eq.true')`, and flatten the embedded `users` object to top-level `display_name`/`avatar_url` on the returned `BetaVideo`.
- Extend the `BetaVideo` TS interface (`mod-beta-video/types.ts`, Q also implied): add `is_reported: boolean;`, `report_dismissed: boolean;`, `display_name: string | null;`, `avatar_url: string | null;`. (Keep them non-optional for the fields the query always returns; `display_name`/`avatar_url` are nullable because the join can yield nulls.)
- Add a `reportBetaVideo(betaVideoId: string): Promise<void>` service function in `beta-video-service.ts` that calls `supabase.rpc('report_beta_video', { p_beta_video_id: betaVideoId })` — the client never does a direct `beta_videos` UPDATE for reporting.
- AC-137 marquee: RN core `Animated` looped `translateX` on the Row-2 text inside an `overflow:'hidden'` container, gated on measured text-width > container-width; static when it fits.
- AC-139 visibility: hide the report icon when `video.user_id === session.user.id` OR `video.report_dismissed === true` — both from data already in hand, no extra query.
- Engineer verification: `supabase db reset` locally; confirm `\d beta_videos` shows the two new columns + the `public.users` FK; confirm `report_beta_video` sets `is_reported = true` + inserts one `video_reports` row and is a no-op on a second call (idempotent); confirm `fetchBetaVideosForRoute` returns the joined names and excludes an `is_reported=true, report_dismissed=false` row but returns an `is_reported=true, report_dismissed=true` row.

#### Approved (looks solid as-is)

- The PRD's `VideoReport`/`beta_videos`-columns/SECURITY DEFINER RPC design is architecturally sound and matches the shipped `submit_route` DEFINER pattern and the production.md SECURITY DEFINER conventions (`SET search_path = public`, derive from `auth.uid()`, `authenticated`-only EXECUTE). No design change needed — only the FK repoint (Q6) and the concrete filter/join specs above.
- The `Report` (MOD-009 user-profile, category+note) and `video_reports` (MOD-005 one-tap, no category) coexistence is clean: they are separate tables with separate write paths and separate admin-review flows (Report → AC-085 email digest; video_reports → Studio manual). No shared schema, no conflict. The Rev 16 boundary shift (beta-video reporting MOD-009 → MOD-005) is consistent — MOD-009 has not shipped a migration yet, so nothing to un-build there.
- The one-tap report affordance's visibility rules (AC-139) are fully derivable from the `beta_videos` row + the auth session — no extra query, consistent with the "no N+1, data already in hand" convention.
- The additive migration (two `DEFAULT false` columns) is metadata-only on PG11+ — no table rewrite, safe on populated data; existing videos default to the correct "not reported" state.

#### Proposed Shared Conventions (for Doc-Sync to carry into production.md)

- **User-owned `public` tables that participate in PostgREST embedded joins or feed/profile joins must reference `public.users(id)`, not `auth.users(id)`.** PostgREST can only embed a related resource (`select('*, users(...)')`) when the FK targets a table in the exposed `public` schema. `public.users.id` mirrors `auth.users.id` (same UUID), so referencing `public.users(id)` is equivalent for integrity and additionally enables embedded joins. New user-owned tables should reference `public.users(id)`; when an existing table (e.g. `beta_videos`) needs an embedded user join, repoint its FK (value-preserving, no data migration).
- **Beta-video visibility has one canonical exclusion predicate, reused verbatim by every listing path.** A beta video is visible iff `is_reported = false OR report_dismissed = true` (hidden iff `is_reported = true AND report_dismissed = false`). Client (PostgREST): `.or('is_reported.eq.false,report_dismissed.eq.true')`. Server (SQL, e.g. the feed RPC): `WHERE (is_reported = FALSE OR report_dismissed = TRUE)`. Never use a bare `is_reported = false` — it would permanently hide admin-dismissed videos and violate the re-appear-on-dismiss rule. Any new beta-video listing surface must copy this exact predicate.
- **Cross-user field flips go through a single-purpose SECURITY DEFINER RPC that writes only the fields it must, derives the actor from `auth.uid()`, and is idempotent.** When a user must set a flag on a row they do not own (e.g. a reporter flipping `is_reported` on another user's video), expose a narrow `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC (`SET search_path = public`, EXECUTE to `authenticated` only, revoked from `anon`/`public`, no `user_id` parameter) that writes exactly the required field(s) plus its own audit row, guarded by `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` for idempotency and by explicit checks (e.g. forbid self-action, require the row to exist). Never widen a DEFINER function to a general table-update helper, and never let it touch admin-only fields (e.g. `report_dismissed` stays Studio/service-role only).
- **Cross-module RPC edits ship in the owning module's migration, ordered after any prerequisite schema.** When one module (MOD-005) adds columns another module's RPC (MOD-006 `get_activity_feed`) must reference, the RPC change is a `CREATE OR REPLACE` in the owning module's own migration, filename-dated after the prerequisite column-add migration so `supabase db reset` applies them in dependency order. Do not edit another module's owned object inside your migration.
- **Simple text-scroll/marquee animations use RN core `Animated` (`translateX` loop in an `overflow:'hidden'` container, gated on measured overflow), never a native marquee/animation package.** Extends the existing "prefer RN built-ins for simple effects; no native rebuild for a small UI behavior" convention to text-overflow marquee.

### Review — 2026-09-28 — change (Rev 14: MOD-003/MOD-004 route detail redesign — AC-125–AC-130, AC-011 revised)

**Context**: PRD Revision 14 [SUBSTANTIVE] redesigns the RouteDetailScreen composition (gym-name subtitle AC-125, added-date footer AC-126, two-column per-user send-summary AC-127, tap-through to a new Send History Detail screen AC-128/AC-129, two action controls AC-130) and revises AC-011 (log-send form must not present a V-grade input). Advisory only — no source, spec, or PRD files changed here. Findings grounded in the shipped code: `send-service.ts` (`fetchMyAscentsForRoute`, `fetchUserAchievements`), `send-logging/types.ts` (`ASCENT_STYLES`, `AscentWithProfile`), `RouteNavigator.tsx` (view-state machine), `RouteDetailScreen.tsx` (current composition + `BetaVideoSection` mount), `LogSendScreen.tsx` (the form). Headline: **no data-model change, no new dependency, no new service function — the redesign is a pure presentation/navigation change over data already in hand, and AC-011's "no grade input" is already satisfied in the shipped form (only a stale doc comment to fix).**

---

#### Q1 — `fetchMyAscentsForRoute` service contract: already exists and is SUFFICIENT for both AC-127 and AC-129. No extension needed.

- **The function exists** at `src/modules/mod-send-logging/send-service.ts:146` and is already the MOD-004 public service consumed by RouteDetailScreen today (imported line 49 of `RouteDetailScreen.tsx`). It is own-user-scoped (`.eq('user_id', user.id)` line 160 — defense-in-depth against the `ascents_select_own_or_public` RLS OR-clause), ordered `logged_at DESC`, and returns `AscentWithProfile[]`.
- **Return shape** (`AscentWithProfile`, `send-logging/types.ts:32`): `{ id, user_id, route_id, style, attempts, note, logged_at, is_private, display_name }`.
- **AC-129 (list style + attempts per record)** — sufficient as-is. `style` and `attempts` are both present on every returned row; the Send History Detail screen renders one row per array element. No new field.
- **AC-127 (Sends count + Attempts sum)** — sufficient as-is; both values are derivable client-side from the same array with no new query:
  - **Sends card** = `myAscents.filter(a => a.style === 'flash' || a.style === 'top').length`
  - **Attempts card** = `myAscents.reduce((sum, a) => sum + a.attempts, 0)`
- **Decision — one function, not two; aggregation is CLIENT-SIDE (no RPC).** The PM asked whether AC-127's aggregate and AC-129's raw list are one function or two, and whether aggregation is server-side. Recommendation: **one function (`fetchMyAscentsForRoute`), client-side aggregation.** Rationale: (1) both surfaces need the same raw per-route own-user ascent array — AC-129 shows every row, AC-127 shows two reductions over the identical set; fetching once and reducing in the component avoids a second round-trip and keeps the count and the list provably consistent (no risk of an aggregate RPC disagreeing with the list); (2) the row count is inherently small (one user's ascents on one route), so client-side reduction is trivially cheap — a server-side aggregate RPC would be over-engineering for a per-user/per-route slice; (3) RouteDetailScreen already calls `fetchMyAscentsForRoute` and holds the array in `myAscents` state (`RouteDetailScreen.tsx:122`) — AC-127's two numbers can be computed from state that is *already loaded*, with **zero new fetch**. Contrast with `fetchUserAchievements` (the *batched, multi-route* case) which correctly needed its own batched signature to avoid N+1 — that reasoning does not apply here because this is a single route.
- **Where the AC-127 computation lives — a pure helper in `send-service.ts`, consumed by the screen.** To keep the reduction logic testable and to keep the MOD-003 screen from embedding MOD-004 domain rules (which style counts as a "send"), expose a tiny pure helper alongside the fetch in MOD-004's public service, e.g. `summarizeAscents(ascents: AscentWithProfile[]): { sends: number; attempts: number }` where `sends` counts `style ∈ {flash, top}` and `attempts` sums the `attempts` column. MOD-003 calls `fetchMyAscentsForRoute` (already does) and passes the result to `summarizeAscents`. This keeps the "flash|top = a send" business rule owned by MOD-004 (the send-logging domain), consistent with the "owning module owns its domain reductions" convention the `flash>top>attempt` precedence review established. A screen-inline `.filter/.reduce` is acceptable for Phase 1 too, but the helper is preferred so the rule lives in one place and both AC-127 (detail) and the new Send History Detail screen (which may echo the totals) agree. **Flag for Doc-Sync**: pin in the MOD-004 spec that AC-127's two values are client-derived from `fetchMyAscentsForRoute` (no RPC, no new query) via a MOD-004 `summarizeAscents` helper; "sends" = count of `flash|top`, "attempts" = sum of `attempts`.

#### Q2 — Send History Detail screen placement: a NEW view state in the existing MOD-003 `RouteNavigator`. Do NOT add a new navigator.

- **Add one view state to the existing `RouteView` union** in `RouteNavigator.tsx:29`. Recommended name: **`'send-history'`** (consistent with the existing lowercase-kebab `list` / `detail` / `submit` naming). New union member: `{ name: 'send-history'; routeId: string }`.
- **Props the new screen needs**: `routeId: string` (passed from the tapped card, AC-128), `session: Session` (own-user scoping for `fetchMyAscentsForRoute`), and `onBack: () => void` returning to the route detail for *that* route (AC-129 back control). Because RouteDetail is reached by `routeId` (not a persisted stack), the back handler must navigate **back to `{ name: 'detail', routeId }`**, not to `list` — otherwise the back control drops the user two levels. Add a navigator handler:
  ```ts
  function navigateToSendHistory(routeId: string): void {
    setView({ name: 'send-history', routeId });
  }
  ```
  and the new screen's `onBack={() => navigateToDetail(view.routeId)}` (returns to route detail, preserving `routeId`). RouteDetailScreen gets a new prop `onViewSendHistory: (routeId: string) => void` wired to `navigateToSendHistory` — the two summary cards (AC-128) call `onViewSendHistory(route.id)`.
- **It sits inside the existing `RouteNavigator`, not a new navigator** — the screen is MOD-003-owned (AC-128/AC-129 are MOD-003 ACs), route-scoped, and reached from and returning to RouteDetailScreen. This is the exact `list → detail` precedent already shipped (view-state machine, no navigation library). A separate navigator would fragment MOD-003's navigation and has no benefit. New screen file: `src/modules/mod-route-catalog/screens/SendHistoryDetailScreen.tsx`.
- **Data source in the new screen**: it calls MOD-004's `fetchMyAscentsForRoute(routeId)` directly (already the established cross-module public-service import — same import RouteDetailScreen uses today). It does NOT query `ascents` directly. Consider a small MOD-003→MOD-004 reuse: the screen can re-fetch, or (simpler and consistent) fetch fresh on mount so the history is current even if the user logged sends since opening detail.

#### Q3 — AC-130 action buttons vs the existing beta-video entry point: REMOVE the entry point inside `BetaVideoSection`; the AC-130 row becomes the single "Upload Beta Video" entry point.

- **Current state**: `RouteDetailScreen.tsx` has a log-send entry point (the "Log Send" `Pressable` in the ascent section header, line 408-415, `onLogSend`→`handleLogSendPress`) and mounts `BetaVideoSection` (line 510), which today contains the AC-037 "Add beta video" affordance internally.
- **AC-130 is explicit**: its two controls *"replace any prior placement of the log-send and add-beta-video entry points on the screen."* This is a directive, not a suggestion — both prior entry points must move into the AC-130 action row, and the old placements must be removed to avoid two ways to do the same thing.
- **What the engineer should do**:
  1. **Remove the "Log Send" button** from the ascent-section header (line 408-415); the AC-130 "Record Send History" control replaces it (still opens the same `LogSendScreen` modal via `handleLogSendPress`).
  2. **Remove the "Add beta video" entry point from inside `BetaVideoSection`** (MOD-005-owned component) and instead surface AC-130's "Upload Beta Video" control in the MOD-003 action row, which launches the MOD-005 upload flow. **Boundary note**: the upload *flow* is MOD-005-owned (AC-037). Two clean options — (a) `BetaVideoSection` keeps rendering the grid/viewer only (no entry affordance) and exposes an imperative "open uploader" via a prop/ref or a MOD-005-provided callback that MOD-003's action button invokes; or (b) MOD-005 exposes a separate public trigger component/callback that MOD-003 mounts in the action row. Preferred: **MOD-005 exposes an `onUploadPress`/launcher the MOD-003 action button calls**, keeping the uploader flow inside MOD-005 while the button lives in MOD-003's AC-130 row — the same host-screen/owning-module split flagged for AC-037 in the 2026-09-23 navigation review. This is a **coordinated MOD-003 + MOD-005 change** — flag both engineers.
  - **Do NOT leave both** — shipping the AC-130 "Upload Beta Video" button *and* the old in-`BetaVideoSection` affordance would violate AC-130's "replace any prior placement" wording and confuse the UX. Exactly one upload entry point (the AC-130 row); the grid+viewer inside `BetaVideoSection` stays (that is display, not an entry point).

#### Q4 — AC-011 revised (no V-grade input on the log-send form): ALREADY SATISFIED in code. No field to remove; only a stale doc comment to fix.

- **The shipped `LogSendScreen.tsx` has no grade input field.** Grade is rendered **read-only** for display only (lines 151-156: a `gradeDisplay` block showing `routeGrade` with a `gradeNote`), never as a selectable/editable control. The form fields are: style selector, attempts, date, note, is_private — none is a grade picker. `logAscent`'s payload (line 108-115) contains no `grade` field, and `AscentLogInput` (types.ts:40) has no grade. So AC-011's "shall not present a V-grade (or any grade) input field" is **already met**.
- **This is purely presentational — and it's already correct.** There is no submission-logic change: grade was never stored (AC-011 original), and the form never had a grade *input*. The revision codifies existing behavior.
- **The only action for the engineer** is cosmetic-doc hygiene: the JSDoc comment block at the top of `LogSendScreen.tsx` is **stale on two counts** and should be corrected so it doesn't mislead: (1) line 12 lists the style selector as `flash / top / attempt / project` — `project` was removed in Rev 9 (the actual `ASCENT_STYLES` in `types.ts:10` is `['flash', 'top', 'attempt']`, so the running code is correct; only the comment lies); (2) the comment should note explicitly that grade is display-only per AC-011 (line 15 already says "displayed but NOT stored or editable" — this is accurate and can stay).
